Deleted references match nothing and~malformed or~unknown rule versions are~rejected. The~normal migration workflow adds Smart Gallery presentation storage; existing Smart Galleries inherit Theme/site defaults until an~override is~saved.

\section{Cooperative galleries}
\index{cooperative galleries}
Cooperative galleries connect independently operated HTTPS installations while each owner retains their albums and~original files. Apply pending migrations through the~normal administration workflow, then enable \ui{Cooperative galleries} in~\ui{Features} on~each server. The~feature is~off by~default; turning it~off preserves identities, friendships, proposals and~preferences.

Open \ui{Settings > Advanced > Friendly galleries} and~pair every participating server directly. Friendship alone shares no~photographs. In~\ui{Album collaborations}, select a~public, listed, password-free, non-NSFW album and~prepare its reference code. One participant combines the~references into an~immutable proposal; every participant reviews the~exact members and~permissions and~explicitly approves locally. \ui{Deliver and~verify} advances a~bounded sequence in~the~existing panel. Email invitations use the~configured account-mail transport; opening an~invitation never grants consent.

After activation, open the~shared photographs from the~collaboration panel or~the~local album's shared-trip link. Each source retains attribution and~pagination. Existing JPEG/WebP thumbnails and~previews are~authorized independently; originals, protected albums and~remote writes are~unsupported. Ordinary links work without JavaScript. Missing derivatives need the~normal thumbnail workflow. New photographs receive complete path-based identities; old names remain intact without bulk rebuilding, but~ambiguous ownership is~refused.

Withdrawal, changed source protection and~local friendship revocation block subsequent authorized reads. An~unreachable peer remains bounded by~the~maximum 120-second verification lease; downloaded pixels cannot be~recalled. Adding a~participant creates a~separate proposal requiring fresh approval from everyone. Ordinary use can~advance technical renewal on~demand; unattended renewal is~optional. Missing or~unknown required schema refuses sharing and~mutations. See \filepath{docs/COOPERATIVE_GALLERIES.md} for~operational limits and~deployment qualification.

\section{Windows companion uploader}
\subsection{Uploader layout}

The~uploader opens on~\ui{Watcher}; its~navigation order is~\ui{Watcher},
\ui{Import}, \ui{Activity}, \ui{AI}, and~\ui{Settings}. The~Watcher screen
shows the~gallery target. Its~top connection control opens a~compact API-key
dialog with a~masked key and~\ui{Save \& Test}; this~connection control remains
available in~the~header on~every screen. Unsaved edits do~not~replace the~saved
key. The~app header also has Settings,
updates, and~help controls. The~Watcher screen keeps the~selected folder path,
watcher status, and~source-delete warning visible.
\ui{Start Watcher} starts only after an~explicit click; while active the~same
contextual control is~\ui{Stop Watcher}. \ui{Check folder only} performs a~folder
dry run without uploads or~starting continuous watching. The~Watcher gear opens
a~settings sheet for~folder choice, recursion, scan interval, file stability,
simulator metadata, and~the~opt-in deletion setting. The~sheet requires stopping
the~watcher before
edits; \ui{Save watcher settings} stores them for~the~next start. \ui{Cancel},
Escape, or~closing the~sheet restores the~opening folder, ignore count, last
scan, and~preferences.
\ui{Sync UTC time} remains an~opt-in beside the~main actions.
Long pages and~dialogs scroll with the~mouse wheel or~scrollbar; text and~table
controls keep their own scrolling.

Manual \ui{Import} still requires preflight review and~an~explicit start.
\ui{Activity} contains the~filtered event history, retry controls, redacted
diagnostics, and~log. A~compact latest-event footer opens \ui{Activity} when
selected. Icon controls expose tooltips and~keyboard focus; the~question-mark help
opens usage guidance. These presentation changes preserve the
existing persistence, tray, update, and~API-key safeguards.
Pillow adds an~ambient cool gradient and~opaque glass-inspired card, footer,
and~pill-button artwork. This~composited treatment simulates frosted glass
without true alpha transparency or~operating-system backdrop blur; text stays
sharp, and~input and~table surfaces stay solid and~high-contrast. It~uses no~whole-window
opacity and~adds no~runtime dependencies. Without Pillow, readable flat ttk
surfaces remain, and~native controls and~source-mode operation, including
\texttt{--once}, stay supported.

\subsection{Windows startup and~one running instance}

The~installed uploader starts after Windows sign-in by~default, minimized
directly into the~system tray. In~\ui{Settings > App \& integrations},
\ui{Start with Windows (minimized to tray)} saves immediately; switching it~off
removes this user's startup entry, and~updates retain that choice. Setup applies
the~preference even if~the~final application-launch checkbox is~cleared.
Source/Python runs leave Windows startup unchanged. Watching and~imports still
need their usual explicit Start action.

Opening the~app again restores the~existing window and~keeps current work.
One Windows process owns the~shared app profile, including executable copies;
another Windows session must open its existing process in~that session.
A~logon launch keeps import-recovery prompts deferred until the~window opens.
If~the~tray cannot become available within five seconds, the~window appears
so~the~app remains accessible. Startup registration belongs to~the~user running
the~application; an~installer explicitly started elevated may~use that account.

\subsection{Update notice and~indicators}

The~app checks for~uploader releases at~startup and~at~most once an~hour;
\ui{Check for updates} requests a~manual check. A~validated offer appears as~a
version banner and~marked update control in~the~main header on~every screen.
\ui{Later} hides the~notice but~leaves both indicators available to~reopen it.
When tray support is~available, its~icon shows a~red update badge, its~tooltip
identifies the~offered version, and~the~first menu action names that version
and~opens the~same notice. Without tray support, the~in-app indicators remain
available. The~app does~not~set a~Windows taskbar overlay or~show
operating-system toast notifications.

A~failed network recheck preserves the~validated cached offer across restart;
the~offer is~validated again before it~is~restored. A~successful check with
no~current offer clears the~banner, header mark, and~tray badge.

\subsection{Windows simulator location}

\ui{Sync UTC time}, beside the~Watch folder actions, is~off by~default and~saves
its~own preference. While watching, a~separate worker checks MSFS 2020 or~2024
every 10 seconds. It~compares full simulator UTC date/time with Windows UTC;
only an~absolute difference above 60 seconds requests year, ordinal day, hour
and~minute correction, followed by~fresh readback. The~worker waits for~a~loaded,
unpaused flight at~1x rate and~five seconds of~settled state. Menus, loading,
pause and~other rates suspend writes. Window/tray stop and~complete exit cancel
UTC work; hiding to~tray preserves it. The~selection survives stopping.
Three failed corrections with 30-second backoff suspend that connection until
reconnection. Uploads and~photo-location acquisition remain independent.

Both generations use the~same time events. The~one-based ordinal day setter
and~any effect on~Live Time/Live Weather still require live verification
in~two separately loaded flights per~available simulator/build; native mocks
do~not~establish those results. See \filepath{winapp/README.md}.
Hosted installers explicitly use x64 CPython 3.14.x. Before packaging,
the~builder verifies its~interpreter and~the~actual frozen EXE's patch version,
implementation and~pointer width through a~pre-GUI report; a~wrong or~missing
report fails preparation. The~independent application source targets 0.10.0.

The~bundled native SimConnect DLL comes unmodified from the~official
MSFS 2024 SDK Core 1.7.3. Its~84,480 bytes and~SHA256 are~documented
in~\filepath{winapp/README.md}. Static x64 and~export checks preserve
the~previous 117 named exports; live camera, flight telemetry and~UTC acceptance
in~MSFS 2020/2024 remain separate. SDK version identifies provenance because
the~DLL has~no~usable embedded file version. Microsoft SDK terms apply.

WinApp 0.3.2 automatically uses the~MSFS 2024 camera world position and~falls back to~the~aircraft position on~bounded camera failure. MSFS 2020 uses the~aircraft position directly through the~same SimConnect runtime. Coordinates are~optional: simulator unavailability does~not~prevent upload. Position-source diagnostics distinguish camera and~aircraft results. The~installer requests application shutdown before replacing files; complete pending work before updating. Live simulator and~upgrade acceptance must be~checked on~the~target PC.

The~frozen updater loads Windows APIs explicitly from System32, avoiding a~collision between the~bundled VERSION text file and~the~Windows version DLL. When upgrading from 0.3.1, manually launch the~verified 0.3.2 installer. The~running older app copies its own helper before installation, so~that old helper may~still report its DLL error after replacing the~app. Future updates started from the~corrected 0.3.2 app use the~repaired helper; verify the~installed handoff on~Windows.

Aircraft replies are~matched by~request and~definition IDs; the~request USER selector does~not~require a~zero reply object ID. Bounded diagnostics record receive metadata, dispatch sequence and~specific rejected-packet reasons. Failure reasons survive path redaction and~activity messages display one SimConnect marker. The~object-ID filter was~a~confirmed code bug, but~the~reported FS2020 timeout did~not~record the~returned object ID. Retest on~the~affected PC; simulated tests do~not~prove a~live fix.

The~installed uploader checks its own stable GitHub installer version at~most once per~hour; \ui{Check for~updates} also starts a~manual check. The~last automatic attempt is~saved independently for~the~current user and~survives application restarts. CMS tags do~not~determine its version. The~highest numeric installer version requires SHA256 from the~GitHub asset or~a~matching same-release \filepath{winapp-update.json}. Missing or~conflicting integrity data refuses the~update; identical installers in~several CMS releases are~accepted. Gallery credentials are~never sent to~GitHub.

Choose \ui{Update} to~download with progress and~optional cancellation, verify the~installer, stop new work and~wait up~to~120 seconds for~background tasks to~finish safely. An~unsuccessful drain refuses installation and~keeps the~uploader available. An~external helper rechecks the~download and~requests Windows UAC approval. Self-update never force-closes workers; normal manually launched installers retain their existing shutdown policy. Actual installation progress comes through a~local session-specific channel. After success, the~helper restarts the~uploader as~the~original user with saved settings and~jobs. There is~no~automatic rollback. Native Python/source runs offer the~verified installer for~manual launch. If~Windows reports that a~restart is~required, restart Windows before reopening the~uploader; cancelling UAC reopens the~existing installation.


\subsection{Flight recording and reports}

The~uploader uses dark glass panels, an~icon navigation rail, and~sliding
switches. Hover or~focus icons for their descriptions. The~header groups
PHP Gallery with its~autopilot subtitle. Settings gears sit directly beside
their gallery, watch-folder, or~flight control. The~flight gear opens a~local
sheet for automatic reports, report languages, interface language, progress,
and~the~report folder. Starting the~watcher automatically arms recording;
there is~no~separate flight-start action. The~Record flight switch pauses
and~resumes acquisition during watching, preserving the~session and~marking
the~gap in~the~track. Starting with recording off opens no~SimConnect
connection. Photo uploads continue independently. Closing settings starts
no~operation. Glass artwork uses opaque RGB pixels for reliable Windows Tk;
source mode without Pillow retains usable native controls for repair.

The~folder path and~picker share Watch folder settings. Saved paths,
gallery URL, and~feature choices are restored after restart. The~interface
language uses four directly visible choices. Scrollbars appear only when
content exceeds the~viewport; helper windows and~tooltips use the~same
dark palette.

The~Windows uploader records MSFS 2020 and~2024 aircraft telemetry through
a~shared read-only provider. Recording and~report creation are enabled by
default; recording can~be opted out independently, and~the~saved language
choices initially include English, Czech, German, and~Swedish. The~bundled
ReportLab renderer and~DejaVu fonts create a~separate chart PDF for each
selected language from one analysis. Reports remain local for retry.
Unsupported signals stay unavailable rather than invented; live simulator,
aircraft, and~build acceptance remains separate for each generation.

The~recorder requests \texttt{ABSOLUTE TIME} in seconds and load factor in
\texttt{GForce}. The~\texttt{E:} prefix belongs to RPN expressions.
Photo-camera success does not establish flight recording. Copied diagnostics
show the~app version, independent recorder state, saved sample count, and last
warning. Rejected SDK operations identify the~variable, units, send ID, and
parameter index. Known SDK/HRESULT codes include a~name, explanation, and impact;
unknown codes stay explicit. Changed failure reasons stay in session warnings.
Connection, first valid frame, and first saved sample have separate log messages.
Missing historical samples cannot be recreated.

The~Record flight row also shows a~single compact IAS/ALT/V/S line in knots,
feet, and feet per minute. It reads the~existing recorder cache without another
SimConnect connection. Paused, stopped, unavailable, or more-than-three-second-old
values become dashes. The~adjacent \ui{PDF} shortcut opens saved flights.

\ui{Saved flights and PDFs} lists start time, aircraft, checkpoint sample count,
local PDF count, state, and retained errors. Refresh reads checkpoints only;
Open app log uses the~existing log. \ui{Finish flight and create PDFs} creates
all four editions, finishes accepted work and~required delivery, then revokes
the~saved key without a~confirmation. It~also works with automatic creation off.

Selected languages render simultaneously in separate processes, one per
language (currently up~to four). Flight analysis and map backgrounds are prepared
once; the~main application uploads only after the~complete set succeeds.
Failure keeps completed PDFs for recovery. Cancellation stops queued work,
waits for running renders and retains their finished files.

With one version, expanding a~flight shows its files directly. Multiple versions
appear as expandable groups, always numerically descending (v5, v4, v3),
regardless of column sorting. Refresh retains expanded flights/versions and
selected files. This display does not move or rename files on disk.

For a~stopped flight, \ui{Create local PDF} or \ui{Re-create local PDF} creates
a~numbered version under \filepath{reports/}. Earlier PDFs, raw samples,
and upload receipts remain intact. No key is~required, no upload/revoke occurs,
and another running flight continues. Partial reports remain incomplete; active,
unclosed, and empty recordings are~refused. Local progress/errors are~retained
in \filepath{manual-report.json}; local creation does not resolve pending
remote delivery. Cancellation retains finished editions. Shutdown/update drains
this worker. Windows layout and live acceptance remain a~separate manual check.

The~\ui{Finish flight} button sits below \ui{Target gallery}; one click
authorizes completion and~key revocation. After failure, \ui{Keep locally and revoke key} and
Retry retain the~same connection's confirmed intent in~this app session.
Cancelling revocation, changing credentials, or~restarting clears that consent.
Ordinary Stop keeps the~key. Recovery buttons wrap into two rows.

\ui{Abandon flight} beside Finish in~Watcher/Import and~in~flight progress
offers explicit local recovery after a~gallery is~deleted or~its~key revoked.
After confirmation, workers stop and~in-flight requests settle; pending delivery
is~abandoned and~the~saved key is~removed from this PC without contacting
the~server. Recordings, photograph sources, PDFs and~receipts remain local.
It~also works with no~pending sessions. The~main action becomes
\ui{Begin flight}. A~still-valid server key needs separate revocation in~Admin.
Submitted revocation must settle first; shutdown or~configuration-save failure
keeps the~key and~recovery available for~retry.

To~upload retained PDFs, save and test a~valid gallery key, including a~new key
for~the~same gallery. In~the~report browser select a~stopped flight and~PDF
files with Ctrl/Shift, then choose \ui{Upload saved PDFs}. Confirm the~gallery and~files;
server inventory verifies the~exact target before transfer. Existing PDFs are
sent without rendering, stopping another flight, or~revoking the~key. Separate
\filepath{upload-receipts.json} retains acknowledged checksums; retries reconcile
server status. Old plans, bindings, and~raw data remain intact. Cancellation
retains finished editions. Protocol errors include redacted HTTP/action/type
context and~a~bounded response excerpt.

Without a~saved key the~main action reads \ui{Begin flight} and opens API
settings. Expand a~flight and, when present, its version to~see PDFs;
double-click opens the~default
viewer. \ui{Open folder with PDFs} opens only the~PDF folder. Filenames contain
recorded UTC, aircraft, language, and~version. A~restartable private catalog
normalizes older layouts without changing bytes or~upload identity; JSONs are
in~\filepath{reports/.metadata/}. Keep that folder with backups.

New reports use A4 landscape (297 by~210~mm), with a~navy header,
teal accents, pale background, and~white panels. Embedded regular/bold DejaVu
uses larger type: 28~pt titles, 12~pt body, 9.5~pt notes, and~9~pt plot axes.
The~overview places value cards beside the~route; full-flight charts appear
in~pairs. Each landing has a~summary and~a~separate detail page with four plots.
Earlier PDF editions remain available; this layout changes no~measurements,
recording, transfer, or~flight completion.

Reports use value cards and~vector phase/configuration icons. Technical versions
remain metadata; simulator build/date use small header text. Rounded Y~axes and
subtle grids show feet AMSL, feet AGL on~approach, IAS knots, and~fpm with
200/1,000 minor/major spacing. Long-flight time grids use ten minutes/hours.
Route axes label coordinates, north, and~first/last captured points. Scalar
profiles bridge gaps up~to~5~s in~curve color and~5--300~s in~amber dashes;
pauses, reloads, and~longer losses remain breaks. This display interpolation
never enters landing measurements. No~runway, score, or~missing rate is~invented.

Expanded PDF rows show page count and~local creation date/time, with version
and~language first. A~star marks legacy file modification time when creation
metadata is~unknown. Phase milestones normally fit one row. Landing profiles
retain original samples before overview thinning; fitted touchdown rate and
the~simulator V/S are~separate, with actual cadence and~partial rollout disclosed.
The~compass compares true aircraft heading with wind-from HDG/KT, such~as
020/05, and~heading-relative components; it~does not~infer runway alignment.
Descending below 100~ft AGL latches every acquired frame through rollout until
10~s at~15~kt or~less on~the~ground. Earlier dense capture below 300~m remains;
a~climb above 500~m exits for~a~go-around. Target acquisition remains 20~Hz.
PDF requests use the~established uploader identification; gateway HTML 401/403
is~reported separately from Gallery authentication. Completion requires
server receipt IDs/checksums, not~activity alone. Fuel defaults to~kg,
uses lb~for~Boeing/PMDG and~gallons for~common light GA. Measured simulator mass
takes priority; old volume-only mass uses a~visible 0.80 kg/L estimate. Raw data
stays unchanged. Landing rollout cannot produce a~false rejected-takeoff event.

Both actions use one durable coordinator. Standalone Stop stops the~watcher,
its~owned UTC synchronizer, and~flight recorder, then drains accepted
photographs and~completes required report delivery; unrelated AI, manual-import,
and~preflight workers remain running. Stop leaves the~key configured.
Confirmed Revoke runs the~same Stop, also stops other authenticated producers,
finishes accepted work, and~requests scoped server revocation only after required
receipts. Progress shows phase and~elapsed time; ETA appears only when measurable.
Failures retain tasks and~local files for recovery, with explicit local-only
resolution available. Restart restores work but~never restores Revoke consent;
a~new confirmation is~required. No~empty report is~created.

The~gallery-scoped token advertises the~optional
\texttt{flight\_reports\_v1} capability. The~JSON
\texttt{flight\_report\_status} action accepts \texttt{report\_id},
\texttt{language}, and~\texttt{sha256}; the~multipart
\texttt{flight\_report} action accepts those fields plus
\texttt{session\_id} and~the~PDF field \texttt{report}. The~server confines
writes to the~token gallery and~reuses general PDF validation, storage, and
protected viewing without adding schema. Stable identity, checksum, and~status
reconciliation make retries idempotent. Reports are separate from OFP,
inherit gallery visibility, and~contain no~raw telemetry.

\section{Adding, describing, and~organizing photographs}
\label{sec:photo-workflow}
\index{photo workflow}
\index{image metadata}
\index{photo organization}

\subsection{Choose an~upload method}
\index{upload methods}

Browser upload suits everyday additions. Select a~gallery, choose files, review the~batch, and~follow progress while the~server validates media and~prepares records and~thumbnails. Filesystem discovery suits large existing folder trees or~transfers made through FTP and~hosting tools. Automation and~WebDAV suit repeated or~mobile workflows after their scoped credentials are~configured.

In~the~right-hand Admin \ui{Upload photos} panel, paste successive screenshots with Ctrl+V on~Windows/Linux or~Cmd+V on~macOS, choose additional files, or~drop images into the~upload area. Each selected image appears in~a~local thumbnail queue before submission, together with its filename and~size. Move images with the~arrow controls, remove an~unwanted screenshot, or~clear the~selection. The~existing \ui{Upload} button sends the~originals through the~normal upload process once; preview tiles are not replacement upload files. This queue is~not a~server-side draft: leaving the~panel asks before discarding unsent files, a~failed upload retains the~selection for retry, and~successful server acknowledgement clears it. Unsupported preview formats show a~format tile without bypassing the~server's upload checks. The~standalone Admin upload page and~no-JavaScript form continue to~use their previous workflow.

For~a~large event, upload in~meaningful groups. Smaller groups make errors easier to~identify and~allow titles, ordering, and~visibility to~be~reviewed incrementally.


% iCloud assisted import contract
\subsection{Import photos from iCloud}
Open \ui{Import from iCloud}, paste a~public Photos Link at~\url{https://share.icloud.com/photos/} or~a~public Shared Album at~\url{https://photos.icloud.com/shared/album/}, select the~destination and~click \ui{Download and import from iCloud} beside the~link. This action recognizes the~link and~starts the~existing upload form in~one step; the~footer shows the~same action for~an~automatic source. Keep browser preparation enabled. Gallery checks public availability, requests Apple's compatible export, downloads it~to~temporary browser disk storage and~imports supported photos automatically. You~do~not~need to~download or~select a~ZIP yourself. A~new gallery must be~Private or~Unpublished; review an~existing gallery's visibility because it~also applies to~partial imports.

The~source types use separate acquisition paths. Photos Links can~expire or~be~revoked; Shared Album availability is~controlled by~the~owner, including optional temporary expiry. Gallery requests compatible JPEG/PNG/GIF representations; received JPEG/PNG/WebP/GIF images are~checked by~their actual signature. HEIC/HEIF/DNG/RAW, MP4/MOV, Live Photo motion and~sidecars are~counted as~unsupported rather than renamed or~silently converted. Compatible JPEG is~not~a~preserved RAW original; capture/GPS metadata and~Apple edits are~not~guaranteed. Apple's download protocol can~change and~a~sign-in-only source cannot be~imported anonymously.

Automatic transfer requires a~supported browser on~HTTPS with OPFS temporary disk storage. Known byte totals produce download progress; unknown totals show received bytes only. The~response is~written incrementally, never held as~one whole-archive memory buffer. The~existing ZIP worker reads bounded metadata and~one image at~a~time, preparing and~uploading small IndexedDB-backed batches. Stored and~Deflate are~supported; ZIP64 and~multi-disk archives fail explicitly. Bounds include 10,000 entries, 4~MiB directory metadata, 2~GiB expanded data and~64~MiB/the~server limit per~image. Manual ZIP selections retain a~64~MiB combined-original budget. Decoders and~thumbnails also use memory; these bounds are~not~a~total heap measurement.

\ui{Cancel import} stops acquisition and~the~active work; accepted photos remain. Keep the~same form open and~choose \ui{Retry the~same import} after an~uncertain result: the~disk source, cursor and~unacknowledged batch/session identity are~retained, so~retry does not~insert duplicates. Source and~target controls remain locked until reconciliation. Safe failure before server work and~successful completion remove temporary sources. Closing the~tab loses in-page retry; the~next acquisition removes abandoned unlocked source files older than one day. No~background import or~cross-session restart is~promised.

If~the~automatic path is~unavailable, open \ui{Manual export and details} and~choose \ui{Open iCloud for manual export}. On~Apple, choose \ui{Most Compatible} when offered, download the~ZIP or~supported images and~select them in~the~existing picker. Clear the~source for~ordinary local uploads. The~link is~cleared from its input after recognition and~retained only in~this form's memory; bearer links, Apple cookies and~credentials are~never sent to~Gallery or~stored in~drafts. Sign in~to~Apple only on~its official page.

\subsection{Write useful photo titles}
\index{photo title}
\index{captions}
\index{alternative text}

A~title identifies the~photograph quickly. A~description or~caption can~explain people, place, activity, technical context, or~a~story outside the~frame. Useful examples include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Charles Bridge before sunrise};
  \item \emph{Arrival of~the~first train at~the~restored station};
  \item \emph{Family group in~the~garden, June 1998};
  \item \emph{Final approach during the~Saturday display}.
\end{itemize}

Meaningful text improves search, accessibility, later identification, and~the~experience of~readers who want more than a~visual grid. File names can~remain operational details when public display settings favor curated titles.

\subsection{Use tags as~a~second organization layer}
\index{tags!practical use}
\index{tagging strategy}

Create a~small vocabulary before adding many near-duplicate tags. Prefer one stable term, such as~\emph{aircraft}, over several accidental variants. Tags can~represent subjects, people, places, techniques, equipment, seasons, or~editorial status.

For~a~travel archive, galleries can~represent trips and~tags can~represent recurring themes. For~a~family archive, galleries can~represent years and~events while tags identify people. For~a~professional portfolio, galleries can~represent clients and~tags can~identify deliverable type, location, and~specialty.

Tag entry fields can~suggest existing tags while you type. Reusing a~suggestion avoids accidental spelling variants and~keeps tag landing pages coherent. The~suggestion is~an~editing aid only; the~ordinary save action still decides which tags are~stored for~the~gallery or~photograph.

\subsection{Arrange the~viewing order}
\index{photo ordering}
\index{storytelling}

Ordering turns a~collection into a~sequence. Start with an~inviting image, establish place or~context, vary wide and~close views, keep closely related details together, and~finish with a~natural conclusion. Chronological order works well for~events, while visual rhythm can~suit portfolios and~exhibitions.

The~public reorder controls help administrators adjust visible cards. Photo reordering on~a~public page is~also scoped to~the~current visible pagination slice and~saves through the~authenticated image-order endpoint. When Picture Manager is~active, dragging selected photo cards over a~visible child-gallery card changes the~gesture from~reordering to~a~validated move into~that child gallery. Pagination can~divide a~large collection, so~review transitions around page boundaries as~well as~the~first page.

\subsection{Review EXIF and~location information}
\index{EXIF!user review}
\index{location privacy}

EXIF can~enrich a~photograph with capture date, camera, lens, exposure, and~GPS coordinates. This information supports maps, date suggestions, duplicate evidence, and~technical context. Review GPS visibility before publishing photographs made at~homes, schools, private workplaces, or~sensitive natural locations.

\section{Publishing, sharing, and~audience scenarios}
\label{sec:audiences}
\index{publishing workflow}
\index{audiences}
\index{use cases}

\subsection{Public portfolio}
\index{portfolio}

A~public portfolio benefits from a~small number of~strong top-level galleries, consistent covers, concise descriptions, and~careful ordering. Use tags for~specialties that cross projects. Keep source archives in~private or~unpublished branches and~copy selected work into public presentation galleries where the~workflow calls for~separate curation.

Test the~portfolio on~a~phone, a~high-density display, and~a~slower connection. Progressive sharpening is~the~default and~safe fallback for~photo cards in~the~open gallery; responsive selection remains a~supported alternative.

\subsection{Private family archive}
\index{family archive}

Organize by~year, family branch, or~event. Use password-protected parent galleries to~establish an~understandable private area, then place related descendants within it. Add names and~dates while memories are~available. Scanned photographs benefit especially from human descriptions because embedded camera metadata may~be~absent.

Share the~protected address and~password through an~appropriate private channel. Explain that recipients can~navigate child galleries without receiving an~administrator account. Periodically review whether old links and~passwords still serve the~intended audience.

\subsection{Client delivery}
\index{client gallery}
\index{proofing workflow}

Create one protected gallery per~client or~assignment. Upload proofs, organize them into useful groups, and~use descriptions to~explain selection or~download expectations. Voting can~support lightweight preference gathering when enabled for~that gallery. A~final delivery gallery can~contain approved files in~the~intended order.

Share links offer another controlled entry method where configured. Test the~exact client journey in~a~private browser window before sending the~address.

\subsection{Event and~community gallery}
\index{event gallery}

An~event gallery can~begin unpublished while photographs are~arriving. Use child galleries for~days, stages, teams, locations, or~activities. Publish the~parent when the~first useful selection is~ready, then add later groups without changing the~familiar public address.

Public voting can~highlight audience favorites. Tags connect recurring participants or~subjects. Downloads provide a~convenient collection transfer when the~event policy allows it.

\subsection{Travel and~location archive}
\index{travel archive}
\index{maps!travel use}

Country, region, city, and~trip galleries provide natural navigation. Date ranges establish chronology, while GPS maps create a~geographical reading path. Review coordinates for~accommodation and~private locations. A~descriptive introduction can~explain the~route, purpose, and~historical context of~a~trip.

\subsection{Historical and~institutional archive}
\index{historical archive}
\index{institutional archive}

Historical collections benefit from careful source notes, uncertain-date wording, names, locations, and~provenance. Use descriptions to~distinguish confirmed facts from family or~institutional recollection. Tags can~connect people, buildings, organizations, and~periods across many galleries.

Create backups before large metadata projects and~export information through available tools when planning external preservation. The~gallery can~provide an~accessible presentation layer while original preservation practices continue alongside it.

\section{Understanding your gallery data}
\label{sec:user-data}
\index{data ownership}
\index{database!for gallery owners}
\index{filesystem!for gallery owners}

Gallery owners interact with two cooperating stores: ordinary files and~a~database. Understanding their roles makes backup, migration, and~troubleshooting easier.

\subsection{What lives in~gallery folders}

The~gallery folders contain the~photographs and~folder hierarchy, so~the~core collection remains recognizable through FTP, a~hosting file manager, a~backup tool, or~a~local copy. Moving or~renaming files outside the~application can~require discovery or~synchronization so~the~database learns the~new state.

\subsection{What lives in~the~database}

The~database remembers titles, descriptions, dates, visibility, passwords and~access configuration, public paths, ordering, tags, votes, image dimensions, EXIF indexes, checksums, thumbnail records, administrator accounts, settings, audit information, and~workflow state.\footnote{Password values use hashes or~protected configuration appropriate to~their purpose. A~database export still contains sensitive operational information and~deserves secure storage.}

The~database makes search and~administration fast. It~also allows a~file to~have a~friendly title, rich description, tags, a~chosen order, and~access rules without changing the~original image bytes.

The~\texttt{public\_content\_widgets} table persists authored titles, Markdown, page scopes, seven in-flow zones, floating anchors and normalized coordinates, publication state, ordering and optimistic revision numbers. An~upgrade creates an~empty table and displays no~widgets until an~administrator explicitly publishes one. Published records are rendered on eligible public home/gallery pages; drafts and disabled records are never included in~public HTML. Back up~these records together with the~rest of~the~database.

\subsection{How users benefit from the~database}
\index{database!benefits}

Everyday benefits include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item searching words across gallery and~photo descriptions;
  \item opening a~gallery through a~stable public path;
  \item displaying photographs in~a~curated order;
  \item remembering tags and~visitor votes;
  \item protecting a~gallery with inherited access rules;
  \item finding exact duplicate content through stored checksums;
  \item preparing suitable thumbnails without re-reading every source file on~every page;
  \item recording migrations and~maintenance decisions with an~audit trail.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Backup as~one complete set}

A~complete backup includes the~gallery tree, database export, local configuration, and~installation-owned custom assets. Give these parts the~same backup date so~they describe one coherent state. Store a~copy away from the~web server and~test restoration in~a~separate location.

\section{Everyday ownership and~care}
\label{sec:daily-care}
\index{daily administration}
\index{monthly maintenance}
\index{ownership checklist}

\subsection{After every important upload}

Review the~destination gallery, photo count, ordering, titles, visibility, and~thumbnail appearance. Open several photographs in~the~lightbox and~check the~gallery anonymously. For~a~protected collection, repeat the~password or~share-link journey from a~fresh private session.

\subsection{Monthly review}

A~monthly maintenance check should include:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Review System Health and~pending migrations.
  \item Check recent Admin audit events.
  \item Inspect available application updates and~their release information.
  \item Confirm scheduled or~manual backups completed.
  \item Open representative public and~protected galleries.
  \item Review thumbnail maintenance when source files or~rendering settings changed substantially.
  \item Remove or~revoke credentials and~share methods that have completed their purpose.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Before a~major public launch}
\index{launch checklist}

Check titles, descriptions, cover images, spelling, mobile layout, privacy, GPS exposure, downloads, maps, voting, search results, and~contact expectations. Test through a~slow network profile if~the~audience may~use mobile connections. Create a~backup immediately before the~launch state.

\subsection{When another person helps administer}

Give each administrator an~individual account where the~account model supports the~intended workflow. Explain gallery scope, privacy expectations, naming conventions, tag vocabulary, backup responsibilities, and~deletion procedures. Individual accounts improve accountability in~audit records and~keep personal duplicate-review ledgers separate.

\section{Public visitor experience}
\label{sec:public}
\index{public gallery}
\index{routing}

\subsection{What a~visitor sees}
\index{visitor experience}

A~visitor opens the~site at~the~home page or~follows a~direct link to~a~gallery or~photograph. The~page presents the~site's name, navigation, gallery title, description, breadcrumbs, tags, child galleries, and~photographs that the~visitor is~allowed to~see. Protected content asks for~the~appropriate access step before revealing private material.

Each gallery has~a~public address that can~be~bookmarked or~shared. A~direct photo address opens the~gallery context and~leads the~visitor to~that photograph. Meaningful titles and~stable public paths help links remain understandable in~messages, bookmarks, and~search results.

\subsubsection{Local history and~new content}

Small icons follow each gallery title, including when the~title wraps. A~bright
dotted circle means unseen, a~part-filled circle means partially visited, and~a~quiet checked
circle means seen. A~physical branch becomes seen only after its~own page and~all
navigable descendants have been opened. Attached Smart Galleries count as~their
own items; opening one does~not mark its~physical sources visited. Covers,
search results and~background requests do~not count as~visits.
Click the~progress icon beside a~card title to~mark that gallery and~its~available
descendants unvisited again. This includes attached Smart Galleries, whose
state is~shared across their placements, and~preserves New-content markers.
Opening a~gallery again records a~new genuine visit.

The~separate sparkle means newly available content since this browser's first
observation or~last acknowledgement. It~can accompany any visit state. Opening
a~parent does~not acknowledge new content inside unopened descendants. Cosmetic
changes and~deletion alone do~not create additions.

The~header bell opens a~small translucent panel immediately beside it, without
dimming the~page. Follow a~gallery link to~open its~content.
\emph{Mark all~new content as~read} appears beside unread notifications
and~acknowledges only their content, preserving gallery visit states.
The~separate footer action defaults to~\emph{Mark all~galleries as~seen}.
Only when every available gallery is~seen does~it~offer
\emph{Mark all~galleries as~unseen}. Both directions preserve content markers
and~work without a~confirmation dialog; neither dismisses notifications.
Escape or~a~click outside closes the~panel. Narrow-screen actions retain
comfortable touch targets. The~panel matches the~PDF/music applets with a~Theme
glass surface, small accent icons and~a~compact outlined read-all action;
the~independent visit toggle remains below the~list.
When there are~no~unread notifications, the~entire news section disappears,
leaving only the~compact visit action, Close and~the~local-history note.
The~panel grows with its~content up~to~the~actual viewport height, including
browser zoom. Only then does its~compact list scroll; heading and~actions stay
visible. There is~no~fixed item-count threshold.

History stays in~this browser's local storage, independently for~each
installation. It~does~not synchronize between devices or~send visitor history
to~the~server. If~storage is~blocked, the~current tab remains usable. Unavailable
content evidence omits notifications and~their action without a~technical
warning or~a~false empty-state claim. The~visit toggle remains usable.
Authenticated administrators never see this feature, including in~anonymous
preview; after logout they receive the~ordinary visitor interface. Any~future
non-Admin Viewer account uses the~same separate visitor identity boundary.

\subsubsection{Typical visit}

One common visitor path is:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Open the~home page and~choose an~interesting gallery cover.
  \item Read the~gallery introduction and~follow child galleries or~tags.
  \item Scroll through smaller preview images.
  \item Open one photograph in~the~large viewer.
  \item Move through nearby photographs with buttons, keys, a~strip, or~the~selected viewing mode.
  \item Open a~map, vote, search, or~download when the~gallery offers those actions.
  \item Use breadcrumbs to~return to~a~broader collection.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Gallery hierarchy and~pagination}
\index{pagination}
\index{gallery hierarchy}

\subsubsection{Direct content and~stable ordering}

A~gallery can~show smaller galleries first and~photographs afterward. For~example, \emph{Italy 2026} can~contain \emph{Rome}, \emph{Florence}, and~\emph{Venice}, while also showing a~few overview photographs directly on~the~Italy page.

Pagination divides a~long collection into manageable pages. This helps the~first page appear promptly and~keeps scrolling comfortable. A~small exhibition may~look best on~one page. A~wedding with 1,500 photographs will usually benefit from pages or~child galleries. The~large photo viewer can~continue through the~wider gallery sequence while loading additional information as~needed.\footnote{Pagination changes how many cards appear at~once. It~leaves the~underlying photographs and~their order intact.}

Long public listings include a~floating \ui{Top} control after the~visitor has~scrolled into the~gallery listing. It~uses smooth browser scrolling, disappears outside the~active listing, and~stays hidden while the~lightbox or~fullscreen viewer is~open so~it~does not~compete with photo controls.

\subsection{Configurable public content widgets}
\index{public content widgets}

Open \ui{Appearance} and \ui{Widgets} in~the~Admin Zone to~create independent content panels such as \emph{Partner galleries}, announcements or~useful links. Each widget has a~title, a~Markdown text body, a~publication state, page scope, appearance and~a~saved position. Create or~edit a~draft, choose \ui{Preview without saving}, and then explicitly \ui{Save}. The~editor also supports duplication as~an~unpublished draft, numeric ordering, disabling and~confirmed deletion. A~concurrent edit from another Admin tab is rejected rather than silently overwriting a~newer revision.

Use headings, paragraphs, emphasis, lists and~links with your own visible captions in~one Markdown body. Both safe internal gallery URLs and~external HTTP(S) links are supported. Executable URL schemes, arbitrary HTML, scripts and~remote embeds are not permitted. Images or~logo uploads are not part of~the~current widget editor. Widget authors must not place privileged information in~public content.

Select the \emph{Homepage}, \emph{Gallery pages}, or~\emph{Homepage and galleries} scope. In~page mode, the~available zones are top/bottom of~public content, left/right content rails, immediately before/after the~homepage gallery grid, and~the~global footer. A~homepage-only grid position on~a~gallery page falls back to~the~bottom of~the~main content for an~all-pages widget. Side rails remain part of~the~document layout and~stack after the~main content on~narrow screens; the~navigation header and~hero are not widget targets.

In~\emph{Floating} mode choose one of~eight viewport anchors or~set custom X/Y coordinates from 0 to~1000. The~published widget may become a~fixed, dismissible panel on~a~sufficiently large desktop viewport. Up~to~two panels are enhanced at~once; unsafe overlaps, small screens, zoom or~insufficient height retain the~same accessible article in~normal document flow. The~visitor's \ui{Close} hides the~panel only on~that page view and~does not update the~administrator's publication settings. With~JavaScript disabled, authored content and~links remain in~the~initial HTML exactly once.

On public pages, floating placement is recalculated when protected controls or widget content change size, including an expanded Gallery toolbar and loaded fonts. Unsafe placement returns the same article to normal flow.

The~protected Home/Gallery preview uses the~real Theme layout and~saved widget order while keeping public scripts disabled. If Home has~no gallery cards, an~after-grid draft remains ahead of~content-bottom and~floating zones; a~new side rail follows the~same responsive stacking behavior as~the public layout. In~the~separate Admin placement map, drag with mouse, touch or~pen, click, or~use arrow keys to~set custom coordinates. Shift with~an~arrow makes a~larger step; \ui{Reset floating position} restores a~valid bottom-right preset. Desktop/tablet/mobile selectors and~the~content preview do~not save the~draft. The~position map remains advisory. Use the~actual public page for final visual verification after saving.

The~EN/CS/DE/SV Admin interface strings are translated, but authored titles and~Markdown remain in~their selected source language without automatic content translation. Theme/Custom CSS can target \texttt{.public-content-widget} or~a~stable \texttt{data-public-widget-id} selector. Widget editing itself never stores arbitrary CSS or~JavaScript.


\subsection{Search, tags, and~navigation}
\index{search}
\index{tags}

Search and~pagination use compact translucent controls in~the~selected Theme,
with a~32~px desktop search bar.
The~compact suggestion list preselects the~highest-ranked result as~results
arrive. Enter opens it; Up/Down or~pointer movement selects another result.
An~explicit choice follows that result when later phases reorder the~list.
Editing the~query resets selection to~its new top match and~immediately
disables old results. Long subtitles are~truncated; mobile rows retain
44~px touch targets. Escape clears the~query and~closes suggestions.
The~current page has~a~subtle accent tint and~line; keyboard focus remains
visible. On~narrow screens, the~search scope moves below the~field and~touch
targets grow. A~small switch selects the~search scope. Search scope and~page
navigation work as~before.

Search can~look across the~whole public site or~stay within the~current gallery and~its children. A~visitor looking at~\emph{Family archive} can~search only that branch for~a~person's name, while a~visitor on~the~home page can~search across every public collection. The~current search service begins at~two visible query characters, returns a~bounded mixture of~gallery and~photo results, and~ranks stronger title/name/tag matches ahead of~weaker text matches. The~default request returns up~to~12 merged results and~the~service clamps a~request to~at~most~30 results.

Searchable evidence includes public gallery/photo titles and~descriptions, image filenames, gallery and~photo tags and~their public descriptions, active maintained-language content where its schema is~available, and~internal AI searchable text when AI metadata is~ready. Access filtering happens as~part of~the~query: private, inaccessible, or~non-public image content is~not~returned merely because its text matches. A~branch search also constrains the~listing context to~that gallery subtree rather than~searching the~whole catalog and~hiding unrelated matches afterward.

Tags provide another route through the~archive. A~\emph{night photography} tag can~connect Prague, London, and~Tokyo even when those photographs live in~separate travel galleries. Breadcrumbs show the~current place in~the~hierarchy, and~favorite shortcuts place important galleries directly in~the~site header.

\section{Media and~gallery administration}
\label{sec:admin}
\index{administration}

\subsection{Gallery inventory and~reviewed changes}
\index{gallery!branch feature plans}
The~Admin Galleries workspace shows direct photographs, descendant photographs, subgallery counts, and~on/off/mixed feature states. Expand a~branch to~inspect its children. Stage Maps, File names, Voting, or~Picture Game changes for~a~root and~all its descendants; staging alone does not~save. Review the~exact affected rows and~dependencies, then apply the~reviewed plan. Later overlapping intentions retain their order. Picture Game requires voting; disabling voting also disables the~game. Cancel or~discard to~keep stored values. If~another edit makes the~preview stale, review again.

Quick password controls affect only the~current gallery's~own password. Enter a~new password to~enable or~replace it, or~remove the~own password. Share-token protection, ancestor protection, visibility, and~unrelated preferences stay in~force. An~outdated revision is~refused. Ordinary forms remain available without JavaScript; panel actions complete in~place and~keep the~panel open.

On~the~public home, signed-in~administrators can~create a~root gallery. The~first-gallery prompt requires a~confirmed empty catalog. Creation groups content and~location/visibility, with secondary settings disclosed; source language, tags, SimBrief drafts, and~remembered defaults remain available.

\subsubsection{Upload preferences and~mobile connections}
Open \ui{Settings > Uploads} for~format/rename preferences, browser workers and~limits, ZIP batch tuning, thumbnail source chunks, and~mobile WebDAV connections. Related limits are~normalized together; size controls display MB. Enable the~legacy upload-menu preference only if~those original links are~useful. Hidden links do~not~disable the~existing routes.

Mobile actions refresh their own workspace. Copy a~newly issued one-time password immediately. If~the~following connection-list refresh fails, the~connection remains saved and~the~password stays visible in~that response; retry the~list separately. Disabled mobile uploads do~not~read the~connection inventory.

\subsubsection{Appearance, language, and~custom stylesheets}
Appearance combines grouped controls with a~live preview and~a~bounded pointer/keyboard resizer on~wide screens. Pane size is~a~local browser preference. Site images groups branding/background tools; Layout separates shortcuts, grids, card details, and~viewer defaults. Language has~Settings, Design, Editor, and~Diagnostics. The~design preview uses that form's~offered languages and~public default; changing a~sample does not~save a~language preference.

All~five Appearance subsections use compact translucent groups and~question-mark
help beside their controls. The~shared live preview and~its browser-local divider
remain available. Reload and~Back/Forward preserve the~active subsection, including
existing deep links. GPS pin/background, display-all-tags, tag scrollbar and~header
transparency switches save immediately and~individually with factory-default
tooltips. Other colors, text, selections and~numbers remain drafts for~\ui{Save theme}.
An~unverified or~refused switch write locks that control and~blocks shared-form
replay until passive reload, without automatic retry. Ordinary form saving
remains available without JavaScript.

The~Language subsections use compact groups, switches and~contextual question-mark
help. Admin language, public default and~the~viewer language list save immediately
and~independently, without submitting other Theme drafts. After changing the~Admin
language, use \ui{Reload interface} to~refresh labels. An~unverified save locks its
group and~blocks shared-form replay until reload; it~is~not~retried automatically.
All~language selectors show bundled flags. The~active subsection survives reload
and~browser Back/Forward navigation through the~page URL. Switcher design remains
a~draft applied with \ui{Save theme}; JSON/import and~diagnostics retain explicit
actions. Native form submission remains available without JavaScript.

Keep the~installed custom stylesheet by~leaving replacement empty. Explicitly choose a~preset or~upload a~valid CSS file to~replace it; an~upload takes precedence. Replacement/reset verifies storage first and~restores prior bytes if~saving the~marker fails. If~recovery is~incomplete, retain the~available copy and~follow the~bounded warning before retrying. Deployment preserves the~installation-owned \filepath{public/assets/custom.css}.

\subsubsection{Card orientation and~operational status}
Vertical cards place the~photograph above the~description; horizontal cards place it~beside the~description. The~normal orientation migration preserves existing appearance across saved settings, physical and~Smart Galleries, sidecars, imported metadata, and~Trash. Back up~the~database, galleries, and~Trash together. If~conversion is~interrupted, resolve the~reported problem and~retry; document markers prevent double conversion. Fresh installations keep the~vertical default. Tags remain complete without JavaScript; browser expansion and~scrolling also work on~gallery cards.

Public search defaults on~only without a~saved choice and~when its feature is~enabled. Updates refresh versions, notes, and~API status together; automatic metadata checks use the~saved interval (five minutes by~default), and~existing background jobs continue independently of~new discovery. OurAirports refresh uses weekly freshness and~hourly failure backoff. Trash shows remaining retention and~safe purge availability. Presentation-schema problems also require action in~System Health.

Optional SimBrief loading sits beside Begin/Finish flight, with an adjacent
identity gear. Enter one pilot ID or username; only a successful OFP fetch saves
the identity. Loading is explicit and never required for recording. The staged
plan is consumed by the next session; plans attached during recording belong
only to that session. No previous flight plan is automatically reused.
Saved flights also offer loading the~latest OFP directly into the~selected flight,
or attaching an archived SimBrief JSON/XML export. A PDF-only OFP cannot supply
machine-readable dispatch data. Create a new local PDF version afterwards;
existing PDFs and delivery receipts remain intact.
Reports include recorded OpenStreetMap route maps independently of SimBrief.
An attached OFP adds a separate planned route, planned passengers and ramp fuel.
These values never replace measured telemetry. Coordinates come from the OFP
navlog and are retained in a bounded, AIRAC-scoped local SQLite evidence cache;
ambiguous names are not guessed, and no external navigation subscription is
required. Displayed OSM tiles are cached for at least seven days, with attribution
and a bounded cache. Missing network tiles leave the route visible on a pale
background. Mercator maps clamp polar latitudes; original coordinates remain
in~the~saved raw recording. Online tile rendering sends viewport tile coordinates
and~the~app User-Agent to~OSM, without Gallery keys or~SimBrief identity.

Both maps fit the~complete recorded and~planned route with a~small margin.
The~overview omits the~redundant note after the~phase timeline, avoiding
a~separate page containing only that note.
Detached jump-and-return position blocks are omitted from~both maps
with a~visible notice; raw samples remain unchanged. Distant starting
or~ending segments and~relocations without a~local return stay visible.

The~main screen offers Load SimBrief or~Unload SimBrief. Unload clears
the~staged/current OFP and~its route, including the~current session snapshot;
older sessions and~existing PDFs remain intact. Loading/finalization blocks
unloading. Stopped flights no~longer show their route on~the~main control.
The~SimBrief identity sheet uses the~dark app theme, validates input inline
and~accepts Enter/Escape. A~checked loaded state and~airport pair identify
the~staged/current flight's accepted plan; failed reloads preserve it. Flight
settings, history, progress and~confirmation dialogs open near their owner.
Click saved-flight headings to~toggle ascending/descending order. Starts include
seconds; times sort by~UTC instant, counts numerically and~names without case.
Created/page columns use the~newest PDF time/total known pages for~flight groups.
Expanded flights and~multi-selection survive sorting and~refresh. Missing pypdf
leaves page counts unknown while saved PDFs remain openable; missing ReportLab
shows dependency-repair instructions for~new rendering. Install requirements
into the~same Python used to~launch the~source app. Update progress closes itself
only after matching installed version and~successful restart; reboot instructions
and~errors remain visible until dismissed.

Report map backgrounds are~composed from native tiles at~least 300 dpi
at~their final PDF size. Recorded tracks and~purple dashed OFP routes stay
vector; the~overview uses a~light background and~the~last map full contrast.
New PDF names may include the~OFP flight number and~departure/arrival ICAO.
Existing editions, names and~receipts remain preserved. Two planning pages
show passengers, cargo/payload, registration, runways/alternate, cruise altitude,
cost index, distance/time, scheduled UTC departure/arrival, fuel components
and~planned weights. Missing fields remain unknown. Timed navlog altitude
and~fuel can~appear beside recorded curves: detected takeoff and~captured
simulator clocks align them even during simulator-rate changes. Known kg/lb
units are~converted explicitly; recording gaps break the~comparison. Missing
time anchors allow a~labelled comparison at~recorded route positions instead:
at~least two ordered OFP fixes must match captured legs within 10 NM. Gaps and~
position jumps still break lines; no~takeoff time is~invented. Missing matching
data suppresses comparison with a~visible explanation. OFP values remain planned
evidence; no~actual passengers, delay score or~unrecorded fuel is~inferred.
Earlier OFP snapshots remain readable; explicitly reload/import them to~obtain
new fields they did~not~retain. Map requests stay limited to~the~displayed
viewport with a~45-second network budget and~stop on~the~first provider failure.

An~attached OFP adds plan-versus-actual statistics in~all four editions while
retaining maps, profiles and~landing detail. The~final repeated-header
flightplan keeps every OFP point with planned/actual elapsed time, simulator
UTC, remaining fuel, pressure height, TAS, GS, wind and~passage distance.
Intermediate matches require ordered passages within 2 NM; airport rows use
detected takeoff/landing within 10 NM. Missing matches remain unknown.
Summary and~phase tables distinguish airborne/block time, distance, fuel,
weights and~estimated delays. More initial fuel is~separate from savings.

Vector deviation profiles show time, fuel onboard, load-adjusted net fuel use,
pressure height and~route separation at~projected OFP positions. These planning
estimates differ from the~ordered final flightplan matches. Time-weighted
histograms and~bias, MAE, RMSE and~absolute-error P95 use original saved samples,
independently of~graph thinning. P95 uses fixed 1-second, 0.1-NM and~25-ft bins;
the~overflow bin gives a~lower bound. Amber map segments exceed the~descriptive
5 NM band; height uses ±300 ft. These are~comparison bands, not~operating limits
or~pilot scores. Phase averages include IAS/TAS/GS, V/S and~track-relative wind.

New optional pressure-height, parking-brake and~Zulu-calendar observations
remain separate from host UTC. Height differences require both airport
transition references. Estimated OUT/IN requires sustained parking/motion
evidence and~does not~identify a~stand. Full flight totals require matched
airports, one captured flight and~complete relevant data. Gaps, fuel increases,
incompatible dates and~legacy missing fields suppress affected claims. Old
recordings remain readable; explicit OFP reload/import or~a~new recording
supplies new evidence. Earlier PDFs and~receipts remain preserved.

Compact analysis panels pair plan, actual and~difference in~one cell and~group
short boundary phases with climb, descent or~arrival. Original statistics stay
preserved. Green indicates lower time/net consumption or~more remaining fuel;
red indicates the~opposite. Speed whiskers show observed min/max with weighted
means; phase range bars compare planned and~actual totals. These are~descriptive
ranges, not~confidence intervals. Verified duplicate airport rows may share
the~same observed endpoint without suppressing valid summaries. Missing values
use a~dash. Legacy AMSL height is~marked with an~asterisk and~never supplies
pressure-height errors; absent simulator UTC leaves an~explicit planned-only
UTC column. The~missing-channel context is~explained once.

The~main SimBrief control marks its loaded plan; saved-flight details show
their own OFP attachment. Finish uses available loaded OFP for~the~current
or~sole pending flight. Manual generation uses it~for~the~selected saved flight
and~creates four new editions without confirmation. With no~loaded OFP, existing
evidence stays unchanged; neither action fetches a~plan or~asks for~an~identity.
Partial recordings retain incomplete facts. Success updates status without
an~OK message box and~closes progress after durable completion/key cleanup.
Errors stay visible; earlier PDFs remain intact.

The~update notice resizes to~fit its wrapped status text and~complete button row
when background checks change the~message. Text and~action-row backgrounds match
the~window; the~primary \ui{Update} action remains distinct.

The~independent uploader source targets 0.8.0. A~separately requested installer
build will place the~installer and~matching generated
\filepath{winapp-update.json} under \filepath{winapp/dist/0.8.0/}. The~installer
and~metadata belong to~the~same hosted release. This build check does~not~replace
acceptance testing of~the~installed Windows app or~the~simulator. Existing
version folders, including 0.4.0, remain preserved.

Develop integration compares the~complete installer inputs with current
\texttt{main}. Changed WinApp runtime, assets, dependencies or~build inputs
require a~version strictly greater than main's; tests, README and~instructions
are~excluded. Several integrations may~keep the~same upcoming version while
main retains its older version. After publication advances main, another runtime
change needs another bump. Admission runs before FULL and~again before protected
publication; release continues to~trust qualified develop.

\subsection{Admin workspace}

After signing in, an~administrator sees private controls for~the~dashboard, galleries, photographs, theme, tags, maintenance, updates, logs, and~account settings. Many edits open in~the~right-side panel, leaving the~gallery visible while one item is~changed and~saved.\footnote{A~full Admin page remains available for~workflows that need more space or~when browser enhancement is~unavailable.}

On~public pages, the~Admin, Test run, Updates, and~Logout actions use compact,
32-pixel-high transparent outlined controls with 16-pixel icons and~localized
labels. The~Admin login action shown to~anonymous visitors, including in~anonymous
previews, uses the~same treatment and~alignment. Language choices remain governed
by~Theme settings.

The~public gallery itself also exposes contextual Admin controls while a~normal administrator session is~active. Gallery and~photo pencil controls open the~same full editors in~the~side panel. Compact gallery removal uses the~recoverable trash workflow when Trash is~enabled; disabling Trash returns future gallery removals to~the~legacy permanent subtree-deletion service. Compact photo removal is~deliberately labeled \ui{Remove photo ... from CMS}: that quick public-page action removes the~catalog image row and~does not~run the~full source-file deletion service. Use the~normal photo/bulk deletion workflow when the~original file and~generated derivatives should also be~deleted. Anonymous-preview mode hides these contextual Admin controls.

The~Admin gallery table can~filter rows by~visibility: all, unpublished, public, or~private. Filtering is~a~browser-side view aid, not~an~access-policy change. A~row hidden by~the~filter is~also unchecked, and~the~master selection is~cleared, so~a~hidden stale checkbox cannot accidentally enter the~next bulk action. The~summary reports the~displayed/eligible gallery count.

The~gallery tree supports per-branch expand/collapse plus \ui{Collapse all} and~\ui{Expand all}. It~remembers which gallery branches the~administrator collapsed. The~browser sends the~collapsed gallery IDs through an~authenticated CSRF-protected action and~the~application stores that UI state, so~returning to~the~Admin tree keeps the~same branches closed rather than~expanding the~entire hierarchy again. Descendants hidden by~a~collapsed branch are~also unchecked before bulk work. Reordering refreshes the~tree controls when a~gallery gains or~loses children.

\subsection{Reliable in-place side-panel changes}
\index{administration!side-panel mutations}
\index{side panel!mutation completion}

When JavaScript is~available, a~persistent action started in~the~Admin right-side panel completes in~that panel. The~drawer stays mounted and~open, the~address in~the~browser remains unchanged, and~the~page is~not~reloaded merely to~show the~result. This applies to~gallery create/edit/move/delete, image upload/edit/delete/move/reorder/visibility/NSFW/cover actions, scan/import, EXIF date suggestions, thumbnail creation, Media Renamer, Metadata Organizer, Duplicate Photo Detector review/deletion actions, Upload Automation credentials, Gallery Migration target pulls, tag edits, and~Smart Gallery create/edit/placement/detach/duplicate/delete actions. A~standalone Admin page and~ordinary POST/redirect behavior remain available when JavaScript is~disabled or~the~route is~opened directly.

Only the~newest panel-open request may~replace its contents; closing or~opening another item cancels ownership by~an~older response. Keyboard focus remains inside the~open drawer and~returns to~the~control that opened it. Unsaved ordinary text can~be~held in~bounded browser memory for~the~current page and~restored when the~same form returns. Passwords, tokens, API keys, file inputs, operation identities, and~other private values are~excluded, and~nothing is~persisted in~browser storage.

The~saved database/filesystem state remains authoritative. After persistence, the~server describes the~changed entity, the~panel fragment that may~need re-rendering, and~every affected public context. Stable gallery, image, tag, and~Smart Gallery identifiers are~used instead of~assuming that the~old URL still names the~saved object. This matters after a~gallery folder/slug rename, gallery reparenting, or~tag slug change: the~application can~fetch the~new canonical render source while leaving the~visible browser address untouched. Source and~destination galleries are~both described for~moves; old/new parents and~the~moved gallery are~described for~reparenting; Smart Gallery placement changes describe every affected root or~physical parent context.

A~successful fragment request is~not~by~itself proof that the~change is~visible. The~refreshed server-rendered HTML must satisfy an~operation-specific observable condition where one exists. Examples include gallery/card presence or~absence and~full counts, exact effective visibility, saved gallery revision, image absence/visibility/NSFW state, gallery-wide image revision for~off-page changes, cover image identity, persisted image order, tag identity, and~Smart Gallery placement/order. Pagination-aware checks accept a~directly visible matching card before using aggregate counts, while an~off-page result still waits for~the~authoritative total to~converge. Thumbnail cache repair is~the~intentional exception: it~refreshes affected contexts but~has no~stable database-backed public-HTML condition.

Independent render requests can~briefly return stale data on~shared hosting. PHP Gallery therefore performs one centralized bounded retry sequence only after the~mutation itself succeeded and~the~declared condition is~not~yet observable. Each completion receives a~new operation generation; a~newer mutation aborts or~supersedes older fetches, retry wakeups, and~panel responses for~the~same context. Structurally valid but~stale HTML is~verified before replacement, so~an~older response cannot overwrite a~newer visible state. Refreshes bypass browser caches and~preserve applicable pagination, sorting, language, filter, active-tab, drawer-scroll, and~page-scroll state.

If persistence succeeds but~the~refreshed view cannot be~verified within the~bounded retry budget, the~panel reports a~synchronization problem and~keeps the~saved server change. It~does not~misreport the~mutation as~failed, silently reload the~page, navigate away, or~rewrite browser history. Authentication, authorization, CSRF, schema readiness, path safety, and~validation failures return an~expected structured error to~the~enhanced workflow and~are~not~retried. Dynamically refreshed controls remain active because the~panel uses lifecycle-safe delegated handling rather than bindings that disappear after fragment replacement.

Browser-assisted create/upload has~additional safeguards. Selecting browser processing is~an~explicit execution choice: a~missing browser capability or~failed worker preparation stops before persistence instead of~silently switching to~server thumbnail generation. PHP validates that every required prepared thumbnail size/format is~present before it~stores originals. Once browser persistence starts, direct-page fallback metadata is~never interpreted as~permission to~replay the~classic create/upload request, preventing duplicate galleries and~duplicate photographs. The~configured ZIP batch size is~a~soft packing target: one atomic photograph package that exceeds that target can~travel alone in~one batch, but~the~effective PHP upload ceiling remains a~hard limit. Windows upload automation may~send the~complete JPEG+WebP compatibility matrix; a~modern WebP-only server accepts required WebP variants and~safely skips valid extra JPEG variants, while malformed, mismatched, unknown-size, or~unsupported thumbnail payloads remain hard failures.

\subsection{Admin navigation}

The~desktop Admin menu starts expanded, with its existing function groups
and~icons. Use the~double-chevron button at~the~top to~collapse it~to~an~icon rail
or~expand it~again. The~choice is~remembered in~this browser for~this
installation; it~is~not~stored in~the~database or~shared with other browsers.
Clearing browser storage restores the~expanded default. If~storage is~blocked,
the~choice lasts only for~the~current page. Both desktop states scroll with
the~page, without a~separate menu scrollbar. Scroll the~page to~reach the~last
account actions.

On~narrow screens, a~labelled hamburger opens the~same groups below its button.
The~panel scrolls independently and~overlays the~page, so~you can~reach content
without first scrolling past the~whole menu. Close it~with the~button, Escape,
a~click outside, or~by~choosing a~destination. Keyboard focus returns
to~the~button after Escape. Moving focus outside also closes it. Opening another page
starts with the~mobile menu closed; returning to~desktop restores its separate
expanded or~collapsed choice. Without JavaScript, all~grouped links remain
visible.

\subsection{Central Settings hub}
\index{Settings!central hub}

The~Admin navigation includes a~central \textbf{Settings} page for~site-wide configuration. General now groups the~website address, site name, Admin/public languages and~navigation preferences. The~other sections cover Public appearance, Content, Media and~browsing, Uploads and~automation, Privacy and~diagnostics, and~Advanced. Existing Website address links still reach General. Stable section links and~ordinary forms remain available without JavaScript.

With JavaScript, sections load when opened and~save in~place. A~sticky save bar shows changed settings and~offers saving or~reverting the~current draft. Advanced summaries remain collapsed until needed. Address or~Admin-language changes offer an~explicit link to~the~new address or~language. The~dashboard first displays its shell, then loads Overview totals or~the~Galleries list as~needed; failed reads offer a~retry. Overview counts indexed originals rather than scanning files or~resolving gallery covers.

A~Spotlight-style field searches settings as~you type. It~matches names, descriptions, categories, and~technical names, including controls that are~owned by~specialist Admin pages. Selecting a~result opens the~relevant section or~specialist page and~focuses the~matching control. Arrow keys, Enter, Escape, and~the~clear button can~be~used without leaving the~keyboard.

Frequently used global settings can~be~edited directly from the~hub, including the~site name, public language, URL rewriting, public search where available, thumbnail rendering, the~global EXIF/GPS display default, and~development diagnostics. Other settings remain on~their specialist pages when they need more context, credentials, file uploads, destructive actions, or~larger editors. Examples include Theme layout, gallery-scoped upload API keys, telemetry, account security, Google/OpenAI configuration, custom CSS, branding, language packs, and~database maintenance.

The~hub does not~flatten existing inheritance. Tag-page layout can~continue inheriting the~global grid and~card design, while gallery-specific card, lightbox, and~EXIF/GPS choices stay with the~gallery that owns them. Context-specific gallery and~photo values are~not~turned into global settings.

Secrets are~never shown in~search results or~summaries. Passwords, tokens, API credentials, and~similar values appear only as~safe states such as~Configured, Not~configured, or~a~link to~their specialist page.

\subsubsection{Guided Setup Wizard}
\index{Settings!Setup Wizard}
\index{Setup Wizard}

Choose \ui{Setup Wizard} at~the~top of~\ui{Settings} to~start or~resume a~guided configuration draft. The~wizard follows the~same eight sections as~the~hub. Short subsections keep related choices together; \ui{Advanced} and~\ui{Expert and~specialist settings} reveal less common options. With JavaScript disabled, all subsection content remains readable and~normal form navigation still works.

Each supported setting has~an~explanation and~an~example. Keep its inclusion checkbox selected to~stage a~value, or~clear it~to~preserve the~original setting. \ui{Skip} preserves the~whole section. \ui{Next}, \ui{Back}, and~subsection navigation do~not~save application settings. Supported Theme choices share the~existing live preview; a~preview is~temporary and~does not~change the~public site.

The~wizard supports safe central Settings values, basic Theme colors and~layout, grid/card and~hero-tag options, bounded upload and~telemetry preferences, thumbnail warm-up, SEO guard, Gallery Trash, automatic updates, and~Scheduled Maintenance preferences. Credentials, API-key actions, uploaded branding, raw CSS, gallery-storage relocation, and~destructive maintenance remain informational. Their specialist pages are~not~opened from the~draft; leave the~wizard before using those workflows. Per-gallery overrides remain unchanged.

The~final review shows proposed changes first. Expand the~unchanged details or~use \ui{Show all settings} to~inspect preserved and~informational items. Select the~approval checkbox and~apply only after reviewing the~summary. \ui{Cancel} or~\ui{Restart} discards the~draft. A~draft belongs to~the~signed-in administrator and~the~current browser session; it~is~not~a~shared configuration document.

Saving validates the~current settings again. If~another session changed an~affected value, the~wizard refuses to~overwrite it. Required database storage must be~verified; missing or~unknown storage blocks the~affected save. Application and~telemetry rows use a~transaction, while website-address changes have a~reversible configuration write for~failure recovery. Trash and~Scheduled Maintenance preferences are~saved through their existing owners as~coherent groups. Secrets are~never placed in~the~draft, summary, or~wizard log.

\subsubsection{Correcting the~website address}
\index{Settings!website address}
\index{configuration!base URL}

Open \ui{Settings > General} to~correct the~public installation URL after setup. Enter an~absolute HTTP or~HTTPS address such as~\url{https://example.com}. Include a~path only when the~gallery is~actually installed in~that subdirectory. If~hosting detection added an~unwanted directory, remove it~from this field and~save.

The~save changes only the~top-level \codeword{base_url} string in~\filepath{config.php}; it~does not~create or~change a~database setting. Other configuration values, comments, and~formatting are~preserved. Both the~file and~its containing directory must be~writable. The~application rejects addresses with credentials, query parameters, or~fragments, and~refuses to~rewrite duplicate or~computed configuration values. Edit a~custom configuration manually when it~cannot be~updated safely.

After a~successful save, the~browser opens the~same Settings section at~the~new address. Subsequent requests use the~updated configuration. This setting corrects generated addresses; it~does not~move gallery files or~configure DNS and~web-server routing.

\subsection{Tag metadata administration}
\index{tags!administration}
\index{tags!metadata}

The editor uses compact translucent groups for the tag catalog, selected tag
and usage references. Groups fit beside one another on wide screens and stack
on narrow screens. The arrow beside the catalog heading jumps to the editor
below sticky navigation. Filter and sorting choices switch immediately through
the native GET form. Apply remains available without JavaScript. A question mark
beside each field explains its meaning in place;
Escape or an outside click closes the help. Review content edits and use
\ui{Save tag}. Music tags retain their internal identifiers and song references
without public tag-page actions.
