Gallery visibility and~inherited access rules are~combined with per-photo visibility, password/share-link state, and~age-restriction rules where configured. The~same authorization applies to~gallery pages, thumbnails, previews, original files, lightbox metadata, maps, search results, and~downloads.

Admin operations require an~authenticated account. Forms include a~private request token, and~the~server validates the~target gallery, photograph, and~requested operation before changing persistent data.\footnote{The~technical name for~the~private form token is~a~CSRF token. Gallery owners normally encounter it~only through ordinary Admin forms.}

\subsection{Passwords and~share links}
\index{password protection}
\index{share links}

Protected galleries store password hashes and~grant session-scoped access after successful verification. Share links use server-generated token state and~expiry policy. Password reset uses one-time hashed tokens, an~optional recovery email, configured lifetime, and~the~selected mail transport. Use HTTPS, strong administrator credentials, restricted database accounts, and~protected mail/API secrets.

Administrator cookie protection, generated URL schemes and~viewer HTTPS checks share one transport policy. Direct TLS is~recognized without proxy settings. When a~reverse proxy terminates TLS and~forwards HTTP to~PHP, configure its exact IP address or~CIDR in~\codeword{security.trusted_proxies} and~enable the~actual protocol header in~\codeword{security.trusted_proxy_protocol_headers} before upgrading. Supported headers are~\codeword{x-forwarded-proto} and~\codeword{x-forwarded-ssl}; client-IP header trust is~configured separately. The~proxy must overwrite client-supplied values in~enabled headers. Untrusted, malformed, multiple or~conflicting protocol assertions cannot prove HTTPS. Both configured proxy hosting and~direct HTTPS remain supported; forwarded headers alone no~longer upgrade public URLs or~set the~administrator cookie Secure flag.

\subsection{Viewer accounts and~invite-only registration}
\index{viewer accounts}
\index{viewer invitations}
\index{viewer account management}
\index{viewer login}

Viewer accounts are~an~optional identity layer for~personalized gallery features. They are~separate from~the~administrator account and, by~themselves, do~not~unlock a~private or~password-protected gallery. Existing gallery passwords, share links, Smart Gallery access, and~media authorization continue to~decide what a~visitor may see.

The~complete viewer-account subsystem is~wrapped by~a~master switch in~\ui{Admin > Features}. This~master feature is~disabled by~default. While it~is~off, the~public gallery shows no~viewer Login/Account controls, viewer routes are~unavailable, and~the~\ui{Viewer accounts} entry is~hidden from~the~Admin Account menu. Existing viewer identities, favourites, and~private collections remain stored; the~switch controls reachability rather than~deleting data.

After enabling the~master feature, the~administrator can~open \ui{Account > Viewer accounts}. That~page retains the~separate \ui{Viewer accounts (invite-only)} mode switch. The~mode switch controls viewer login/invitations inside the~enabled subsystem, while the~master Features switch controls whether the~subsystem is~exposed at~all.

An~administrator manages viewer identities from \ui{Account > Viewer accounts}. The~existing invite-only workflow remains available: the~administrator may optionally bind an~invitation to~an~intended email address and~copy its~show-once secret link. Opening the~link does~not~create an~account. The~recipient submits an~email address, receives a~verification message through the~configured mail transport, confirms the~verification in~the~browser, and~then chooses a~password of~at~least 15~characters. There is~no~general \ui{Register} or~\ui{Sign up} page.

The~same Admin page can~also create a~viewer account directly. Enter the~viewer email and~either provide a~temporary password that meets the~viewer password policy or~leave the~password blank so~the~gallery generates a~strong one. The~temporary password is~shown once after creation. If~the~optional account-created notification is~sent, the~message contains the~trusted login address and~instructions but~never the~temporary password; provide that password separately through a~trusted channel. With the~master feature enabled, direct account creation remains available while the~subordinate viewer frontend mode is~disabled so~accounts can~be~prepared in~advance, but those viewers cannot sign in~until that~viewer mode is~enabled.

A~directly created viewer cannot use the~temporary password as~a~durable login. After the~first successful credential check, the~browser is~sent to~the~private first-login page and~must choose a~different compliant password. Until that replacement succeeds, the~gallery does~not~establish the~normal viewer identity, issue \ui{Remember me}, or~allow viewer favourites/collections authority. The~ordinary forgotten-password reset can~also replace the~temporary credential through its~independently verified recovery flow. A~simultaneous administrator login in~the~same browser remains independent.

Existing viewer accounts are~listed on~the~Admin page with~their account state and~security controls. An~active viewer can~be~suspended with~\ui{Suspend} or~signed out everywhere; a~suspended viewer can~be~reactivated with~\ui{Restore}. Suspension blocks viewer authentication and~invalidates the~viewer's existing session, remember-me, reset, pending verification/email-change, and~prepared collection-share authority through the~central viewer lifecycle service. Restoration makes the~account active again but~does not~revive any credential or~session that existed before suspension, so~the~viewer must authenticate again normally. A~forced first-login password replacement remains required across suspend/restore. \ui{Sign out everywhere} leaves an~active account active while revoking its~viewer login authority on~all devices. These security controls remain available to~the~administrator while the~master feature is~enabled but~the~subordinate viewer frontend mode is~disabled. Turning the~master feature off hides the~viewer-management surface as~well. None of~these switches or~security controls removes favourites, private collections, collection items, photographs, galleries, Smart Galleries, gallery share links, or~administrator authentication.

Admin deletion remains a~separate destructive action with explicit confirmation. It~removes the~viewer identity and~viewer-owned authentication, lifecycle, favourite, and~collection state through the~prepared database relationships. It~does not~delete photographs, galleries, Smart Galleries, gallery share links, or~administrator accounts/sessions.

The~public header shows \ui{Login} only while viewer accounts are~enabled. After sign-in it~shows \ui{Account}. The~account page shows the~verified email, links to~the~private \ui{Favourites} and~\ui{Collections} pages, \ui{Change password}, \ui{Change email}, \ui{Delete account}, and~a~viewer-only \ui{Log out} action. Logging out a~viewer or~changing viewer account authority does~not~log out an~administrator who happens to~be~authenticated in~the~same browser session.

\index{favourites}
A~signed-in viewer can~mark an~authorized photograph as~a~favourite from the~small heart control on~a~gallery card or~in~the~lightbox. The~same photograph is~kept synchronized between the~card and~lightbox, and~the~private \ui{Favourites} page lists saved photographs that the~viewer is~still allowed to~open. Removing a~favourite deletes only the~viewer's saved reference; it~does~not~delete or~modify the~photograph.

A~favourite is~never an~access grant. If~the~source gallery later becomes private, its~password/share permission expires, or~the~photo is~otherwise no~longer authorized for~that request, the~saved favourite does~not~bypass those rules and~the~source metadata is~not~shown on~the~Favourites page. A~browser that is~simultaneously signed in~as~administrator and~viewer still needs the~viewer's independent source authorization before the~viewer can~save that image as~a~favourite.

\index{viewer collections}
A~signed-in viewer can~also create private named \ui{Collections}. A~collection is~an~ordered list of~references to~existing photographs; it~is~not~a~gallery, does~not~copy photographs, and~does~not~change the~administrator-owned gallery hierarchy. From an~authorized gallery card, Smart Gallery card, or~the~private \ui{Favourites} page, the~viewer can~use \ui{Add to collection} and~choose one of~their own collections. The~private Collections area supports creating and~renaming a~collection, removing photographs, moving visible photographs up or~down, and~deleting the~collection itself.

Collection ownership and~photograph access are~separate decisions. Saving a~photograph stores only its~internal image reference and~order. The~collection does~not~store a~gallery password, gallery share token, filesystem path, thumbnail path, or~a~remembered access decision. Each time the~collection is~opened, the~gallery re-evaluates the~current source-gallery and~photo authorization. If~a~previously saved photograph is~no~longer authorized, it~is~omitted without exposing its~title, filename, gallery title, thumbnail, EXIF data, or~reason for~denial. The~saved reference can~remain so~the~photo becomes visible again if~normal source access later returns.

This rule also applies when the~same browser is~signed in~as~both administrator and~viewer. Administrator visibility does~not~become viewer collection authority, and~a~collection can~never be~used as~a~second route around gallery passwords, share grants, age restrictions, or~media-serving checks. Favourites and~collections are~independent: a~photo may be~in~either, both, or~neither, and~deleting a~collection never deletes a~favourite or~the~underlying photograph.

\index{collection sharing}
A~viewer may create one unlisted, read-only share link from an~owned collection. The~link expires after 30~days and~can~be replaced or~revoked at~any time. The~complete secret link is~shown only immediately after creation or~replacement; later the~collection page shows only that a~share is~active plus its~creation and~expiry times. Replacing a~share makes the~previous link unusable. Revoking a~share removes only the~sharing authority and~does~not~delete the~collection, its~items, favourites, or~photographs.

A~recipient does~not~need a~viewer account. Opening the~secret link validates it, establishes only a~narrow session permission for~that one collection, and~redirects to~a~clean address without the~secret. The~link is~reusable until expiry/revocation, so~automatic mail or~chat link scanners do~not~consume it. The~shared page is~unlisted, non-cacheable, and~marked not~for search indexing.

The~share grants access only to~the~collection container. It~never unlocks a~source gallery or~photo. On~every shared view, each stored photo reference is~checked again against the~recipient's current source-gallery authorization. A~password-protected source remains hidden until that~recipient independently unlocks it through the~normal gallery mechanism; an~independent existing gallery-share permission may likewise authorize it normally. Even when the~same browser is~also signed in~as~administrator, administrator bypass is~not~used to~populate the~shared collection. Direct thumbnail/media routes continue to~enforce their own existing source rules. Inaccessible items are~omitted without exposing their hidden title, filename, path, gallery, or~reason.

Suspending the~collection owner revokes active collection shares, and~restoring the~account does~not~revive them. \ui{Sign out everywhere} only revokes viewer login authority and~does~not~revoke an~otherwise valid share. Deleting the~owner or~collection invalidates its~share without deleting source photographs. The~global Viewer Accounts feature switch also gates collection sharing and~remains disabled by~default.

Viewer \ui{Remember me} uses a~dedicated rotating viewer credential rather than~the~administrator persistent-login token. Restoring that credential signs in~only the~viewer and~does~not~count as~recent password reauthentication. Forgotten-password requests return a~generic response and~use a~two-step reset confirmation so~automated link scanners cannot reset a~password by~opening a~URL.

Sensitive account changes require recent viewer password proof. A~viewer restored only through \ui{Remember me} is~therefore asked to~confirm the~current viewer password before changing the~password, starting an~email-address change, or~deleting the~account. The~return path is~limited to~those account actions and~cannot redirect to~an~arbitrary page.

Changing the~viewer password uses the~same viewer password policy as~activation and~reset. A~successful change invalidates the~old viewer password authority, viewer sessions, and~viewer remember credentials according to~the~security lifecycle and~then asks the~viewer to~sign in~again. Saved favourites remain attached to~the~same viewer account. An~administrator signed in~through the~same browser session remains signed in.

Changing the~viewer email is~staged. Submitting a~new address does~not~replace the~current verified address. The~gallery first sends a~verification link to~the~candidate address within the~viewer mail-abuse limits. Opening that link only validates it and~prepares a~short-lived confirmation state; it~does not~change the~account. The~final change occurs only after the~viewer submits the~separate protected confirmation form. A~successful switch signs the~viewer out so~the~new verified email can~be~used for~the~next login.

\ui{Delete account} is~a~permanent viewer-only action. After recent password confirmation, the~viewer must explicitly confirm deletion. The~operation removes the~viewer account and~viewer-owned data such as~favourites and~private collections according to~the~prepared database lifecycle. It~does not~delete gallery photographs, galleries, Smart Galleries, or~their existing share links, and~it~does not~destroy a~simultaneous administrator login.

Viewer security and~private account/favourite/collection/lifecycle pages are~served as~private, non-cacheable responses. Invitation, verification, reset, and~email-change links use the~configured trusted application origin. Viewer verification, reset, and~email-change mail remains subject to~the~viewer address, visitor, and~global abuse budgets before the~configured PHP~mail or~SMTP transport is~used.

Unlisted read-only collection sharing is~available only inside the~Viewer Accounts feature. Public collection discovery, public viewer profiles, recipient editing/collaboration, viewer uploads/comments, open registration, viewer social login, TOTP, passkeys, and~magic-link login remain unavailable.

\subsection{Administrator account and~mail settings}
\index{administrator account}
\index{SMTP}
\index{Google login}

The~account area supports username and~password changes, an~optional recovery address, password-reset lifetime, and~a~test message. Recovery mail can~use PHP \codeword{mail()} or~an~authenticated SMTP transport with host, port, encryption, username, and~password settings. Treat SMTP credentials like database credentials and~never include them in~exports or~diagnostic screenshots.

Google login is~optional. The~administrator configures the~OAuth client and~authorized callback/redirect URI, then links a~Google identity only while already authenticated with the~normal administrator password session. An~unlinked Google identity is~rejected at~the~login page and~is~not~allowed to~create or~claim an~administrator profile. After linking, only that stored Google identity can~sign in~through the~Google button for~the~profile. Disconnecting the~link requires the~current administrator password. External login does~not~replace password login; if~OAuth configuration or~identity-link storage is~missing or~cannot be~verified, ordinary password authentication remains independent.

The~login form accepts the~administrator username or~the~configured recovery email. Authentication attempts are~throttled by~both visitor and~normalized submitted identifier when the~rate-limit schema is~available. The~throttle table stores hashed subjects rather than~raw IP addresses or~raw submitted identifiers. Current policy limits repeated failed logins within a~15-minute window and~password-reset requests within longer bounded windows; successful login clears the~relevant login throttle state.

Password recovery deliberately returns a~generic request result so~the~public form does not~confirm whether a~submitted username/email exists. Reset links use one-time hashed token storage and~the~configured 15-to-1440-minute lifetime. Completing a~reset changes the~password, marks the~token used, and~revokes persistent-login tokens for~that account. The~Account page can~send a~test recovery email through the~configured PHP \codeword{mail()} or~SMTP transport.

The~\ui{Keep me signed in} option uses a~hashed browser token so~an~administrator can~survive ordinary shared-host PHP session cleanup without storing the~raw persistent credential in~the~database. Persistent login remains optional. It~is~revoked by~the~appropriate account/logout security workflows and~can~be~unavailable while its schema state cannot~be~verified, without disabling the~current ordinary PHP session.

\subsection{Database readiness contract}
\label{sec:db-readiness-contract}
\index{System Health!database readiness}
\index{database!readiness}
\index{503 response}

\ui{System health} groups checks by runtime and recovery, access and sign-in,
galleries and files, maps and routes, public features and reports, and tools.
A checkmark means required storage was found; a dash means the integration is
disabled in configuration. Neither proves a complete workflow or external API
connection. Question marks explain the purpose of each check. Problems appear
first within their group with visible impact and guidance; \ui{Details} retains
safe database identifiers and request references. Opening the page does not
repair data or run migrations. Viewer diagnostics saves its preference in place,
with a native Save fallback; an uncertain save locks until a passive reload.

Features that depend on~database schema use one readiness contract. The~important distinction is~between a~schema that was~successfully inspected and~found to~be~older, and~a~schema that cannot currently be~inspected.

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.12\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.20\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.35\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.17\textwidth}}
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Meaning} & \textbf{Application behavior} & \textbf{Owner action}\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Meaning} & \textbf{Application behavior} & \textbf{Owner action}\\
\midrule
\endhead
Available & Required storage was~found and~checked. & Normal reads and~writes for~the~capability. & None.\\
Missing & Inspection succeeded, but~a~required object is~absent. & A~documented older-schema path may~remain available; otherwise the~feature asks for~a~migration. & Back up~and~apply the~pending migration.\\
Unknown & The~schema could not~be~inspected reliably. & Protected reads may~fail closed or~optional read-only output may~be~omitted. Dependent writes are~blocked. & Repair database connectivity, selected schema, or~metadata permissions.\\
Disabled & An~optional feature is~turned off. & The~feature is~not~used. & None unless the~feature should be~enabled.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\ui{System Health} and~\ui{Runtime Diagnostics} use these states consistently. A~\ui{Missing} result normally points to~migration work; \ui{Unknown} is~an~operational problem and~should not~be~treated as~proof that a~table or~column never existed. Diagnostics may~show the~capability name, status, validated database-object names, suggested checks, and~a~request reference. They exclude SQL text, raw database exceptions, credentials, tokens, cookies, and~private filesystem paths.

\subsection{Security-sensitive exceptions}
\index{NSFW Guard}
\index{password reset!database readiness}
\index{Google login!database readiness}
\index{share links!database readiness}

A~few capabilities have stricter or~more specific behavior:

\begin{description}
  \item[NSFW Guard.] If~inspection confirms the~older schema without NSFW fields, the~site retains its pre-feature behavior until migration. If~the~fields are~\ui{Unknown}, public requests that could expose age-restricted media fail closed and~NSFW editing is~paused.
  \item[Gallery access.] A~database is~treated as~the~older pre-protection format only when inspection succeeds and~all core access fields are~absent. A~mixed layout is~an~incomplete migration, so~protected routes remain unavailable until it~is~repaired. The~older publication value \codeword{draft} is~written only when the~field definition is~known to~require it; current schemas use \codeword{unpublished}.
  \item[Share-link revocation.] Revocation may~use the~smaller known field set needed to~clear the~validating hash because that operation reduces access. It~still stops when its own required fields are~\ui{Unknown}.
  \item[Persistent Admin login.] A~missing remember-login table disables persistent browser tokens but~does not~prevent an~ordinary password login from creating the~current PHP session.
  \item[Password reset.] Recovery requires both the~recovery-email field and~reset-token storage. Missing storage produces migration guidance; \ui{Unknown} storage produces a~temporary failure instead of~an~incorrect account/token result.
  \item[Google identity links.] External login requires both valid OAuth configuration and~known identity-link storage. If~that storage is~missing or~unknown, password login remains available independently.
\end{description}

\subsection{Writes, maintenance, and~credentials}
\index{database!mutation safety}
\index{uploads!database readiness}
\index{updates!database readiness}

Operations that change files, credentials, or~persistent records perform their readiness check before the~first target-changing step. This includes gallery/photo mutations, uploads, WebDAV writes, gallery migration, thumbnail maintenance, database repair/cleanup, and~application update activation. An~\ui{Unknown} result stops the~operation before the~target is~changed. A~confirmed \ui{Missing} result may~use an~older-schema compatibility path or~migration bootstrap only where that behavior is~documented.

Credential revocation follows the~same restrictive principle as~share-link revocation: it~may~use a~smaller known field set when those fields are~sufficient to~disable access. Creating or~trusting a~credential still requires the~complete storage used by~that authentication path.

When a~write is~blocked as~\ui{Unknown}, repair database inspection before retrying. When the~state becomes \ui{Missing}, back up~and~apply the~required migration. When it~becomes \ui{Available}, retry the~operation once and~check both filesystem and~database results. Resume an~existing recoverable migration/update job rather than starting a~duplicate job.

\subsection{Optional features}
\index{maps!database readiness}
\index{Picture Game!database readiness}
\index{voting!database readiness}
\index{OpenAI!database readiness}
\index{SimBrief!database readiness}
\index{telemetry!database readiness}

Optional presentation features can~degrade without disabling the~main gallery when doing so~cannot reveal protected content or~grant access. A~map may~be~omitted if~its optional storage cannot be~read, for~example, but~saving a~GPS/lightbox override still requires the~relevant field to~be~known.

Voting and~Picture Game both write persistent state. Picture Game records a~displayed pair before a~winner is~chosen, so~loading a~new pair is~also a~write boundary and~requires known storage. AI queues, telemetry maintenance, navigation tokens, saved SimBrief route data, and~similar optional writes follow the~same rule. A~feature may~return a~read-only result without optional persistence only where the~interface makes that limitation clear.

\subsection{Operational security}

Recommended practices include:

\begin{itemize}
  \item Serve the~application through HTTPS.
  \item Restrict database credentials to~the~application schema and~required operations.
  \item Keep \filepath{config.php}, backups, caches, and~generated archives outside public discovery wherever the~hosting layout permits.
  \item Apply migrations and~updates through authenticated maintenance workflows.
  \item Review audit logs and~security diagnostics.
  \item Test protected galleries through anonymous visitor sessions.
  \item Maintain coordinated filesystem and~database backups.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Media storage boundaries and recoverable migration}
\index{media!filesystem containment}
\index{gallery migration!atomic installation}

Public gallery media, covers, and gallery branding are authorized before streaming. Restricted or age-gated gallery images use private cache semantics even when an administrator is viewing them; an authenticated response is not automatically safe for shared caches. Stored cover, branding, and image paths are confined to their owning gallery directory, including checks of actual symbolic-link targets. Global Theme backgrounds and branding resolve only within their owned cache directories, and image responses accept supported raster types rather than active SVG content. The retained original Theme background is available only to an authenticated administrator; the normal public derivative remains viewable.

During gallery-to-gallery migration, asset manifest enumeration and authenticated API-key file delivery independently verify the final file's containment in the source gallery. An incoming asset is copied to a unique temporary file in its destination directory; its copied byte count and any manifest SHA-256 are checked before installation by rename. A failed copy removes the staging file without creating a partial final asset. Matching installed files are reused on retry, while conflicting files and symbolic-link destinations are refused. Browser-prepared store-only ZIP uploads also reject duplicate normalized entry names, invalid CRC-32, and incomplete or inconsistent central-directory records.

\section{Theme, presentation, and~localization}
\label{sec:theme}
\index{theme}
\index{localization}

The~Theme area controls site identity, colors, dimensions, page width, backgrounds, branding, favicon assets, language, gallery-card presentation, lightbox defaults, GPS presentation, favorite navigation, and~public thumbnail rendering. Settings use normalized scalar values or~focused service models.

Under \ui{Theme > Layout > Cards \& badges}, choose the~global default breadcrumb style. The~gallery editor's \ui{Display > Advanced} controls and~Smart Gallery presentation controls can~inherit or~override it. Each selector shows a~real rendered breadcrumb example inside a~native radio card, so~administrators can~choose by~appearance. The~inherit card in~gallery selectors previews the~currently effective Theme style. Native radio controls support keyboard selection and~work without JavaScript. The~stable IDs are~\codeword{minimal}, \codeword{chevron}, \codeword{pills}, \codeword{surface}, \codeword{ribbon}, \codeword{nodes}, \codeword{tabs}, \codeword{tiles}, and~\codeword{gradient}. Ribbon forms arrow segments, Connected nodes link decorative dots, Tabs are~compact, Tiles are~raised rectangular cards, and~Gradient adds a~Theme-colored surface with an~accent stripe. All ancestors remain reachable as~the~path wraps, including without JavaScript.

The~Theme editor does not use a~separate light/dark-mode switch. Instead, the~saved color controls and~an~optional Custom CSS skin define the~public visual palette directly. It~also sets gallery-card and~subgallery layouts, the~default breadcrumb style, and~up~to~three header shortcuts. Each shortcut can~target the~main page, one gallery, or~remain empty. Anonymous visitors see only configured gallery shortcuts that are~still public and~listed; duplicate or~deleted gallery selections are~discarded when the~settings are~saved. Leaving all three slots empty hides those favorite-gallery buttons. Gallery cards may~also show a~configurable contained-picture count badge. When that badge is~effective for~a~gallery, opening the~gallery keeps the~same branch count visible in~the~hero panel, counting images in~the~current gallery and~its accessible subgalleries under the~same public/access rules as~the~card. Public cards stack nonzero PDF and~music counts compactly below the~photo icon with tighter gaps between badges. When the~photo-count badge is~enabled, the~opened gallery's action bar shows those media counts inline immediately to~its left. Each count covers unique files in~the~gallery and~its subgalleries, counting a~recorded SHA-256 once and~following public visibility, hidden-state, access and~capability rules. Missing, unknown or~dirty derived snapshots omit that badge; a~ready zero count remains hidden. Per-gallery presentation can~inherit the~site defaults or~override the~choices offered by~the~gallery editor. Breadcrumbs wrap onto additional lines on~narrow screens; every ancestor remains an~accessible link, including without JavaScript.

The~\ui{Animations} subsection sets the~opening and~closing duration for~the~public information panel on~gallery cards and~the~Admin side panel. Each value ranges from~0~to~800~ms; 0 disables that motion. The~defaults are~320~ms and~260~ms respectively. The~public panel contains the~gallery title, description, date, and~complete tag list. Its native disclosure works without JavaScript; with~JavaScript, the~panel stays within the~viewport and~can~be~dismissed by~clicking elsewhere or~pressing Escape. The~interaction honors the~browser's reduced-motion preference.

If~the~durable initial-pass receipt is~missing, the~first successful public
HTML request for~the~home, a~gallery, or~a~Smart Gallery queues the~media-count
pass after the~response. Page rendering does~not~scan original files.

\subsection{Theme control reference}
\index{theme!controls}
\index{pagination!Theme settings}
\index{favicon}
\index{Custom CSS}

The~current Theme page is~divided into \ui{Appearance}, \ui{Branding \& media}, \ui{Layout}, \ui{Language}, and~\ui{Custom CSS}. The~following reference records the~owner-visible controls and~the~normalization enforced by~the~current build.

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.20\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.27\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.41\textwidth}}
\toprule
\textbf{Area} & \textbf{Control} & \textbf{Current behavior}\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Area} & \textbf{Control} & \textbf{Current behavior}\\
\midrule
\endhead
Appearance & Site identity and~colors & Site name plus accent, dark-accent, page-background, panel, open-gallery panel, header-title, and~gallery-title colors. The~``dark accent'' is~a~hover/outline color, not~a~dark-mode selector.\\
Appearance & Corners and~font & Corner radius is~0--32 pixels. Font mode is~\ui{Classic serif} or~\ui{Clean sans-serif}. The~live preview updates while these controls are~edited.\\
Appearance & Public page width & \ui{Default}, \ui{Wider}, \ui{Full width}, or~\ui{Custom}. Custom width is~clamped to~1024--2048 pixels and~defaults to~1440 when no~valid value exists.\\
Appearance & GPS pin & When the~GPS-maps feature is~enabled, photo-card pins and~their background underlay can~be~shown or~hidden separately. Pin size is~14--48 pixels with~26 as~the~default. The~underlay slider is~displayed as~0--48 pixels, but~the~current server normalizer treats 0 or~another non-positive value as~unset and~restores 22 pixels; use the~separate underlay checkbox to~hide the~background.\\
Appearance & Public tag pages & Dedicated tag-page columns and~rows use the~same safe maxima as~other public grids: 1--12 columns and~1--50 rows. Tag-result cards can~use the~vertical or~horizontal gallery-card design independently from ordinary gallery listings.\\
Appearance & Gallery hero tags & Sort by~usage or~alphabetically. Either show all immediately or~show 1--200 tags before the~in-place expansion control; the~default threshold is~20. Optional scrolling uses 1--12 visible rows, default~5, and~activates only when the~rendered tags actually wrap beyond the~configured rows.\\
Appearance & Interface animations & Set the~gallery-card public-information-panel duration to~0--800~ms (default~320~ms) and~the~Admin side-panel duration to~0--800~ms (default~260~ms). A~duration of~0 disables that motion.\\
Layout & Header shortcuts & Three ordered slots. A~slot can~be~empty, point to~the~main page, or~point to~a~gallery found by~the~gallery picker. Public visitors see only public/listed gallery targets.\\
Layout & Default breadcrumb style & Set the~global default under \ui{Theme > Layout > Cards \& badges}. Gallery overrides can~inherit it; invalid values safely fall back to~the~built-in \codeword{chevron}.\\
Appearance & Gallery-card design & \ui{Vertical system} places the~photograph above the~description; \ui{Horizontal system} places it~beside the~description. Fresh installations start with vertical cards; migration preserves older installations' appearance. A~physical gallery can~inherit or~override the~Theme choice where the~presentation schema is~available.\\
Layout & Picture-count badge & Global contained-picture badge is~enabled by~default. Individual galleries can~inherit it~or~save an~override. When enabled for~the~opened gallery, the~hero panel also shows the~current gallery's branch image count, including accessible subgalleries.\\
Layout & Public thumbnail renderer & \ui{Progressive thumbnail sharpening} is~the~default and~safe fallback. \ui{Responsive browser selection} remains the~supported legacy choice with complete server-rendered candidates and~no-JavaScript behavior. Covers and~collages retain their responsive pipeline.\\
Layout & Pagination & Global pagination is~off by~default. Columns default to~3 and~are~clamped to~1--12; rows default to~3 and~are~clamped to~1--50. Effective items per~page are~columns multiplied by~rows.\\
Layout & Main-page grid & The~front page has~its own 1--12-column and~1--50-row grid. This does not~change grids inside galleries.\\
Layout & Per-gallery grid reset & \ui{Reset all custom gallery grids} clears database overrides, restores default subgallery-inheritance state, and~removes matching grid keys from~\filepath{gallery.json} sidecars so~a~later discovery pass cannot re-import stale grid settings.\\
Layout & Lightbox default & \ui{Single image}, \ui{Picture strip}, or~\ui{3D carousel} while the~lightbox-modes feature is~enabled. The~classic single-image mode is~the~fallback and~the~only mode exposed when that feature is~disabled.\\
Branding & Public header banner & Optional Theme-level JPG/PNG/GIF/WebP banner, maximum 8~MB. It~replaces the~visible site title when no~gallery-specific banner is~configured.\\
Branding & Header separator & Optional JPG/PNG/GIF/WebP separator, maximum 8~MB. Width~0 follows the~responsive page width; a~positive width is~clamped to~160--3840 pixels. Height defaults to~72 and~is~clamped to~8--512 pixels. Normal mode preserves aspect ratio and~treats height as~a~maximum; stretch mode uses the~configured render box exactly and~can~distort the~image intentionally.\\
Branding & Favicon & Upload JPG/PNG/GIF/WebP, drag a~square crop, and~zoom the~crop from~1--3 times. Saving produces 32-, 48-, and~180-pixel PNG favicon variants.\\
Branding & Theme background & Preserve the~original upload and~optionally generate a~WebP derivative when GD/WebP support is~available. The~optimized longest side is~1024--3840 pixels, default~1920, with~128-pixel slider steps. It~can~be~regenerated or~removed without deleting the~original.\\
Branding & Background visibility/fallback & Background visibility is~0--100 percent, default~65. The~gallery fallback source can~be~none, a~new Theme upload, an~existing gallery image, or~a~collage generated from~public galleries. Separate actions reset all per-gallery background selections, remove the~Theme background, or~remove the~favicon.\\
Custom CSS & Preset skin or~upload & Presets are~the~CSS files currently present in~\filepath{custom_css/}; selecting one copies it~to~\filepath{public/assets/custom.css}. A~manually uploaded \filepath{.css} file replaces that active file. The~active custom stylesheet loads after built-in CSS and~before generated Theme rules.\\
Custom CSS & Reset actions & \ui{Reset custom CSS} deletes the~active custom stylesheet and~clears its preset marker. \ui{Reset to CSS} clears saved palette/font/radius/background-source/GPS visual overrides so~the~underlying CSS becomes authoritative again; structural page-width settings are~intentionally preserved.\\
Language & Admin/public language & Admin language is~stored for~the~administrator's session/browser; the~public language is~the~site-wide anonymous default. The~viewer selector is~an~independent visitor override, described below.\\
Language & Pack maintenance & Supported packs show string counts and~coverage. The~JSON editor validates an~object containing string values, imports/exports JSON, and~can~clear authenticated missing-key diagnostics. English remains the~source/fallback catalog.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Advanced appearance and~manual CSS overrides}

Open \ui{Theme > Appearance > Advanced appearance} for~gallery-grid gaps (0--64 pixels), gallery-card internal padding (0--48 pixels), card elevation (current, none, soft, raised), and~public content typography (80--140 percent). The~defaults retain the~installed appearance: 16-pixel spacing/padding, current elevation and~100-percent typography leave existing component rules intact. Sliders have numeric alternatives and~visible units; reset restores only the~selected control. The~existing preview follows unsaved changes; save Theme to~apply them.

\ui{Transparent site header} defaults off. Enabling it~removes the~public top navigation container's background, image, blur, border highlight, shadow and~pseudo-element layers. The~logo, title, navigation, language selector and~administrator links retain their dimensions and~click targets. The~hero panel and~Admin interface keep their backgrounds. Choose a~readable Header text color for~the~underlying page background; resetting the~switch restores the~existing header backdrop independently.

Open \ui{Theme > Custom CSS > CSS overrides / Editor} to~edit the~final public CSS layer. \ui{Save overrides}, \ui{Discard / reload} and~confirmed \ui{Clear overrides} operate independently from~ordinary Theme saves and~the~preset/upload asset. The~editor marks unsaved text and~warns before navigation. \ui{Preview draft} links the~draft stylesheet inside an~isolated, sandboxed public sample; it~does not save or~style the~Admin page. Ordinary selectors, custom properties, media queries and~\codeword{!important} are~supported. The~visible selector example is~illustrative.

The~cascade order is~built-in styles, installed \filepath{public/assets/custom.css}, generated Theme rules, and~finally \filepath{public/assets/custom-overrides.css} on~public pages. Specificity and~importance still apply. Both custom assets remain installation-owned; deployment/integrity inventories exclude them. Manual overrides survive updates, presets, uploads and~unrelated resets. Saving or~clearing changes the~SHA-256 stylesheet revision or~removes its~link, so~new public loads use the~current CSS.

Only authenticated administrators with CSRF authority may~read the~editor or~invoke its~actions; saved CSS itself is~a~public asset. Input must be~UTF-8 and~at~most 256~KiB, without binary control characters. A~fixed-path writer lock, staged byte verification and~atomic replacement preserve the~prior file on~failure. Stale revisions are~refused: copy the~draft, reload the~saved version and~reconcile before retrying. CSS syntax mistakes use browser recovery and~can~be~corrected by~reopening the~editor; they do~not corrupt Theme settings. Missing/unwritable storage reports a~bounded error.

\paragraph{Visual editor availability.} The~launch button remains visible and~disabled until JavaScript loads the~workspace and~validates the~protected preview address. If~startup fails, its localized explanation identifies an~invalid address, a~different origin, a~missing preview marker, unavailable modules, or~failed initialization. Continue in~Manual CSS; ordinary Theme controls and~explicit saving remain available. With JavaScript disabled or~blocked, the~initial message says that the~editor is~not ready. The~preview uses the~current installation path and~browser origin, including subdirectories and~nonstandard ports, independently of~the~configured public domain. A~redirect to~another origin is~still refused. If~the~entire editor section is~absent, check deployed files; a~disabled launcher and~a~preview refusal are~different states.

\paragraph{Live Visual CSS Editor.} The~visual editor opens the~real public home or~gallery page in a~script-disabled preview, with the~Admin controls outside the~public document. Switch between signed-in and~anonymous visibility and~desktop, tablet or~mobile widths. Select an~element to~inspect its stable selector, match count, effective style and~any existing visual-editor value. Inspection alone does not change CSS. A~deliberate edit uses a~limited set of validated properties; advanced CSS variables, functions and~rules remain available in Manual CSS. Choose the~site, tablet (maximum width 768~pixels) or~mobile (maximum width 480~pixels) scope explicitly. The~preview width does not create responsive rules automatically. If existing CSS contains \codeword{@import}, the~visual preview is refused while the~CSS draft remains unchanged and~available in Manual CSS. The~shared-layout controller prepares the~marked stylesheet URLs and the~layout view only renders them. Bootstrap carries preview context to dependent resources only on same-origin, in-mount GET/HEAD requests with an~exact-origin referrer; cross-origin resources receive no~preview referrer. The~server refuses an~import before its target can load. The~preview also refuses an~installed \filepath{public/assets/custom.css} file larger than 8~MiB or~a~stylesheet it cannot inspect safely; this bound applies only to preview inspection. Manual CSS remains available, and~reducing an~oversized installed file to~8~MiB or~less permits visual inspection again.

\paragraph{Selecting and styling elements.} Ordinary clicks in the~preview select elements but do~not activate links or~actions. To navigate, select a~link and~choose \ui{Follow link}; \ui{Style element} opens the~selected element's controls. Use Tab to~reach focusable preview controls; Enter or~Space selects the~focused element without activating it, and~the~inspector's ancestor buttons move selection to~a~containing element. Resize handles have accessible directional labels but use pointer or~touch dragging; Escape cancels an~active drag. For keyboard edits, use the~properties offered by~\ui{Style element}.

\paragraph{Visual session controls.} \ui{Reset visual session} restores the~CSS and~pending image captured when the~workspace opened, clears visual undo/redo history and~keeps the~workspace, preview route, audience and~viewport open; it sends no request. \ui{Clear draft} empties the~CSS draft and~removes the~pending image, while \ui{Undo clear draft} restores that one-step snapshot. \ui{Restore saved CSS} replaces the~draft with the~saved snapshot and~clears the~pending image; when unsaved work exists, confirm the~replacement. It does not save or fetch current server state: the~textarea and~CSS revision are set to the~last confirmed saved snapshot rendered on this page or returned by the~last successful Save. Later saves from another tab are not reconciled; a~subsequent explicit Save can therefore be refused as stale. Escape first closes an~open inspector or~popover; otherwise choose \ui{Apply to CSS editor \& exit}, \ui{Discard session} or~\ui{Continue editing}. Discard restores the~entry CSS and~file and~closes the~workspace. These session actions do~not send a~request. The~separate confirmed \ui{Clear overrides} action remains the~way to~delete the~persisted stylesheet. Use \ui{Collapse controls} and~\ui{Expand controls} to~change the~workspace panel layout.

Color controls preserve alpha. A~hero color change accounts for the~theme's real pseudo-element overlay; resetting it removes only the~editor-owned companion rule and~preserves an~independent override or~hand-written CSS. Supported resize handles appear only for the~approved hero, public layout, card and~media profiles. A~drag previews temporary styles, commits one undoable edit when released, and~can~be~cancelled with Escape. Media retains its aspect ratio; inline text and~ambiguous layout axes are not resized.
\paragraph{Styling profiles.} The~\ui{Text}, \ui{Header}, \ui{Hero}, \ui{Card}, \ui{Media}, \ui{Layout} and~\ui{Links and buttons} profiles expose only their supported controls. Links and~buttons support foreground and~background color with alpha, typography, borders, radius, padding and~shadow; headings and~paragraphs offer text decoration. Header and~hero expose separate standard and~WebKit backdrop filters. Reset removes only the~managed property; Undo and~Redo preserve each edit as one CSS-draft step.

The~Page width control offers \ui{Default}, \ui{Wide}, \ui{Custom} and~\ui{Full width}; custom values range from 1024 to~2048~pixels. Custom width has a~synchronized slider and~editable numeric pixel field. A~separate readout shows the~effective rendered main-content width in~the preview, including viewport clamping. Preview changes the~header, main area and~footer together. Reset removes the~managed width declaration and~reveals the~underlying Theme or~Manual CSS draft value. Background opacity affects only the~image layer, not page content. Preview and~draft edits do~not save Theme settings.
\paragraph{Page width precedence.} An~unsaved visual width is a~CSS-draft override that takes precedence over the~saved Theme Appearance width in the~preview. After the~existing explicit CSS save, the~rule also applies on public pages; neither the~draft nor saved CSS changes the~Theme setting.

The~visual draft stays in memory until \ui{Apply to CSS editor \& exit} merges its managed block into the~existing CSS textarea. \ui{Cancel \& exit} restores the~entry draft. Neither action publishes CSS: only the~existing explicit \ui{Save overrides} request saves the~stylesheet. A~selected background image also remains a~browser-only pending file until reviewed and~submitted with that save. The~save validates CSS and~image revisions together and~stages the~new background and~stylesheet as one coordinated operation; CSS-only saves remain compatible. A~stale revision keeps the~local draft and~file for review, while an~invalid image receives a~validation error. If an~operational failure leaves database rollback uncertain, retain immutable background assets that may still be referenced and~review Theme settings before retrying. The~editor does not write a~second stylesheet, setting or~server draft.
The~background review targets only the~global Theme image. Its~source label reports the~actual authorized image visible on the~current page: the~global image, a~Theme gallery fallback, a~gallery-specific background, or~no image. If a~gallery-specific background or~Theme fallback is visible, the~editor disables \ui{Replace or choose image} and~\ui{Remove background} and~guides you to~Home; it~does not silently change that gallery's cover or~background source. Choose \ui{Keep current}, \ui{Replace or choose image} or~\ui{Remove background} as an~in-memory operation. Remove deletes only the~global Theme image and~its optimized derivative; it~preserves the~Theme gallery fallback mode, per-gallery background choices and~gallery cover assets. Replace, Remove and~a~pending file take effect only through the~existing explicit Save, which sends the~global target, selected operation and~background revision. Keep remains available to~discard a~pending global operation while viewing an~independent gallery background.
If the~global image or~its~revision cannot be read safely, only image operations become unavailable; the~existing localized message explains this, while Manual CSS and~CSS-only Keep Save remain usable. A~readable state with no~saved global image is~normal and~allows the~first upload. If a~safe preview URL cannot be prepared, the~visual launch remains unavailable; background draft controls may~still initialize, but no~preview frame is~shown.
When the~current preview target is~the global Theme image, \ui{Background fit} offers \ui{Cover} or~\ui{Contain}; \ui{Background position} shows the~effective horizontal and~vertical percentages and~lets you edit each from 0 to~100\%, with its numeric field synchronized to~the slider. Each reset removes only the~editor-managed declaration and~reveals the~underlying Theme or~handwritten CSS draft value; each edit can~be~undone. These controls write only the~site-scope managed rules for \codeword{body.public-page .theme-background-image}; they do~not change Theme settings or~gallery-specific and~Theme-fallback hero images. If stronger CSS prevents an~override from taking effect, check Manual CSS for \codeword{!important} or~a~more specific selector. Unsupported current effective values are reported for review in Manual CSS rather than silently converted. To replace an~unsupported position, enter valid percentages for both axes; a~partial pair leaves the~draft unchanged, and~an~empty field does~not mean 0\%.
\subsection{Lightbox browsing modes}
\index{lightbox!browsing modes}
\index{picture strip}
\index{3D carousel}

The~Theme editor defines the~default public lightbox presentation. \ui{Single image} keeps the~classic viewer. \ui{Picture strip} adds nearby thumbnail previews below the~active photograph. \ui{3D carousel} places a~small set of~neighboring photographs behind the~active one to~provide more visual context while browsing.

A~physical gallery can~inherit the~Theme default or~save its~own lightbox-mode override. Smart Gallery presentation can~also resolve an~effective lightbox mode from~its own presentation settings and~the~site defaults. The~modes share the~same authorized lightbox metadata and~navigation pipeline, so~fullscreen, slideshow, zoom, maps, voting, and~progressive quality behavior continue to~follow their ordinary rules. Disabling the~global lightbox-modes feature switch hides these browsing controls without changing stored photographs.

\subsection{Choosing the~interface language}
\label{sec:language-selection}
\index{language}
\index{localization!language selection}
\index{translation packs}

PHP Gallery treats the~language of~the~program interface separately from the~text that you write yourself. Changing the~interface language changes buttons, menu items, help text, status messages, dialogs, and~other application wording. Gallery and~photo titles/descriptions can~have optional maintained-language versions, but~they are~edited and~saved separately; tags and~other owner content remain unchanged.

Open \ui{Theme > Language}. Two independent selectors are~available:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \ui{Admin interface language} changes the~language seen by~the~administrator in~the~current browser.
  \item \ui{Public visitor language} sets the~default interface language for~public pages.
\end{itemize}

The~optional \ui{Viewer language selector} lets individual visitors override that public default in~their own browser. The~administrator chooses which maintained languages appear in~the~public selector; disabling the~feature returns visitors to~the~site-wide public language. Admin and~public languages do~not~have to~match.

Public pages use a~compact language control with native-language labels and~locally served flag icons. Choosing a~language keeps the~visitor on~the~same public page and~remembers the~choice for~later visits in~that browser. \ui{Use site default} removes the~personal override. The~equivalent \codeword{?lang=} parameter remains available for~direct links and~no-JavaScript use.

\subsubsection{Customizing the~viewer selector}

The~selector has~five presets: \ui{Classic}, \ui{Solid pills}, \ui{Outline}, \ui{Soft cards}, and~\ui{Minimal}. \ui{Settings > General} provides the~basic preset/flag choices, while \ui{Theme > Language} contains the~detailed design controls and~live preview. These appearance settings do~not~change the~offered languages, site default, Admin language, or~a~visitor's personal choice.

\subsubsection{Multilingual gallery and~photo text}
\index{localization!gallery content}
\index{localization!photo titles}

Gallery and~photo editors keep the~existing title and~description as~source content. Use \ui{Language of~the~current title and~description} to~classify that text as~English, Czech, German, Swedish, or~leave it~\ui{Not~specified}. Existing installations are~not~automatically classified as~English.

Open \ui{Other languages} with the~globe control to~enter optional translated titles and~descriptions. Gallery titles and~descriptions fall back independently. A~saved photo-language row is~instead treated as~one caption variant: blank fields remain hidden rather than mixing source-language text underneath translated photo text. Leaving both translated fields blank removes that language row. Saving occurs through the~normal gallery/photo form and~remains in~the~existing Admin side panel.

Public pages use the~viewer's selected maintained language. Matching text is~shown on~cards, gallery descriptions, photo overlays, lightbox metadata, search results, alt text, and~SEO metadata. Missing gallery fields fall back to~source content; a~present photo caption variant displays only its saved translated fields. Translations do~not~change URLs, slugs, folders, filenames, ordering, visibility, passwords, NSFW rules, or~media authorization.

If~OpenAI text assistance is~configured, \ui{Suggest translation with OpenAI} sends the~source field and~inserts a~reviewable draft in~the~target field. The~draft is~never saved or~published automatically. Manual translation remains fully available without a~provider.

\subsubsection{Available languages and~their current state}
\index{language!English}
\index{language!Czech}
\index{language!German}
\index{language!Swedish}

English is~the~canonical source language, the~new-installation default, and~the~fallback language. Czech, German, and~Swedish are~also maintained as~complete catalogs. These four are~the~currently selectable interface languages.

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.20\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.10\textwidth} >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.58\textwidth}}
\toprule
\textbf{Language} & \textbf{Code} & \textbf{Current role}\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Language} & \textbf{Code} & \textbf{Current role}\\
\midrule
\endhead
English & \codeword{en} & Complete canonical source, new-installation default, and~fallback language.\\
Čeština & \codeword{cs} & Complete maintained selectable Czech translation.\\
Deutsch & \codeword{de} & Complete maintained selectable German translation.\\
Svenska & \codeword{sv} & Complete maintained selectable Swedish translation.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\important{Only English, Czech, German, and~Swedish are~currently selectable. English remains the~fallback if~a~maintained translation is~missing.}

\subsubsection{How translation packs work}
\index{translation packs!fallback}
\index{translation packs!JSON}
\index{translation packs!diagnostics}

A~translation pack is~simply a~dictionary of~named interface messages. The~program asks for~a~stable message key such as~``save gallery'' or~``delete selected photos'', and~the~active language pack supplies the~wording that should be~shown. The~internal key stays the~same in~every language; only the~displayed text changes.

The~\ui{Detected language packs} table shows coverage for~the~supported languages, and~the~\ui{Diagnostics} subsection records missing keys observed during an~authenticated Admin session. English supplies fallback wording when a~maintained translation lacks a~key.

The~\ui{Pack editor} is~intended for~careful maintenance or~for~importing a~prepared translation. Language data is~stored as~JSON. In~that editor, the~text on~the~left side of~each JSON pair is~the~stable key and~should not~be~translated. The~text on~the~right side is~the~value that may~be~translated. Placeholders such as~\codeword{\{count\}}, \codeword{\{gallery\}}, or~\codeword{\{time\}} represent live values inserted by~PHP Gallery and~must keep the~same names in~every language.

A~language pack can~also be~exported as~JSON, translated outside PHP Gallery, and~imported again. Imports are~accepted only when the~file is~a~JSON object containing text values, so~a~translator can~work on~the~pack without receiving access to~the~gallery server.

For~developers and~maintainers, the~maintained catalogs live under \filepath{app/lang/}. JSON is~the~canonical editable format. English, Czech, German, and~Swedish PHP dictionaries are~retained only as~compatibility fallbacks for~installations that do~not~have the~corresponding JSON file. Server-rendered pages and~browser JavaScript use the~same active-language plus English-fallback model, so~a~translation does not~need to~be~duplicated in~separate front-end and~back-end catalogs.

Custom CSS supports installation-specific visual refinement. Theme-generated CSS exposes validated variables and~computed selectors through a~dedicated route. Gallery branding can~override selected site-level assets while preserving effective fallback behavior.

\subsection{Gallery hero tags}
\index{tags!gallery hero}
\index{theme!gallery tags}

The~\ui{Appearance > Gallery tags} subsection controls the~compact tag collection below the~title of~an~open gallery. The~tag area uses the~complete width of~the~hero panel, so~tags can~continue wrapping toward the~right edge instead of~being limited by~the~normal readable paragraph width. Direct gallery tags and~tags collected from contained content remain separate semantic groups, but~each group follows the~same global display policy.

The~same subsection also controls the~public tag landing page. \ui{Galleries per~row} and~\ui{Rows per~page} define the~tag-result gallery grid and~its page capacity, while \ui{Gallery-card design} selects the~vertical or~horizontal card presentation for~those results. These values affect public pages opened through a~tag; they do~not~change ordinary gallery pages. If~the~dedicated values have not~been saved, the~global Theme grid and~gallery-card design remain the~fallback. The~global pagination switch still determines whether a~long tag result is~split into pages. From \ui{Edit tags}, \ui{Configure tag display} opens this subsection directly, and~\ui{Manage tag metadata} provides the~reverse contextual link.

By~default, the~initial server-rendered page shows 20 tags. The~limit can~be~changed from 1 to~200 with either a~range slider or~an~exact number field. When more tags exist, the~ellipsis control opens an~\ui{All tags} panel containing every direct and~contained tag in~its original group order. The~panel uses the~same animation and~dismissal behavior as~gallery cards, keeps the~header dimensions stable, and~wraps long names. With JavaScript, it~stays inside the~viewport and~can~be~closed through the~same control, an~outside click, or~Escape; Escape returns focus to~the~control. All tags are~already in~the~HTML, so~opening the~panel needs no~server request. Without JavaScript, native disclosure opens the~panel below the~header and~keeps long lists reachable by~scrolling. Enable \ui{Display every tag immediately} to~show every tag from the~first response; an~exact-limit collection also needs no~disclosure control.

Tag ordering is~configurable as~\ui{Most used first} or~\ui{Alphabetical}. \ui{Most used first} is~the~default. Usage is~the~total number of~direct tag assignments to~galleries plus direct tag assignments to~photographs across the~installation. Equal usage counts fall back to~a~natural case-insensitive alphabetical order and~then a~stable identifier tie-break. Direct gallery tags are~sorted within their group and~contained tags are~sorted within their group; the~groups are~not~mixed together.

The~tag preview can~also use an~internal scrollbar. This option is~enabled by~default with a~five-row height cap; the~row setting can~be~changed from 1 to~12 with a~slider or~exact number field. The~server supplies the~height cap in~the~initial markup, so~the~configured preview does not~wait for~JavaScript or~change geometry when the~module starts. Wrapping follows the~available width, and~scrolling is~needed only when the~preview exceeds the~cap. The~open overlay is~not~clipped by~this cap. Disabling the~scrollbar allows the~preview to~grow naturally.



\section{Downloads, sharing, and~transfer}
\label{sec:sharing}
\index{downloads}
\index{ZIP}
\index{WebDAV}

Gallery downloads prepare authorized content as~ZIP archives and~stream the~finished archive to~the~browser. A~normal gallery download traverses the~permitted gallery subtree; Smart Gallery downloads re-evaluate the~saved query and~current access rules; Picture Manager selection downloads package only the~currently selected owned photographs; the~administrator can~also request an~all-galleries archive. Archive entry names are~normalized to~prevent traversal and~keep deterministic paths.

On~a~modern browser, a~public gallery or~Smart Gallery download first requests a~private, bounded manifest. The~browser then requests each authorized original through an~independent media endpoint and~assembles the~ZIP locally, showing progress and~allowing cancellation or~retry without holding one long PHP archive request open. Every source request repeats the~current gallery, Smart Gallery membership, visitor visibility, media-version, and~path-containment checks; an~image identifier alone never grants access. File-count, cumulative source-byte, duplicate-name, memory, and~ZIP64 limits keep the~workflow bounded. Direct links and~no-JavaScript clients retain the~existing server-side ZIP fallback.

Generated gallery/all/selection ZIPs use a~content signature derived from~the~relevant gallery/image metadata so~a~still-current cached archive can~be~reused and~a~changed collection produces a~new cache identity. This \emph{content signature} is~cache invalidation metadata, not~a~public HMAC download authorization token. Authorization is~checked by~the~download controller before an~archive is~served.

\subsection{Optional original PDF and~music files in~ZIP downloads}
\index{ZIP!optional media}
New and~upgraded installations include photographs only. In~Admin Settings, the~Media category provides two independent global switches for~PDF and~music originals. The~physical gallery editor provides corresponding gallery switches and~individual original-file preferences in~the~PDF and~music managers. All~three levels default to~OFF; inclusion is~permitted when the~global, source-gallery, or~item switch is~ON. A~higher-level switch overrides lower-level ZIP preferences without changing the~saved lower-level choices or~standalone music/PDF download permissions.

Archive inclusion never bypasses inherited gallery access, visibility or~hidden state, feature master switches, file-integrity checks or~Smart Gallery rules. Smart ZIPs include originals only from~physical galleries contributing at~least one authorized matching photograph; they do~not sweep unrelated source trees. Eligible files use~the~\texttt{PDF/} and~\texttt{Music/} ZIP folders, preserve original extensions, and~are~deduplicated by~recorded content identity. Browser manifests, individual media delivery and~bounded server ZIP fallback recheck current authorization; progress, cancellation, size limits and~ZIP64 continue to~apply. Media-count badges remain inventory counts, not~the~number of~items selected for~an~archive.

\subsection{Gallery-to-gallery migration}
\index{gallery migration}
\index{API migration}

The~gallery editor's API area supports both transfer directions between compatible PHP Gallery installations. \ui{Export this gallery to~another instance} treats the~current gallery as~the~source and~uses a~gallery-scoped API key created for~the~receiving parent gallery. \ui{Import into this gallery from~another instance} treats the~current gallery as~the~receiving parent and~uses an~API key created for~the~remote source gallery. The~import creates a~new child gallery below that parent; it never overwrites or~moves the~selected receiving gallery.

\ui{Include subgalleries} is~enabled by~default. When selected, the~versioned manifest describes the~source gallery and~its complete descendant hierarchy in~parent-first order. The~target recreates that hierarchy below the~new imported root and~preserves supported gallery/image metadata, translations, flight-map state, originals, existing thumbnails, and~gallery branding assets. Turning the~option off transfers only the~selected source gallery. The~current compatibility policy requires source and~target to~run exactly the~same application version.

The~receiving server creates a~deterministic package plan bounded by~its upload limits. Each original remains grouped with its already-generated thumbnails, while gallery-level assets can~be~packaged independently. ZIP entries use stable asset keys, store existing bytes without recompressing photographs, and~are~checked against the~manifest's sizes and~SHA-256 checksums before installation. Exact API-key scope, gallery-tree structure, target paths, database readiness, and~asset completeness are~validated at~their mutation boundaries.

The~administrator chooses a~reconnect interval from~5 to~300 seconds. After a~timeout, reload, or~interrupted connection, the~active side asks the~durable target job which asset keys are~already present and~skips completed packages instead of~blindly retransmitting them. Folder mappings and~received assets are~saved incrementally so~the~same transfer can~resume safely. Completion remains a~separate step and~is refused until every declared asset is~present. Cancelling the~browser workflow stops further requests; credentials should still be~revoked when a~temporary migration key is~no~longer needed.

\subsection{Mobile WebDAV transfer}
\index{WebDAV!mobile upload}

\ui{Settings > Uploads} creates a~WebDAV-compatible connection for~one selected gallery. Record a~recognizable label, choose the~gallery, create the~connection, and~copy the~generated server URL, username, and~password into the~mobile application. The~password is~shown only once and~stored as~a~hash. Existing rows show their last-use time and~can~be~deleted immediately from~Admin.

Use the~WebDAV target type in~clients such as~PhotoSync. Current server ingestion accepts JPG, PNG, GIF, and~WebP, so~enable HEIC-to-JPEG conversion in~the~client where available. WebDAV uploads do~not~bypass gallery ingestion rules: the~credential is~gallery-scoped, authentication is~required on~every write, the~gallery/image schema must~be~known, and~accepted media is~then scanned, optionally auto-renamed, and~processed through the~ordinary thumbnail pipeline.

\subsection{Public discovery and~selected-photo tools}
\index{robots.txt}
\index{sitemap.xml}
\index{image sitemap}
\index{picture manager}

The~public \codeword{/robots.txt} response describes crawler boundaries, while \codeword{/sitemap.xml} lists eligible public gallery and~image URLs with image metadata and~last-modified information. Unpublished, private, password-protected, age-restricted, or~otherwise unauthorized content is~not~made public through these outputs. Canonical URLs, social metadata, and~structured image data use the~same access-aware public path model.

The~Picture Manager is~a~logged-in administrator overlay on~the~public gallery view, not~a~visitor feature. An~administrator can~select photographs and~physical subgalleries, delete a~mixed selection, or~copy it~into a~new physical gallery below a~chosen parent. Photo-only selections can~also be~dragged onto a~visible child gallery, moved or~copied through the~searchable destination picker, shared through the~native device share sheet where supported, or~downloaded. Native sharing falls back to~the~selected-photo ZIP when the~browser cannot share the~prepared files. Smart Galleries are~excluded from~physical subtree operations. The~selection itself is~only browser state and~does not~grant permission. Every mutation/ZIP request rechecks the~normal logged-in account, CSRF token, source gallery, selected ownership, and~destination where applicable. Ordinary visitors do~not~receive these controls. Picture Manager is~intentionally independent from~the~Inline Administration capability: disabling Inline Administration removes direct public-page edit, add, delete, and~reorder affordances, while an~independently enabled Picture Manager can~still provide its~selection, deletion, move, copy, physical-gallery creation, and~selection-export workflows.

The~compact top toolbar shows the~selection count when visible. Open it~to~select
all items, clear the~selection, share or~delete, and~choose a~destination
for~moving or~copying photos. Expand \ui{New gallery from selection} only
when creating a~gallery; its~parent defaults to~the~current gallery.
The~plain \ui{?} control opens selection, drag, copy and~move guidance;
click outside or~press Escape to~dismiss it. Picker instructions and~the~gallery
directory link appear while the~picker has~focus. Progress, success and~errors
remain visible; the~idle ready message is~omitted.

With no~selection, the~manager is~hidden above the~page. Move the~pointer
to~the~top centre edge to~reveal and~open it. Selected photos or~physical
subgalleries keep it~visible. Keyboard focus also reveals it; on~touch devices,
select a~card first. Open help remains available until dismissed.

\section{Maintenance, updates, and~recovery}
\label{sec:maintenance}
\index{maintenance}
\index{updates}
\index{backup}

\subsection{Migrations}

Migration callbacks use explicit database connections and~independent migration
models. Replacing files does~not reload a~runtime model already included by~the
updater; \codeword{require\_once} retains its~old definitions. Initial content
evidence therefore uses a~separate bootstrap with fixed migration limits.
FULL tests execute the~real callback after an~old-model/file-swap scenario with
populated physical galleries, Smart Galleries, PDF and~music. They also cover
the~previous seed symbol, replay, optional readiness deferral and~SQL rollback.
Database owners run this vector on~disposable MySQL/MariaDB; source owners use
an~isolated SQLite adapter. Fresh-install tests remain separate coverage.

Schema changes live in~timestamped PHP files under \filepath{database/migrations/}. The~migration runner prevalidates pending definitions, applies them in~filename order, and~records successful versions. New schema work receives a~new migration file so~existing installations retain a~deterministic history.\footnote{Some migrations include repair callbacks in~addition to~SQL. Compatibility tests protect partial-update scenarios where an~older runner encounters newer migration definitions.}

Database-readiness checks are~remembered only for~the~current PHP request. This
normally reduces repeated metadata queries, but~a~migration can~change the~answer while the~same Admin, installer, updater, or~command-line process is~still running. The~migration runner therefore clears those remembered answers
after schema-changing statements and~after successful repair callbacks. A~post-migration check then reads the~new database catalog instead of~reusing an~answer from before the~migration. Ordinary data-only migration statements do~not~clear the~readiness cache because they cannot create or~remove a~table,
field, or~index.

Version~0.106 added the~\codeword{maintenance\_jobs} table through an~ordinary migration. Run it~from the~Gallery administration page as~usual if~it~has~not~already been applied. It~requires the~installation's normal table-creation authority, but~does~not~require database triggers, stored routines, \codeword{SUPER}, a~global server-variable change, or~separate database-administrator intervention. Until the~migration is~available and~verified, the~Maintenance Center refuses to~start a~job instead of~attempting maintenance without durable checkpoints. The~cooperative and~thumbnail identity migrations use the~same normal upgrade workflow.

\subsection{How the~gallery checks database readiness}
\index{database!readiness}
\index{schema inspection}
\index{migrations!missing objects}
\index{System Health!database inspection}
\index{troubleshooting!database connection}

The~shared readiness states and~their owner actions are~defined in~Section~\ref{sec:db-readiness-contract}. Maintenance uses the~same contract when checking tables, fields, and~indexes needed by~password reset, NSFW Guard, upload automation, thumbnail metadata, and~other schema-dependent features.

During an~upgrade, \ui{Missing} can~be~expected when new application files arrive before their migration has~run. \ui{Unknown} instead means PHP Gallery could not~inspect the~selected database reliably. For~\ui{Missing}, back up~and~apply the~pending migration. For~\ui{Unknown}, check database connectivity, the~selected schema, and~metadata-inspection permissions before changing schema.

After repair, reload \ui{System Health} and~retest the~affected feature. Keep any request reference with the~time and~application version when asking a~hosting provider or~maintainer for~help.

\subsection{Gallery trash and~recovery}
\index{gallery!trash}
\index{gallery!restoration}
\index{trash!automatic purge}

Trash uses compact translucent groups with visible preference switches
and~contextual question-mark help. With JavaScript, changes save immediately
together with retention and~batch values. During the~write, Trash controls lock;
confirmed state refreshes in~place and~returns focus to~the~edited field.
A~refused or~unverified result requires a~passive state reload without replaying
the~write. Without JavaScript, use the~normal Save form. Narrow screens retain
labelled metadata and~explicit actions without horizontal scrolling. Restore,
permanent deletion and~Empty Trash retain their separate confirmations.

Open \ui{Maintenance > Trash} to~review recoverable gallery deletions. Each active row shows the~original path, deletion time, gallery/photo counts, stored size, lifecycle status, and~automatic-purge deadline when that policy is~active. Problem entries remain visible for~inspection; PHP Gallery does~not~guess that an~ambiguous filesystem/database state is~safe to~destroy.

The~main Trash switch controls future gallery deletes and~is~enabled by~default. Turning it~off does~not~hide or~delete existing entries: restore and~manual permanent deletion remain available. Automatic purge is~a~separate opt-in and~is~off by~default. Its retention defaults to~30~days, its maintenance batch is~bounded, and~automatic deletion pauses whenever Trash is~disabled. Enabling automatic purge after a~pause gives current recoverable entries a~fresh full retention window before scheduled deletion can~begin.

Migrations \filepath{202609070001_gallery_trash_bin.php} and~\filepath{202609070002_gallery_trash_state_machine.php} add the~durable operation records. When Trash is~enabled, deletion checks this schema before the~first filesystem move or~database-row deletion. Confirmed missing or~unknown storage stops recoverable deletion and~directs the~administrator to~apply/check migrations; it~does not~silently fall through to~permanent deletion. Scheduled Site Maintenance reconciles stale in-progress operations conservatively and~runs expired auto-purge work only when both Trash and~automatic purge are~enabled.

Trash payloads live in~protected persistent \filepath{data/gallery-trash/} storage rather than~the~cache or~live gallery tree. Deployment packages carry the~directory's access-policy file but~exclude stored trash contents. Include that runtime directory and~the~database in~backup and~restore planning; neither half alone is~a~complete recoverable entry.

\subsection{Maintenance Center}
\index{Maintenance Center}
\index{maintenance!central workflow}

Open \ui{Maintenance > Maintenance Center} for~a~guided \ui{Analyze},
\ui{Review}, \ui{Execute}, and~\ui{Verify} workflow across routine
maintenance owners. Analyze is~read-only for~gallery, telemetry, log, Trash,
cache, thumbnail, and~database content; it~writes only the~durable checkpoint
needed to~resume the~inspection.

The~compact Review has~three tabs: Cleanup, Media, and~Database. Optional tasks
use shared switches with short labels; open the~adjacent question-mark details
for~an~explanation. Safety preflight and~final verification run automatically.
They are~not user choices and~do~not appear as~disabled switches. Deep
thumbnail verification remains available as~an~explicit opt-in, even when
analysis finds no~thumbnail work.

Expand technical activity when diagnosing a~run. Errors retain their useful
status and~identify the~safe source basename and~line when available, without
exposing a~private path or~stack trace. Undefined PHP function, class, or~constant
errors can~also show the~safe symbol name; raw exception text remains hidden.
Deep media verification is~routed to~the~thumbnail administration owner.

The~server loads the~reviewed subsystem dependencies again for~each separate analysis or~execution request before running its~task. Checking for~pending migrations only reads the~migration ledger; a~missing ledger or~failed inspection is~treated as~pending work. Apply migrations through the~explicit migration workflow; Analyze does~not~create or~repair migration storage.

The~reviewed plan is~bound to~the~current application version, applied migration revision, Maintenance Center registry revision, and~plan content. If~one of~those inputs changes, execution refuses the~stale plan and~asks for~a~new analysis. The~server rechecks task dependencies, capability state, and~database-table eligibility at~execution time; browser checkboxes are~presentation input rather than~authorization.

Execution advances through bounded checkpoints, so~you can~reload or~close the~page and~resume the~same job later. Only one running or~paused central job owns the~durable mutation claim, and~two browser tabs cannot advance one job concurrently. Automatic Site Maintenance yields before its~first mutation while that claim is~held. Public read-only gallery requests remain available.

Pause and~Cancel are~cooperative: they take effect between bounded operations and~do~not~undo work already committed. A~database \codeword{ANALYZE TABLE} or~\codeword{OPTIMIZE TABLE} already running must~return before cancellation can~continue. The~center processes at~most one currently eligible table per~browser step, skips protected, unknown, oversized, or~newly ineligible tables, and~records an~uncertain atomic outcome instead of~blindly replaying a~table after a~lost response.

The~final report separates removed rows and~files, analyzed or~optimized tables, skips, warnings, and~errors. It~reports physical before/after storage only when inventory evidence supports that conclusion.
