The~generic workflow never applies migrations, performs schema repair, installs updates, deletes Admin-log archives, empties all Trash entries, or~regenerates media; use the~specialist pages for~those deliberate operations.

Download/cache cleanup advances through the~manifest cache, legacy download-artifact cache, and~generated-ZIP cache as~separate bounded steps. These caches are~independent optimizations: if~one is~unavailable on~the~current host, the~center preserves cleanup already completed, records a~safe warning, and~continues with the~other owners and~later tasks. The~warning can~identify the~operation and~a~stable error category, but~does~not~expose a~raw exception message, SQL, credential, private path, or~stack trace. After an~update changes this execution contract, an~older ready plan is~intentionally stale; run Analyze again before continuing.

Cache lifecycle work admits at~most 32 short browser steps per~central run.
Completed regions are~not~forced to~restart while another region waits for~retry.
Deferred or~unavailable work keeps its~checkpoint; later tasks continue.
The~Admin log records the~reason, step count, coverage and~pending bytes under
\codeword{maintenance\_center.cache\_lifecycle\_deferred}.
Pause and~Cancel also work while a~request is~pending: the~control follows
the~current step, and~cancellation releases the~central claim through a~final
guarded step. A~service restart preserves the~job; after updating, reload
the~Maintenance Center to~continue or~cancel that existing run.

\subsection{Thumbnail maintenance}

Maintenance tools inspect generated variants, identify missing or~stale derivatives, and~rebuild selected sizes. Browser-assisted rebuilding can~prepare source chunks where configured. Background warm-up~handles small public-rendering gaps with limited requests, while explicit Admin maintenance remains the~primary large-scale repair path.

\subsection{SEO request guard and~public query hygiene}
\index{SEO request guard}
\index{query-string spam}
\index{canonical URL}

The~maintenance area includes an~optional public SEO request guard, enabled by~default. For~anonymous public GET requests it~keeps an~explicit allowlist of~query parameters for~each route. Known analytics parameters such as~UTM fields and~common ad-click identifiers are~ignored because they do~not~change rendered content. Unexpected parameters are~rejected before the~normal public renderer with~a~404, \codeword{noindex,nofollow}, and~no-cache response so~crawler-generated parameter spam does~not~multiply indexable gallery URLs.

Admin routes and~machine endpoints such as~setup, WebDAV, upload automation, gallery-migration receivers, and~the~maintenance cron endpoint are~outside this public query guard. Signed-in administrators are~also exempt so~diagnostic/admin workflows are~not~blocked by~public crawler rules. Rejected-query logging can~be~enabled independently and~is~sample-limited per~UTC day to~prevent a~crawler flood from~filling the~Admin log. The~same service supplies fallback canonical URLs when a~public page has~not~already emitted its own canonical tag.

\subsection{Database maintenance}

Database inspection inventories schema objects, references, ownership categories, retention policy, and~cleanup confidence. Logical cleanup uses allow-listed rules with deterministic selection, limited batches, and~audit records. Schema repair provides a~dry-run and~idempotent migration path. \codeword{ANALYZE TABLE} can~refresh optimizer statistics for~selected tables. Physical \codeword{OPTIMIZE TABLE} is~available only as~a~preview followed by~separately confirmed selected-table operation; it~is~never an~automatic cleanup step.\footnote{Storage-engine allocation values such as~data-free estimates describe engine state and~do~not~guarantee a~precise number of~filesystem bytes returned after optimization.}

\subsection{Storage statistics and~Complete report}

The~Files view also shows counts and~bytes for~PDF and~music originals,
plus an~overall media total. Update all reads PDF/music/OFP totals from
the~database after photo batches. The~first update imports missing accounting
from at~most two physical galleries per~step, including empty galleries. Hidden,
private and~disabled files and~tracks with downloads denied still consume
storage. Each central music asset counts once, including unassigned assets;
unmigrated local originals count separately. Smart playlists add no~copy.
Generated PDF pages already count as~photographs. Indexes, locks, conversion
state, unindexed files, Trash and~other caches are~outside these media totals.
Unreadable indexes or~unsafe/missing files produce partial-accounting warnings
without repair. Passive pages read cached results; attachment mutations mark
them stale when the~advisory revision write succeeds. After upgrading from
photo-only snapshots, apply the~accounting migration and run Update all once.
Attachment uploads, replacements and deletions maintain category totals from
committed metadata; repeat updates do~not reopen attachment files or~indexes.
External filesystem changes require explicit accounting invalidation before
Update all; see \codeword{docs/GALLERY\_PDF.md}.
With durable thumbnail metadata, ordinary photos are~accounted for~during
the~initial database pass without additional photo-batch requests. Only DNG
display masters retain bounded file checks. Installations without thumbnail
metadata retain the~full bounded filesystem fallback; a~failed required thumbnail
aggregate preserves the~last successful snapshot rather than reporting zero.
For performance diagnostics, export the~latest Admin log event
\codeword{storage.refresh\_all.completed}. Its~run ID links progress checkpoints;
the~summary includes wall/phase times, request counts, media totals, observed file
calls and~read bytes, inspection source counts and~bounded per-table ANALYZE times.
File-call counts are~application observations, not~physical disk I/O. Private paths
and~workflow capabilities are~excluded; a~logging failure does~not~fail the~update.
The final inspection records separate times for~schema and~migration reads,
source discovery and~reads, SQL-literal extraction, identifier indexing and~matching,
cleanup counts, legacy findings, report assembly, thumbnail distribution and~saving.
Read byte counts and~table/column comparison counts expose the~workload;
nested times overlap. Source references use a~fresh whole-word index per file
and~SQL literal, preserving production/test separation and~same-literal evidence.
Unusual identifiers retain regex matching. No~persistent analysis cache is~used.
Required photo-query errors and~prematurely empty derivative batches fail
the~file stage and~preserve the~last successful snapshot. Later stages continue
with an~overall partial result; a~query failure never means completed scanning.

\index{storage statistics}
\index{Complete report}
\index{gallery report}

Detailed \ui{Storage statistics} are~calculated only when an~administrator requests them, so~the~normal dashboard does~not~perform a~full derivative-filesystem scan on~every visit. The~Files view separates indexed source photographs from~generated JPEG/WebP thumbnails and~DNG browser-display masters, shows source file-type and~size distributions, largest galleries and~sources, unknown sizes, and~scan warnings. A~cached snapshot is~marked stale when gallery data changes. Refreshing the~snapshot scans expected generated files in~small browser-driven batches. The~same area links to~database storage details and~database maintenance.

The~Database tab reads information-schema storage statistics separately from~the~filesystem scan. It~distinguishes Gallery-owned tables from~the~whole selected database where possible and~shows the~largest tables rather than~forcing a~full table inspection into the~ordinary dashboard request. The~\ui{Update all} action refreshes the~file scan, updates database-table estimates through bounded \codeword{ANALYZE TABLE} requests, and~then runs the~read-only database inspection. Progress is~kept in~a~server-owned workflow, and~the~selected view refreshes in~place when~the~steps finish; repeating the~action during the~same administrator's active workflow resumes its~saved stage. Partial failures are~reported by~stage and~written to~the~Admin log. This~action does~not~remove data, repair the~schema, optimize tables, or~execute the~Maintenance Center plan.

\ui{Maintenance > Complete report} builds one self-contained HTML report covering storage, database structure and~size, gallery structure, image and~EXIF statistics, GPS overview, telemetry, operational logs, feature/settings state, and~runtime diagnostics. Image and~generated-media work is~processed in~bounded browser-driven batches so~the~report remains practical on~shared hosting. Transient timeout, rate-limit, and~server failures are~retried with~short increasing delays, while high-cardinality EXIF and~GPS summaries remain bounded to~control session-storage and~memory use on~large libraries. The~selected telemetry period affects only the~telemetry section; image statistics always cover the~complete image library. GPS place summaries use local approximate clustering and~offline reference data; probable simulator or~game screenshots are~excluded from~real-world place frequency where the~available evidence supports that classification.

The~completed report is~returned to~the~browser as~a~download and~is~not~saved as~a~report file on~the~server. To~keep a~PDF copy, open the~generated HTML and~use the~browser's Print or~Save as~PDF function; print styling is~included in~the~report. Treat any exported report as~administrative data because it~can~contain detailed operational and~collection statistics.

\subsection{Telemetry and~privacy controls}
\index{telemetry}
\index{privacy!telemetry}

The~compact workspace separates \ui{Overview}, \ui{Collection and~privacy},
\ui{History and~maintenance}, and~\ui{Reports}. Its~query URL preserves
the~selected tab across reload and~browser history. Each preference saves
independently after a~change; native forms retain explicit Save without
JavaScript. Question marks explain collection, privacy, limits and~defaults.
An~unverified save locks its~own form and~offers a~passive reload without
retrying the~write. Segmented HTML reports open in~a~new tab; Settings and~Logs
are~separate destinations. Rollup/purge remains an~explicit action, with
technical results in~a~disclosure.
Report links display the~standalone HTML document directly in~the~new tab,
which remains available for~reading.

In~Overview, click a~ranked photo name to~open its~public detail, or~a~gallery
name to~open that gallery. Existing access checks still apply. Six small daily
bar charts accompany the~totals. Each chart has~its~own scale; hovering shows
the~date and~exact value, and~\ui{Daily detail} provides the~same values
in~a~keyboard-accessible table. Missing or~expired days remain gaps. Retention
may leave totals without daily detail. Image bytes include photographs
and~thumbnails, excluding downloads. Unavailable public-path metadata leaves
ranking names readable without links.
The~small photo icon uses the~same preview as~the~gallery Images editor:
hover or~keyboard focus reveals it, clicking pins it, and~Escape closes it.
The~bounded thumbnail loads only when the~preview is~opened.

The~optional \ui{Telemetry} area records privacy-controlled operational measurements and~presents them as~Admin reports. Available controls cover the~master telemetry switch, public usage measurements, performance, cache and~database events, honoring Do~Not~Track, excluding administrators, the~maximum counted photo-view duration, and~retention for~raw, hourly, and~daily data. Reports include measures such as~anonymous sessions, page views, photograph opens and~capped view time, client errors, measured media bytes, browser mix, and~cache/database activity where those categories are~enabled.

The~telemetry subsystem is~designed not~to~store raw IP addresses, raw browser user-agent strings, raw referrer URLs, names, email addresses, account identifiers, request bodies, or~exact visitor locations. Exports contain aggregated anonymous telemetry and~session hashes rather than~raw visitor identifiers. Maintenance rolls up~and~purges telemetry according to~the~configured retention policy, and~the~Admin screen can~export a~standalone HTML report for~offline review. Review the~privacy controls before enabling collection on~a~public site.

SQL diagnostics have their own retention: seven days by~default, configurable
from one to~90 days. This does not~shorten hourly or~daily usage history.
Maintenance compacts historical daily summaries one day at~a~time while preserving
counts, durations, photo identity and~bot segments. Browser, OS, viewport, country
and~referrer details are~neutralized for~known daily metric families; browser mixes
continue to~come from sessions. Unknown metrics retain their dimensions.
The~database migration preserves measurements and~rebuilds aggregate indexes
around short physical keys; plan temporary disk space and~a~DDL maintenance
window. Deleted data pages become reusable; allocated database size may~require
manual physical optimization. Actual savings depend on~the~stored data.

Scheduled telemetry is~eligible every five minutes, with a~ten-second normal
slice and~up~to~eight batches of~5000 expired rows per~table (40000 rows).
Tables alternate by~batch and~the~first table rotates between slices. The~shared
clock may~reduce actual progress. Maintenance Center keeps its four-second UI
slice. Successful public visits to~home, gallery and~smart-gallery pages dispatch
a~private telemetry worker after rendering. Shared throttling admits work about
every five minutes when traffic exists, independently of~thumbnail windows.
The~visitor never waits for~retention SQL and~receives no~maintenance token.
External cron is~optional; without incoming requests, work waits for~a~later visit.
Preview, media and~failed responses cannot trigger work. Telemetry OFF and~central
maintenance ownership remain respected; retention ages are~unchanged.

\subsection{Runtime diagnostics, integrity, and~gallery benchmark}
\index{Runtime diagnostics}
\index{integrity checking}
\index{gallery benchmark}

\ui{Runtime diagnostics} provides a~copyable environment and~support view for~PHP, database-readiness classes, hosting limits, GD, Imagick/ImageMagick, and~relevant media-format support. It~also exposes the~DNG conversion policy described earlier. The~readiness panels separate presentation/reporting dependencies, authentication/security dependencies, and~mutation-sensitive dependencies so~an~administrator can~distinguish an~optional missing capability from~a~condition that must~block a~write.

The~same bounded model reports gallery edit-revision readiness and~unresolved image moves. A~missing or~unknown required schema is~an~action state and~stops the~affected mutation before target files or~rows change. Pending-move summaries contain only capped operation/gallery identities and~safe state categories; use the~documented command-line recovery workflow for~an~explicit operation rather than~expecting the~diagnostics page to~move files.

The~separate \ui{Development diagnostics} switch is~administrator-only. When enabled for~a~signed-in administrator it~adds viewer instrumentation for~preload, cache, memory, network, and~frame-timing work in~the~public/lightbox/fullscreen experience. It~does not~turn those diagnostics on~for~ordinary anonymous visitors. Keep it~off during normal ownership unless you~are~investigating rendering or~performance behavior; the~gallery benchmark also depends on~this development-diagnostics context.

The~lightbox DEV dashboard opens as~a~compact overview. Use Freeze or~Resume to~hold or~continue observations, Collapse to~keep the~overview, and~Expand to~open separate pages for~media, preloading, graphs, viewport, and~lifecycle details. Pages fit the~available viewport without scrollbars, preserve your selected page during updates, and~remain within the~viewer in~native fullscreen. The~media page distinguishes the~displayed photograph from~a~pending navigation target and~shows loaded dimensions, displayed quality, and~requested quality. Source labels omit credentials, query parameters, and~fragments.

The~three graphs have separate labeled scales: retained decoded-cache pixel estimates, cache entries and~active detached loads, and~animation-frame intervals with a~16.7~ms reference. Up~to~90 samples at~350~ms intervals cover about 31 seconds. The~pixel estimate excludes the~live image, GPU allocations, and~browser heap; frame intervals include scheduling and~background throttling rather than measuring render cost alone. A~past successful decode does~not~prove that the~viewer still retains that image. Unsupported browser measurements show an~unavailable marker. Diagnostics observe existing work without requesting extra media or~metadata; closing stops monitoring and~teardown removes the~panel. When DEV is~off, the~dashboard module is~not~loaded.

The~Admin \ui{Integrity} workflow checks integrity-managed core files against the~installed manifest and~helps identify incomplete or~unexpected deployment changes. It~is~a~runtime verification tool; coordinated backups are~still required because an~integrity manifest does~not~contain user photographs or~replace recovery media.

When development diagnostics are~available, a~signed-in administrator can~start a~\ui{Public gallery load benchmark} for~the~current gallery. The~browser loads the~gallery several times in~a~hidden same-origin frame using an~anonymous preview, combines PHP render counters with browser navigation timing, and~then offers the~benchmark log as~JSON. Use the~same gallery, presentation mode, and~network/cache conditions when comparing two changes; the~benchmark is~most useful as~a~before/after measurement rather than~as~a~universal score.

\subsection{Scheduled site maintenance}
\index{scheduled maintenance}
\index{cron!site maintenance}

The~Media and~cache tab uses the~same compact translucent groups as~Features
and~Content and~display. Complete groups flow down one to~three columns, with
independent heights. Question marks open contextual help; scan status and~the
reason for~an~unavailable action remain visible. Schedule preferences save
immediately on~change, together with their time and~numeric values. Pending
writes lock only their form. An~unverified result retains the~intent and~offers
a~reload without replaying the~write. Without JavaScript, use the~normal Save
form. Thumbnail generation, cleanup, reset and~token rotation remain explicit
tasks with their existing safeguards.

In~Admin > Maintenance > Media and~cache, use~\emph{Clean updater cache now}
to~run one bounded step in~place. The~step removes only already staged updater
trash in~\filepath{cache/updates/prune-trash}; it~preserves photos, thumbnails,
downloads, rollback backups and~unknown cache data. Force bypasses the~timer,
but~respects active updates and~maintenance locks. If~debt remains, use~
\emph{Continue cleanup}. The~result reports actual files and~bytes removed.
For~unattended progress, run~\filepath{scripts/site_maintenance.php} with
\texttt{--cache-only} every minute from~hosting cron. This cleanup is~independent
of~thumbnail scheduling and~does~not~establish an~overall cache quota.
Compatibility settings and~schedule diagnostics remain available in~the~native
disclosures; Maintenance Center retains ownership of~download-cache cleanup.

\paragraph{Measured cache retention.}
Automatic work rotates persistent database regions. Last service and~the~last successfully completed check are~separate. Thirty-second opportunities serve the~oldest due work for~at~most five seconds; partial areas resume their saved cursor and~yield to~other due areas. Healthy completed areas are~due after one hour; errors wait fifteen minutes. Root discovery finds new directories without creating one database row per~file. Drained absent identities are~retained seven days and~pruned in~small batches.

The main cache card shows recorded size, confirmed total removal and~the~verified queue. The~question-mark help contains scope, coverage and~UTC times; technical details remain expandable. A~small waiting bar counts down to~the~scheduled check, never scan completion. The~local timer redraws four times per~second; a~passive AJAX read refreshes visible evidence every fifteen seconds without starting work. Historical measurements, current manual-cycle counters and~lifetime receipts remain separate.

Search, cleanup and~refresh use labeled outline icons. Manual scan activity stays visible above expandable details; its spinner uses CSS and~respects reduced motion. Help has short labeled sections, and~operational dates use structured UTC fields. Each thin owner bar shows measured eligible bytes as~a~share of~recorded size, never scan completion or~the~live queue. Maintenance subtab URLs survive reload and~browser history; the~anchor is~aligned after deferred content loads.

Historical flat download ZIPs expire after seven days from payload modification; changing permissions or~reading them does not renew expiry. Native abandoned temporary ZIP outputs retain six-hour inactivity. Only recognized paths with positive ZIP proof and~free producer/serving leases are~reclaimed. Historical update ZIPs require a~verified Gallery source snapshot: the~installed application stays in~place, and~two previous recovery copies are~retained, counting the~latest job rollback and~selected backup first. Active or~unproven recovery remains protected. Unfinished regions may reuse spare admission within the~existing five-second burst; dense catalogs stream names with bounded memory. After ninety seconds without progress, a~foreground-contended autonomous worker gets only one forty-millisecond slice. Explicit OFF and~all ownership guards remain effective.

The~regional sample shows the~area, state, last successful completed check and~next check or~retry in~compact rows. Technical details retain the~relative path, observation round, last service, both planned dates, failures and~saved cursor. Dates explicitly use UTC; missing completion remains unrecorded. The~cursor is~a~position, never a~percentage. The~sample does~not~cover every region. AJAX updates preserve the~same localized presentation, open details, panel and~URL; reading starts no~work.

The~compact overview labels last-known measurements and~their age, completed coverage, current work, pending eligible debt and~the~last confirmed deletion separately. A~new partial check keeps the~completed measurement visible; a~smaller completed check can~replace it. Historical bytes are~not~current size and~deletion is~not~subtracted from them. Reading status never scans storage. DB checkpoints own cursors; private disk journals retain names and~detached payloads. Unfinished legacy work is~drained safely before conversion. Invalid checkpoints remain unverified. OFF preserves the~registry and~progress; the~existing authenticated server continuation needs no open browser. A~killed chain still needs a~later restart opportunity.

Registered disposable browser profiles and versioned release working copies have
a fixed 24-hour lifetime. Use does not renew expiry. New browser profiles record
creation; older workspaces use a filesystem-derived age anchor saved once.
Pins and unsafe or busy ownership can defer physical removal. Persistent release
preparation/qualification storage remains protected.

The same Media and cache page adds \emph{Scan cache} and an explicit
\emph{Clean eligible cache} approval for the exact preview. Inventory is
non-destructive; continuation stays on the page. The compact table shows observed
and eligible bytes by owner, candidate examples, partial scans and actual
reclamation. Completed test audits and verified downloaded CI diagnostics expire
after 24 hours; generated managed ZIPs remain available for seven days. New audit
ownership markers also permit expired interrupted reports once their producer
lease is free. Updater retention has no time limit: current installed files and
the latest successful rollback retain current/previous versions; active jobs,
selected beta recovery and rollback sources remain protected. Older verified
completed/cancelled work is reclaimable; failed jobs and unversioned legacy
backups require recovery review. Unknown content, originals, resume state and
\texttt{.keep} pins are preserved. GitHub response metadata expires after one day,
and SimBrief drafts after their existing 30-minute expiry plus a one-minute grace.
Hosting cron with \texttt{--cache-only} also advances this independent lifecycle;
it never auto-approves a manual preview. Dense, slow or unreadable directories
are explicitly partial and preserved. Expiry establishes eligibility, not instant
deletion. The existing managed-download capacity limit remains; there is no global
cache quota. Inventory controls are excluded from measured payload totals.

Shared hosts such as~WEDOS may disable \codeword{disk_free_space()}. Automatic cleanup continues with explicitly unknown free capacity, successful scheduler and~candidate checkpoints, and~the~same bounded journals. Known capacity below 16~MiB still blocks admission; actual checkpoint write failures stop work. Unknown capacity is~neither a~measured reserve nor~an~account quota. No~manual Scan action is~required.

Automatic cache cleanup is~enabled on~installation and~upgrade through the~canonical Admin Features preference; explicit OFF survives updates. One ordinary GET visit can start an~authenticated server chain that continues without an~open browser. New inventory is~due every 30~seconds and~independent workers admit up~to~5~seconds of~checkpointed inventory and~immediate reclamation of~verified eligible data. Unfinished cursors, originals, pins and~active/recovery data remain protected. Waiting occupies a~PHP worker; configured-origin HTTP access and~sufficient runtime are~required. A~terminated process or~failed handoff can interrupt the~chain; a~later suitable visit or~optional independent scheduler can recover it. Actual removals are~coalesced in~the~Admin log at~most once per~five minutes, without individual filenames or~empty checks.

A~measured eligible backlog of~at~most 128~MiB receives an~attempt of~up~to~600~ms and~eight short slices where response finishing is~supported. Larger backlogs receive 120~ms and~two slices. Without that support, admission is~40~ms and~two slices; the~client may~wait for~this short shutdown work. These are~admission deadlines, not~OS-call latency guarantees. PHP retains its worker even after response finishing. A~shared 60-second throttle, nonblocking worker locks, foreground leases and~arrival signals make cleanup yield to~other visits. Each inner slice admits at~most 60~ms, inventory at~most 5000 observations and~a~4096-name catalog, deletion 180 operations, with eight MiB incremental memory admission and~eight MiB PHP memory headroom. Many tiny files therefore continue across visits even below 128~MiB. Known committed eligible candidates can~be~reclaimed before the~whole scan finishes; scan and~deletion alternate fairly.

An~unapproved manual preview idle for~fifteen minutes receives a~new scheduled identity; its old ID is~refused, never automatically approved. Approved deletion cursors survive interruption. Attempts, actual progress, stale observations, next due, current pending bytes and~successfully removed bytes remain separate from~historical measurements; HTTP 200 alone proves no~progress. Optional CLI or~protected HTTPS cron can~supply opportunities on~an~idle installation, with at~most ten seconds and~32 slices per~invocation. No~hosting setup is~needed for~visitor-driven operation. See~\filepath{docs/CACHE_MAINTENANCE.md}. Fixed workspace lifetime remains 24~hours, ZIP retention seven days and~updater recovery contextual without a~TTL.

Manual runs immediately show a~diagnostic ID and~a~trace linking to~filtered
Admin Logs. Events record locks, checkpoints, observed entries, removed bytes
and~safe failure codes. Busy results identify the~owner; force respects it.
Failed log writes are~visible in~diagnostics, with best-effort fallback
to~PHP's error log. Hard process termination can~leave an~incomplete trace.

Use~\emph{Copy log} inside the~disclosure to~copy its current JSON trace.
A~thin progressbar uses known updater phase counters; unknown totals remain
indeterminate. \emph{Stop update} uses safe pre-activation cancellation and~never
forces an~activation-stage stop. Busy refusal remains visible. Read-only progress
refreshes while the~panel is~visible and~does~not~advance the~update.
Failed phases show the~safe updater error/reference, also included in~the~copied
trace. A~phase counter at~100\% does~not~mean successful validation.
Copied checkpoints also include age, worker-lock observation, counter movement
and~available retry history. A~stored running status alone does~not~prove live
work; an~unlocked worker is~only an~instantaneous observation.

Routine maintenance can~run in~bounded slices instead of~one long request. The~maintenance service chains safe web requests when traffic is~available and~can~also be~called from~hosting cron with the~CLI command shown in~Admin. It~can~check thumbnail state, refresh metadata, process cleanup tasks, roll up~telemetry, and~archive eligible Admin-log days according to~their configured policies. The~hidden web-cron token is~rotatable; rotating it~invalidates previously published web-cron URLs. Maintenance is~non-destructive by~default and~does~not~replace explicit confirmation for~database optimization or~other consequential actions.

\subsection{Cron Overview and~visitor scheduling}
Open \ui{Maintenance > Cron Overview} for~a~dense, sortable table of~automatic
updates, log archival, site/cache maintenance, updater-trash cleanup,
telemetry retention and~one-shot attachment-count reconciliation. Each owner
supplies its effective cadence, next eligible threshold and~actual last-attempt
evidence. Unknown evidence remains unknown; admission is~not~completion.
Ordinary visitor and~crawler PHP page requests provide scheduling opportunities.
The~shared scheduler admits at~most one bounded background owner per~tick,
retaining owner locks, enablement, UTC windows and~the~Maintenance Center claim.
Scheduled gallery publication still happens before the~triggering page renders.

Sort the~column headings; the~visible page refreshes existing cells about once
per~second without executing jobs. Observation pauses while hidden and~backs off
on~errors. Play submits one protected task action in~place, overriding only
its time gate; native POST remains available without JavaScript. A~task can~be
busy, continued or~deferred rather than complete. Next eligible is~a~threshold,
not~an~exact execution promise: without visitors, work waits for~the~next request.
Play is~directly before the~task name. The~pause icon beside the~heading freezes
only this live preview; resume reads immediately and~background tasks continue.
Future thresholds retain their absolute time with~the~remaining duration
in~parentheses.
Task names link to~their existing settings or~operational controls. No next
deadline is~labelled Disabled, Completed or~Nothing pending cleanup where
appropriate; Unknown is~reserved for~unavailable evidence.

The~collapsed \ui{Optional hosting cron} section creates or~regenerates a~copyable
protected URL and~can~revoke it. The~URL respects installation subdirectories
and~query routing without rewriting. Its example uses \codeword{curl -fsS} every
one to~five minutes, optionally. It~calls the~same due-only scheduler and~never
disables visitor scheduling. Treat the~URL as~a~secret. No hosting cron, browser
job timer, resident process or~external queue is~required.
The~256-bit token and~shared locks are~supplemented by~one accepted hosting call
per~minute. Repeated calls cannot force work past its owner gates. Network/ICMP
flood protection remains the~hosting provider's responsibility.

\subsection{Admin log history and~archives}
\index{admin logs}
\index{log archive}

The~compact log table keeps event names, messages, severity, timestamps and~instance counts visible. Open the~event name for~IDs, request/route metadata, the~first and~latest occurrence, and~individual group members; the~code icon beside a~message reveals its structured context. Copy and~download icons have descriptive tooltips and~accessible names. Row actions export one event or~the~entire group indicated by~that row; inside group details you can~also copy, save or~select individual members. Every export includes the~full diagnostic text and~context.

Search includes event names, messages, JSON context and~request/route metadata. A~row marked \ui{Match in~details} matches inside its collapsed context. Live filtering commits only the~newest request, keeping rows, counts and~the~URL tied to~the~same result even during rapid typing. For~automatic cache failures, \codeword{cache.automatic\_failed} records the~entry point, failure stage, exception class, source filename/line and~validated SQLSTATE/driver codes when available. It~omits raw messages, SQL, private paths and~credentials. Repeated failures are~coalesced for~five minutes; changed diagnostics retain a~thirty-second minimum. Logging failure preserves the~worker checkpoint and~safe refusal. Complete pending migrations when schema diagnostics require them; observe automatic progress through ordinary visits and~passive status without starting a~manual scan.

Use the~checkboxes and~the~header checkbox to~select entries on~the~current page. In~Admin bulk lists with item checkboxes, click one row and~Shift-click another in~the~same list to~apply the~second checkbox's resulting state to~enabled visible rows between them on~the~current results page. The~range anchor resets when live filtering, pagination, or~another row replacement changes the~results. Severity filters continue to~control which log rows are~visible; \ui{Selected}, \ui{This page} and~\ui{All filtered} retain their existing log export scopes. The~toolbar offers those scopes for~both copying and~saving a~UTF-8 TXT file. Selecting a~group includes all its instances, including those not~yet loaded; overlapping individual selections appear only once. \ui{All filtered} exports matching raw events across all pages in~the~chosen time order. Selection clears when filters or~pagination replace the~rows. PHP reads large exports in~batches; copying still needs the~complete chosen text in~browser memory, so~prefer saving for~very large histories. If~clipboard access is~unavailable, a~dialog selects the~complete text for~manual copying. TXT downloads also work without JavaScript; the~existing full CSV/JSON ZIP export remains available.

Everyday administrative work creates a~useful history: uploads, gallery changes, security decisions, maintenance results, warnings, and~other operational events can~leave a~record in~the~Admin log. That history is~valuable when you want to~understand what happened, when it~happened, and~which account or~request was~involved. It~is~also normal for~the~table to~become large on~an~active installation. Version~\version{} therefore treats log care as~its own explicit maintenance task rather than allowing generic database cleanup to~remove audit history unexpectedly.

The~Admin log screen can~show individual entries or~collapse repeated events into a~summary. A~group may~represent repeated thumbnail warnings, upload results, or~maintenance messages; opening it~shows the~individual records. Filters and~workflow status controls help narrow operational review, while bounded exports preserve selected history without loading the~entire table in~one request. Large lists, raw-instance expansion, and~exports are~processed in~limited portions, so~requesting a~complete history does not~require the~browser or~PHP to~hold the~entire table in~memory at~once.

The~owner can~choose how long recent logs should remain in~the~live database. Choosing \emph{Forever} disables automatic archival. Choosing a~finite live period does not~immediately throw older records away. The~maintenance workflow first selects complete calendar days that are~outside the~live retention window, writes a~protected archive containing both machine-readable JSON and~a~readable static HTML report, checks that the~archive contains the~expected number of~records, and~only then removes those same rows from the~live table. The~archive is~stored under \filepath{data/admin-log-archives/}, which is~application data rather than a~public gallery area and~carries an~additional web-server protection rule.

Archive maintenance is~resumable. It~writes temporary files before promoting a~completed archive, records a~small state file, and~uses a~lock so~two maintenance requests do~not~process the~same history simultaneously. If~hosting limits, a~connection interruption, or~another problem stops the~work, the~Admin screen can~show the~incomplete state and~the~next maintenance attempt can~verify, continue, or~recover it. An~archive is~never considered a~reason to~delete live records merely because a~file with a~similar name exists; the~row-count and~archive metadata checks are~part of~the~safety boundary.

Administrators should still include the~database and~the~\filepath{data/admin-log-archives/} directory in~coordinated backups. The~live database contains recent operational history, while the~archive directory contains older history selected by~the~explicit retention policy. Keeping both gives the~owner a~continuous record and~makes a~restored installation easier to~investigate after an~update, migration, or~unexpected event.
