\end{longtable}

\subsection{Application updates}

Automatic stable checks use the~saved 5/15/30/60-minute interval, defaulting
to~five minutes. The~first admitted gallery visit after the~interval, including
an~anonymous visitor or~allowed robot, can~start the~update. After a~successful
response, independent authenticated server requests continue three-second
worker slices until completion. Login, JavaScript, an~open Updates panel
and~hosting cron are~unnecessary. Blocked SEO requests do~not~start this work.
The~configured installation URL must be~reachable from~the~server with valid
TLS. Lost handoffs expire after 120 seconds; later visits recover them.
GitHub backoff and~worker failures may~delay progress. Five minutes controls
discovery eligibility, not~total installation time; a~future release still
needs an~eligible visit. See~\filepath{docs/AUTOUPDATE.md}.

The~compact release summary keeps installed and~available versions, channel, status, and~the~main actions together; repository and~GitHub API details remain in~a~collapsed diagnostics section. When a~job completes, the~browser refreshes the~summary from~local metadata in~place, keeps the~panel open and~the~URL unchanged, and~hides an~obsolete stable-update action. This refresh does~not~perform another GitHub check. If~it~fails, use the~status-refresh button to~retry the~local read without repeating the~installation.

On~Windows, an~updater checkpoint commit allows up~to~ten atomic replacement attempts, with~50~ms pauses and~a~450~ms total pause limit, to~handle brief file-sharing locks. If~the~commit still fails, the~previous checkpoint is~preserved. A~persistent permission problem requires fixing storage access before retrying the~job. Windows uses the~same bounded retry policy when migrating orientation metadata in~\filepath{gallery.json}; a~concurrent edit or~persistent refusal preserves the~existing document and~stops the~migration.

The~updater checks configured channels, validates packages, stages content, verifies required runtime files and~sizes, preserves overwritten files in~recovery storage, and~activates the~update. Rate-limit handling and~retry state prevent repeated remote requests during provider wait periods. A~resumable job card appears in~both \ui{Status} and~\ui{Advanced tools}, so~a~beta install, restore, or~reinstall continues to~show its progress where it~was~started. The~page may~be~closed and~reopened without discarding completed checkpoints.

The~Admin update area also shows cached release information and~versioned patch notes for~the~available channel, so~an~owner can~review a~release before starting its~job. Stable and~beta channels remain separate choices; the~Beta label belongs to~the~release choice in~the~Admin interface and~does~not~change the~updater's durable job or~manifest terminology.

Patch notes are~stored as~raw Markdown in~the~installed \filepath{PATCH_NOTES.md} and~the~versioned cache. The~Admin viewer renders the~selected version when it~is~displayed, including entries loaded from~older caches; previously cached HTML is~discarded rather than~trusted. Reading the~viewer uses locally bundled or~cached notes without making another GitHub request. A~separate, bounded release-discovery operation may~refresh remote notes; when that fails, installed notes remain available.

Markdown links such as~\codeword{[issue](https://example.org/issue/123)}, angle-bracket autolinks, and~plain HTTP(S) addresses become clickable. They open in~a~new tab with~\codeword{noopener} and~\codeword{noreferrer} protection. Other URL schemes remain escaped, inert text. The~renderer preserves headings, lists, emphasis, fenced blocks, and~literal inline code; it~does not~turn URLs inside code spans into links. Link punctuation, underscores, and~query parameters are~retained without accepting arbitrary HTML.


Temporary download or~hosting failures may~be~retried. A~package whose files do~not~match its own integrity manifest is~different: downloading the~same published build again cannot change those bytes. PHP Gallery therefore asks the~administrator to~cancel that prepared job and~choose a~newer release or~beta code instead of~offering an~endless retry loop.

Stable discovery and~package work are~separate bounded operations. A~discovery request may~create a~durable background job, while a~later safe request or~the~CLI runner advances a~short slice through download, archive validation, extraction, manifest verification, staging, rollback backup, activation, migrations, and~cleanup. Range requests can~resume interrupted downloads, worker locks prevent concurrent advancement, and~a~stale lock can~be~recovered after its~owner expires. On~idle shared hosting, schedule \codeword{php scripts/application_update.php} from~cron, for~example every five minutes. Each invocation performs bounded discovery or~advances existing background work; correctness does~not~depend on~\codeword{set_time_limit()} or~\codeword{ignore_user_abort()}.

Current releases also reconcile the~small set of~application-owned server-policy files during update preparation and~activation. This protects installations whose older updater skipped a~policy file: the~validated release copy is~compared and~published atomically, rollback metadata is~extended before replacement, and~a~migration-backed bootstrap path can~repair an~already activated older tree. The~reconciliation is~limited to~known policy paths and~does~not~alter gallery media or~user configuration.

Before activation, an~administrator can~cancel or~retry a~recoverable job. After activation, the~Admin recovery action can~restore managed application files from~the~saved rollback snapshot, but~file rollback does~not~reverse database migrations. Stable restore, clean reinstall, and~beta installation remain resumable jobs with their own package validation. The~standalone \filepath{reset.php} emergency path is~a~separate recovery tool for~returning to~the~stable branch when the~normal Admin route cannot be~used; it~still requires the~same careful backup and~integrity review.

During the~short activation critical section, a~dependency-free early runtime gate prevents new ordinary requests from~loading a~partially replaced application. Such requests receive a~private, non-cacheable \codeword{503 Service Unavailable} response until activation completion is~durably recorded. The~authenticated update recovery/status path remains available, so~an~interrupted activation can~continue. Corrupt or~incomplete gate state fails closed instead of~presenting the~installation as~healthy.

The~same early runtime boundary converts uncaught and~catchable fatal PHP failures into bounded \codeword{500} responses when PHP can~still control the~response. Production hosting must keep \codeword{display\_errors} disabled; otherwise the~PHP runtime itself may~print warning or~fatal details before application-level handling can~format a~safe response.

\important{Run a~coordinated backup before an~application update. Keep the~backup available until runtime checks, migrations, public galleries, protected access, uploads, and~Admin operations have been verified.}

Before activation, updater archive validation also requires the~complete new request runtime and~its reviewed module plan. A~partial archive is~refused while prepared files remain outside the~active installation. Keep all shipped runtime files together during manual deployment; replacing only the~bootstrap would leave an~incomplete application.

Updater activation uses the~reviewed updater subset of~the~production inventory. Incoming inventories list WinApp sources separately so~older installed updaters can~validate the~release before installing its reader. The~CMS updater does~not~install those sources. Older source archives without that inventory retain a~bounded path based on~their owned manifest; a~malformed present inventory is~refused. Obsolete cleanup requires prior ownership evidence and~preserves unknown local files. Rollback uses the~server-written snapshot index and~refuses unindexed files or~paths redirected by~symbolic links or~Windows junctions. These file safeguards do~not~reverse database migrations.

Failed jobs from automatic, Admin/API and~Advanced commit installs share
three transient retries after 60, 120 and~240 seconds. Undefined functions
and~deterministic integrity failures do~not~repeat the~same defective package.
New jobs pin an~immutable trusted GitHub commit. The~publisher integrity sweep
is~retained but~disabled until explicitly enabled; the~short stage is~labelled
\ui{Preparing package}. Staging and~activation check actual copy bytes.
Non-core assets and~rollback snapshots additionally preserve
exact bytes through raw SHA-256 identities. At~least 600 seconds after the~first
failure, an~untouched
pre-activation job releases its active slot. A~safe post-activation failure
creates one separate rollback job restoring the~actual previous managed files
and~channel settings, including deleted files and~removal of~newly created paths.
Both identities, archives, backups and~diagnostics remain preserved.
A~failed rollback cannot create another rollback.

The~sole public maintenance switch is~\codeword{maintenance.lock}
in~the~installation root. An~empty manually created file also enables maintenance;
removing it~ends manual maintenance. Active jobs and~rollback records alone
do~not~close the~website. Inspect updater-owned markers before removal after
an~interrupted write. Legacy activation markers are~imported before admission,
with their exact bytes retained privately. The~marker stays outside deployment
and~rollback payloads. Runtime commit ends the~isolated automatic rollback
window. Application files continue to~be~updated in~place.

File restoration requires verified backup bytes, matching actual schema/ledger
and~proof that no~migration effects occurred. Shared runtime leases drain before
activation; supported targets keep the~gate through migrations and~required
finalization. They then commit the~usable runtime and~reopen before physical
cleanup, which may~pause or~retry while the~website remains available.
Ordinary use during the~observation window invalidates rollback safety even
with unchanged schema. Entered/unverified migrations, unknown DB state, missing
proof or~unsupported older packages stay failed with repair instructions.
Database migrations and~application data are~never reversed. Automatic discovery
OFF and~beta still prevent new automatic installation; capability OFF stops
installer work. After safe restoration, discovery can~skip known failed versions
and~install a~newer stable release. Gated failures need hosting CLI cron
or~an~authenticated Admin request; denied visitors cannot run the~scheduler.
No~idle worker waits through backoff.

Applied migration rows remain history when their files were intentionally
retired from the~installed manifest. Database evidence format~2 retains
the~complete ledger with timestamps and~actual schema, recording that absence
explicitly. Missing declared files and~changed bytes still refuse activation;
old saved witnesses retain their original strict comparisons.
An~old \codeword{activate} checkpoint alone does~not~prove partial activation:
pointer release requires unchanged originals, verified backups and~absent
created paths under start, worker and~exclusive runtime locks. Admin requests
release their own bootstrap read lease only after authentication and~CSRF.
Integrity progress shows the~verified-file cursor; entering each new stage
resets the~previous counters. The~compact progress surfaces remain synchronized
on~desktop and~narrow screens.
The~normal action remains Update followed by~automatic continuation.
Details call the~lifetime counter Processing runs: it~includes short worker
requests and~waits, and~Retry keeps the~same job and~count. Waiting for~other
requests is~explicit and~uses slower polling. Unmanaged local PHP files remain
preserved. Private worker diagnostics include stage/reference, exception owner
and~line, safety receipts and~the~validated migration identity when relevant;
unavailable Admin logging falls back to~the~PHP error log without blocking work.

An~older stuck updater does~not~receive this mechanism merely because a~new
release is~published. In~maintenance mode, stop workers, preserve coordinated
backups and~all failed-job evidence, establish actual database compatibility,
deploy the~complete verified compatible fixed release and~run forward migrations.
Only after verified repair may~the~administrator archive the~matching old active
pointer. Never replace only the~bootstrap, edit a~job's target/package, discard
a~live activation gate or~restore application files alone. See
\filepath{docs/AUTOUPDATE.md} for~the~complete repair procedure and~limits.
That procedure includes the~reported \codeword{c2e2bc77} installation and
its pinned older job: after separate approval, deploy the~complete compatible
fixed release in~maintenance mode, preserve the~old job and~reconcile its
owner only after verified forward repair. Retry never changes a~job's target.
For~overwrite-only FTP deployment, upload the~complete release without deleting
old local files, jobs or~backups. Choose \ui{Reset updater / finish FTP update}
on~the~job card or~in~Advanced tools. It~stops previous jobs at~a~safe boundary,
preserves their records and~gate receipts, then starts fresh forward completion.
\ui{Install stable} is~always available and~downloads the~latest stable branch
head, overwriting every packaged file even at~the~same version. It~also replaces
stuck or~paused jobs without requiring FTP. Both actions preserve configuration,
media, unlisted local files and~backups. File-content differences are~warnings
with the~relative file and~expected/actual digests in~the~log when the~optional
verifier is~enabled. Its~sweeps are~currently disabled for~all installation jobs.
Safe archive paths, required files and~runtime
ownership remain enforced. With the~optional verifier enabled, a~missing,
malformed or~stale manifest also becomes
a~warning and~cannot stop either recovery action. Reset closes visitor admission
before existing requests drain, so~ongoing traffic cannot prevent its~start.
Actual activation and~migrations wait for~those requests to~finish.
Ordinary automatic updates also close admission before waiting for~existing
readers, so~new visitors cannot indefinitely delay activation.
While the~gate is~closed, private workers retain a~paced successor when waiting
for~old readers; another visitor is~not~required. A~one-second handoff delay
runs after locks are~released. The~configured Admin session name survives
post-response session close, keeping authenticated recovery reachable.
The~start log names the~target version; a~separate completion event records
the~previous and~installed versions only after durable completion.
During activation, visitors see a~compact English maintenance page instead
of~a~plain server message. It~refreshes the~current page every three seconds,
even without JavaScript, and~offers \ui{Try again}.
The~self-contained light/dark layout needs no~database or~application assets.
HTTP 503 remains in~effect; POST and~machine replies never refresh automatically.
Busy takeover is~shown explicitly and~continues
automatically. FTP retry rechecks installed files instead of~downloading.
Recovery runs forward migrations; database changes are~never reversed.
\ui{Pause} works at~the~next safe checkpoint in~every stage;
\ui{Continue update} resumes the~same job. A~partial activation stays gated.
Advanced commit installation also accepts older supported release commits;
database migrations are~never reversed and~a~compatible DB backup may~be~needed.

\subsection{Integrity manifest}
\index{core manifest}

\filepath{app/core-manifest.json} records the~application version and~hashes for~integrity-managed core files. The~local generator under \filepath{scripts/generate_manifest.php} calculates final hashes after runtime changes. Documentation and~user-data inclusion follow the~generator's established policy.

During deterministic release preparation, the~manifest generator accepts the~optional \codeword{SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH} build timestamp for~its \codeword{generated_at} field; without it, normal generation uses the~current clock. The~hosted release workflow derives the~epoch from~the~release metadata and~uses it~for~both the~manual PDFs and~the~final manifest, so~identical prepared inputs retain reproducible timestamps. The~manifest still has~to~be~regenerated after changing an~integrity-managed application file.

\section{Optional background: how PHP Gallery works}
\label{sec:architecture}
\index{architecture}
\index{controllers}
\index{services}
\index{views}

The~following pages describe the~request path used by~the~application. They are~mainly relevant to~maintenance and~development; ordinary gallery administration does not~require them.

The~generated module plan declares shared dependencies and~one canonical file order, preserving the~complete route closure and~include order. Keep dependency inputs and~the~generated plan together when changing routes. Request/session adapters own transport access; language, navigation tokens, gallery grants, NSFW acknowledgement, and~flash behavior retain their established owners. The~lightbox separately owns navigation generations and~decoded resources, while the~viewer keeps presentation and~authorized quality selection. This separation provides regression boundaries; it~does~not~establish faster browser operation. See \filepath{docs/BROWSER_LIFECYCLE.md} and~\filepath{docs/COMPATIBILITY_LIFECYCLE.md}.

\subsection{Bootstrap, routing, and~dispatch}
\index{bootstrap}
\index{routing}
\index{controller dispatch}
\index{request!life cycle}

Ordinary requests enter through \filepath{public/index.php}; the~repository-root \filepath{index.php} is~a~supported compatibility entry point for~other hosting layouts. The~public entry loads \filepath{app/early\_runtime.php} before \filepath{app/bootstrap.php}, then both layouts reach the~same normal request pipeline.

The~small request-local kernel under \filepath{app/runtime/} coordinates the~existing request lifecycle through Request, Router, RouteRegistry, and~ModuleLoader before invoking the~procedural controller. The~canonical route and~security table remains in~\filepath{app/bootstrap/dispatch.php}. A~reviewed generated plan loads logical modules for~the~selected route; production performs no~source scan or~dependency compilation and~has~no~load-all fallback. Existing PHP~8.1 and~shared-hosting requirements remain sufficient, without Composer or~a~Node build. Legacy CLI/test consumers may~use \filepath{app/bootstrap\_full.php} for~the~complete procedural API.

Shared Admin help and~factory-value markup belong to~the~existing
\filepath{app/views/admin\_ui.php} owner; Maintenance sections remain deferred.
Theme reuses the~complete gallery-access policy closure already needed by~its
picker and~preview, while scoped saves use canonical mutation responses.
These owners add no~logical module or~runtime file. Hosted preparation and~FULL
verify the~unchanged duplicate-ownership limits.

The~loader validates the~complete module plan in~each request. It~checks
canonical layer/path order in~one pass, checks each selected physical path
once within a~load preflight, and~counts completed files without repeatedly
scanning module lists. Later loads check their paths afresh. No~validation
cache is~shared between requests, so~updates and~concurrent requests need
no~additional cache invalidation. Optional OPcache remains a~server setting;
verify its~state in~the~web PHP runtime and~keep timestamp validation
or~deployment resets effective. The~existing Test Run trace separates plan
loading, full validation, dependency planning and~includes. Developer
measurements use disposable public/Admin requests and~distinguish opcode-cold
and~warm samples; they do~not~establish production latency.
The~compiler explicitly reviews the~private tracing callback; only local loader
operations supply it, with no~additional module dependency.

Thumbnail delivery reuses the~existing complete public-media module.
Compilation accepts this dependency only while its~full file closure already
belongs to~the~thumbnail owner. Route file limits and~the~duplicate-ownership
ratchet remain enforced.

Bootstrap reads local configuration and~establishes the~request context: database access where required, language, session state, security prerequisites, and~shared runtime services. Routing then translates the~incoming URL or~query-style fallback into an~internal route plus parameters such as~a~gallery path, page number, media size, or~share token. Dispatch calls the~controller registered for~that route.

The~ordering is~deliberate. A~request must~be~classified before code decides how to~serve it, and~access checks must run before protected output is~assembled. A~direct-looking image or~thumbnail URL is~therefore still an~application request; the~media controller authorizes it~before returning bytes.

\begin{lstlisting}
browser request
  -> index.php or public/index.php
  -> app/bootstrap.php
  -> cms_run() -> Gallery\Core\Kernel
  -> Request -> Router -> RouteRegistry
  -> ModuleLoader -> app/bootstrap/dispatch.php
  -> controller function
  -> service functions
  -> HTML, JSON, media, archive, or redirect response
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{Controllers}
\index{controller}

Controllers own HTTP-level coordination. They receive a~resolved route and~request context, validate the~operation, call the~services needed for~that operation, and~choose the~response type. A~public gallery route normally returns HTML; an~Admin side-panel action may~return JSON or~an~HTML fragment; a~media route returns an~authorized file; a~download route can~stream an~archive.

Public gallery rendering begins in~\filepath{app/controllers/public_gallery.php}, with related responsibilities split into focused controller files. Uploads, maintenance, updates, downloads, media access, and~Admin features have their own entry points, while shared domain rules remain in~services.

Controllers are~not~an~authorization shortcut. Thumbnail, map, lightbox, search, download, and~direct-media routes repeat the~relevant access decision instead of~assuming that a~visitor previously opened the~gallery page successfully.

\subsection{Services}
\index{services}

Reusable application rules live in~\filepath{app/services/}. Services resolve galleries and~images, evaluate access, normalize settings, manage uploads and~mutations, prepare thumbnail/media manifests, perform searches, maintain metadata, and~coordinate other domain work. A~controller should be~able to~ask for~one of~these operations without reimplementing its rules.

The~separation matters because the~same photograph can~be~reached from several routes. Access policy, thumbnail selection, or~deletion semantics should not~depend on~whether the~request came from a~gallery card, search result, lightbox, Admin tool, or~direct link. Source photographs remain in~the~gallery tree; services coordinate the~database records and~generated derivatives that describe or~support them.

\subsection{Views and~browser modules}
\index{views}
\index{browser modules}

Views render prepared data into HTML. They are~responsible for~structure and~presentation, not~for~deciding whether a~visitor is~authorized or~how a~thumbnail is~generated. Server output includes semantic links, forms, titles, alternative text, and~initial media candidates before JavaScript enhancement begins.

Browser modules add lightbox behavior, search suggestions, voting, upload progress, drag-and-drop actions, thumbnail upgrades, diagnostics, and~the~Admin side panel. The~code remains framework-free. Public pages therefore retain a~useful server-rendered baseline, while authenticated workflows can~add richer in-place interactions.

The~Admin side panel is~dynamic: its fragment can~be~replaced without navigating away from the~page. Modules that own panel controls consequently use delegated or~lifecycle-aware event binding so~a~freshly rendered copy continues to~work and~an~old fragment no~longer owns listeners.

\subsection{Public selected-gallery render pipeline}
\index{render pipeline}

A~selected-gallery request first resolves the~gallery and~its effective access policy, including inherited protection, password/share state, publication state, and~age restrictions. Only permitted content proceeds to~rendering.

The~controller then loads the~direct child galleries and~the~current page of~photographs, together with the~metadata needed for~titles, descriptions, tags, dates, votes, ordering, and~navigation. Thumbnail metadata is~prepared in~batches, and~request-local media manifests describe the~authorized candidates available for~each visible image.

The~view receives that prepared model and~renders the~page title, branding, navigation, breadcrumbs, cards, pagination, lightbox configuration, and~footer assets. Browser modules enhance the~result after delivery. Access remains a~server responsibility; JavaScript can~make an~interaction faster or~more convenient, but~it~does not~turn an~unauthorized candidate into an~authorized one.

This server-first split also preserves useful behavior when scripts are~blocked or~late. Direct links, semantic content, and~the~initial image choices are~already present in~the~HTML, while JavaScript handles the~features that genuinely need an~interactive client.

\section{Optional background: what the~database remembers}
\label{sec:database}
\index{database schema}
\index{migrations}

The~database stores application state that does not~belong in~image filenames: descriptions, privacy, ordering, tags, votes, dates, public addresses, accounts, and~maintenance history. The~overview below is~enough for~backup planning and~hosting discussions; column-level definitions remain in~the~database reference \cite{database}.

\subsection{Principal domains}

\begin{longtable}{>{\bfseries}p{0.25\linewidth}p{0.67\linewidth}}
\toprule
Domain & Persistent responsibility\\
\midrule
\endhead
Users and~authentication & Administrator identity, password hashes, recovery email, external identity links, reset tokens, and~scoped credentials.\\
Galleries & Folder path, hierarchy, public path, metadata, visibility, access, branding, presentation overrides, and~operational ordering.\\
Images & Gallery ownership, relative path, filename, metadata, dimensions, visibility, checksum, EXIF data, ordering, and~derived state.\\
Tags & Reusable tag identity plus gallery and~image relationships.\\
Votes & Gallery and~image scoring records with viewer association.\\
Thumbnails & Valid generated variant inventory, dimensions, format, status, and~derivative version.\\
Settings & Scalar application, theme, feature, update, maintenance, and~rendering values.\\
Audit and~telemetry & Administrator events, operational diagnostics, privacy-controlled measurements, and~rollups.\\
Duplicate ledger & Per-administrator canonical pair decisions and~exact-gallery suppression rules.\\
Jobs and~tokens & Bounded browser, detector, migration, WebDAV, reset, sharing, and~maintenance state where persistence is~required.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Relations and~cascades}

Foreign keys express ownership where schema evolution and~compatibility permit it. Cascades remove dependent workflow records when their parent identity disappears. Content deletion continues through application services so~filesystem and~database effects remain coordinated. Unique constraints encode identities such as~canonical duplicate pairs, exact administrator-gallery rules, slugs, tokens, and~metadata variants.

\subsection{Application settings}

The~\codeword{app_settings} table stores key-value configuration. \codeword{app_setting()} reads values, \codeword{set_app_setting()} persists normalized values, and~focused services interpret domain-specific settings. Public language configuration uses \setting{public_language} for~the~site default, \setting{public_language_selector_enabled} for~the~default-on~viewer override feature, and~\setting{public_language_selector_languages} for~its ordered non-empty language list. The~public thumbnail renderer uses \setting{public_thumbnail_rendering_mode} with \setting{responsive} and~\setting{progressive} values.

\section{Optional reference for~developers}
\label{sec:development}
\index{development}
\index{coding style}

This reference is~for~people changing PHP Gallery itself. Owners who only need publication and~maintenance checks can~continue with Section~\ref{sec:testing}.

\subsection{Repository organization}

\begin{longtable}{>{\ttfamily}p{0.27\linewidth}p{0.65\linewidth}}
\toprule
\textrm{Location} & \textrm{Responsibility}\\
\midrule
\endhead
app/controllers/ & HTTP controllers and~response coordination.\\
app/services/ & Reusable domain and~infrastructure services.\\
app/views/ & Layout and~reusable server-rendered view helpers.\\
app/lang/ & Server and~browser translation catalogs.\\
database/migrations/ & Timestamped schema evolution.\\
public/assets/ & Framework-free JavaScript, CSS, and~public assets.\\
scripts/ & Migration, integrity, deployment, and~maintenance utilities.\\
tests/ & Standalone PHP and~focused JavaScript tests.\\
winapp/ & Windows-specific tools and~integrations.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

For~new or~materially changed Admin interfaces, follow the~permanent visual
guidance in~\filepath{docs/ADMIN_DESIGN.md}. It~defines the~compact media-player
and~WinApp direction and~feature-scoped CSS review.

\subsection{Coding conventions}

New PHP files use strict types and~four-space indentation. Controllers coordinate request behavior, while services own reusable logic. New JavaScript and~CSS remain framework-free and~belong under public assets. Migrations use timestamp-prefixed filenames. Existing explanatory comments, docstrings, and~PHPDoc receive preservation and~correction as~behavior evolves.

Declaration docstrings are~required for~named functions, methods, and~class-like declarations, including interfaces, traits, and~enums. Place PHPDoc/JSDoc immediately above the~declaration and~Python docstrings first inside its~body. Named functions and~methods need a~factual purpose summary, a~typed and~described parameter entry for~each explicit parameter, and~a~typed return entry, including void. Classes need a~factual purpose summary. Anonymous functions, closures, arrow functions, callbacks, variables, constants, and~class properties do~not~require declaration docstrings; named declarations nested inside callbacks remain checked. File attribution, native signature typing, operational-policy comments, and~parser coverage remain separate requirements.

Atomic modules should own one cohesive responsibility. Shared contracts deserve normalized inputs, explicit outputs, and~focused tests. Dynamic Admin and~public fragments require lifecycle-safe setup and~teardown.

\subsection{Authoring code before source checks}

Readability guidance covers code, TeX and~Markdown source. Prefer handwritten
lines near 80 display columns; consider natural breaks at~81--100 and~seek
readable splitting above 100. This is~advice, not~a~maximum or~CI gate. Put
lengthy arguments and~collection entries on~separate indented lines, group
conditions and~separate logical steps/declarations with idiomatic blank lines.
Wrap documentation prose at~existing spaces while preserving paragraphs,
TeX commands, non-breaking bindings and~Markdown structure. Preserve semantics,
types, comments and~docstrings; allow necessary longer literals and~URLs.

For~an~optional whole-tree report, open \ui{Code readability report}
in~GitHub Actions, choose \ui{Run workflow} and~select only the~branch.
Its~workflow, checker, rules and~source use that branch's exact event SHA;
the~summary records the~branch/SHA. Download the~artifact for~complete HTML,
Markdown, JSON and~per-file diagnostics covering PHP, JS, MJS, Python, CSS,
TeX and~Markdown. Findings never fail CI or~rewrite source; tooling failures
remain visible. The~workflow appears in~the~sidebar after reviewed
default-branch installation. Rules, exclusions, Unicode/tab measurement
and~focused examples are~in~\filepath{docs/CODE_READABILITY.md}; the~central
optional audit profile is~\codeword{readability}. Required qualification
remains unchanged.

The~canonical \filepath{AGENTS.md} requires each named declaration and~its~complete documentation in~the~same edit. Start with a~factual purpose before tags, document every explicit parameter (including defaults and~added parameters), actual return variants and~concrete collection/object shapes. PHP/Python native types remain independent. Anonymous callbacks need no~declaration docstring; named declarations inside them still do. Runtime policy sites independently need purpose, type, units, scope, consumers and~rationale immediately before the~site. Checked examples in~\filepath{docs/AGENT_AUTHORING.md} are~parsed by~the~existing scanners in~a~registered regression, with negative fixtures preserving failures for~incomplete contracts.

Python sources consistently use typed Google-style \texttt{Args:} and
\texttt{Returns:} sections. Each parameter entry names the~argument, its~type
and~its~actual meaning; procedures document \texttt{None}. Native annotations
remain independent. Legacy tag inputs stay supported by~the~scanner
for~compatibility, while authored Python docstrings use one consistent format.

GitHub Actions runs the~source preflight before hosted preparation. Coding agents follow CI-first: identify the~current authorized working branch and~HEAD, verify branch-scoped push access, finish source/tests/documentation, commit and~push that exact branch. Never directly mutate \codeword{develop} or~\codeword{main}. Missing authorization or~CI access is~BLOCKED. Local audits, generators and~test loops are~exceptional diagnostics, not~routine handoff gates. Hosted CI retains the~immutable merge-base with \codeword{origin/main}; releases retain the~previous stable tag. Red or~slow CI does~not~justify local-only qualification.

Hosted qualification covers PHP/Node/WinApp tests, source contracts, required Chromium, real MySQL/MariaDB workflows, PHP compatibility and~Linux/Windows/macOS package checks. Every mandatory job and~the~exact-SHA gate must pass. The~release workflow compiles all~four PDF manuals once after the~final source edits, not~during routine development.

The~hosted portable WinApp job installs the~existing Pillow requirement into
the~Python interpreter selected for~the~audit, so~tray-badge pixel coverage
runs instead of skipping. Local runs without Pillow may~still skip this
optional visual fixture; the~headless hosted job does~not~install pystray.

The~title-picture regression extends the~already registered Admin side-panel delegation and~browser lifecycle suites, plus the~existing gallery edit concurrency HTTP/database suite. These fixtures remain under \filepath{scripts/audit.php}; they add no~YAML test owner or~alternate runner, and~the~protected evaluator registry is~unchanged.

Reports record source HEAD, resolved comparison SHA and~dirty state. A~modified checkout is~feedback, not~clean-commit qualification. The~linked \filepath{source-failures.md} lists all~failed changed-source findings, blockers and~findings in~over-budget categories; complete value-free JSON retains the~evidence and~historical debt stays identified. Candidate workflows upload these together. Repair the~whole batch before rerunning; unchanged input is~not~a~repair. Finish all~four manual sources and~push the~authorized working branch. Hosted Candidate preparation regenerates artifacts after the~final edit and~runs the~mandatory full CI matrix for~the~exact prepared SHA. Record SHA, base, profile/result and~material incomplete coverage. Measure first-attempt results and~repair cycles from actual reports; expected savings remain unknown until observed.

\subsection{Adding a~feature}

A~typical feature change follows this sequence:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Trace current implementation and~identify the~owning controller, services, views, browser modules, persistence, and~tests.
  \item Define access, CSRF, scope, fallback, migration, and~no-JavaScript contracts.
  \item Add focused services and~route integration.
  \item Add append-only migration work where persistent schema changes are~required.
  \item Add translated interface strings.
  \item Add focused automated tests and~documented manual verification.
  \item Update permanent Markdown references and~all~four TeX manual sources whenever current user, administrator, architecture, or~maintainer guidance changes.
  \item Hosted Candidate preparation runs \filepath{scripts/prepare_candidate.php} and~its read-only check: runtime modules, indexed production inventory and~complete-byte integrity hashes are~generated in~dependency order. It~commits only changed artifacts back to~the~same authorized branch, refuses concurrent writers, and~qualifies the~exact resulting SHA. Agents push focused source commits and~inspect CI; they do~not~duplicate generation locally.
  \item Report the~GitHub Actions URL, checked working branch, exact prepared candidate SHA, immutable comparison base, required job results and~manual or~missing coverage. A~stale, missing, skipped, cancelled, failed or~blocked mandatory job is~not~green qualification. Fix source and~push the~same branch for~fresh CI.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Preparing source candidates for~CI}

Atomic source commits are~allowed during development, but~qualification must use a~candidate whose generated artifacts match the~exact source revision. Permanent technical documentation is~part of~the~source edit. Changes to~administrator behavior, architecture, runtime ownership, security, packaging, or~CI also require reviewing all~four TeX manual editions, not~only user-facing pages. Routine development leaves \filepath{PATCH_NOTES.md} and~the~tracked manual PDFs unchanged.

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Hosted Candidate preparation runs \filepath{scripts/prepare_candidate.php} and~its read-only check: runtime modules, indexed production inventory and~complete-byte integrity hashes are~generated in~dependency order. It~commits only changed artifacts back to~the~same authorized branch, refuses concurrent writers, and~qualifies the~exact resulting SHA. Agents push focused source commits and~inspect CI; they do~not~duplicate generation locally.
  \item Report the~GitHub Actions URL, checked working branch, exact prepared candidate SHA, immutable comparison base, required job results and~manual or~missing coverage. A~stale, missing, skipped, cancelled, failed or~blocked mandatory job is~not~green qualification. Fix source and~push the~same branch for~fresh CI.
\end{enumerate}

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

\section{Checking your gallery before publication}
\label{sec:testing}
\index{testing}
\index{quality assurance}

Testing for~a~gallery owner means walking through the~site as~the~intended audience. A~technically healthy page can~still contain a~private location, an~unclear title, an~incorrect date, an~accidental download permission, or~an~awkward mobile layout. A~short human review catches these presentation and~privacy concerns.

\subsection{A~visitor-centered publication check}
\index{publication checklist}

Open a~private browser window and~follow the~same link you plan to~share. Check:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item The~title and~first paragraph explain the~collection.
  \item The~chosen cover remains clear at~a~small size.
  \item Child galleries appear in~a~sensible order.
  \item Photograph titles and~descriptions match the~visible subjects.
  \item Private photographs and~galleries stay hidden.
  \item Password or~share access works from a~fresh session.
  \item GPS maps reveal only intended locations.
  \item The~first page loads comfortably on~a~phone.
  \item The~large viewer, forward and~backward controls, and~close action feel natural.
  \item Search, tags, voting, and~downloads behave according to~the~gallery's purpose.
\end{enumerate}

Ask another person to~try the~gallery without instructions. Their first click and~first question often reveal navigation or~wording that felt obvious only to~the~owner.

\subsection{Automated checks for~software maintainers}

The~central audit covers clipboard MIME/name handling and~synthetic browser paste events, ordinary multipart upload and~panel persistence. These fixtures do~not~read the~operating-system clipboard. Record actual Windows Chrome/Edge/Firefox, Linux Chromium/Firefox and~macOS Safari/Chrome/Firefox results using the~manual matrix in~\filepath{TESTING.md}, for~both browser and~server preparation on~direct-page and~side-panel forms. Unexecuted checks remain pending.

The~registered miniature tests cover bounded raster headers, cancellation and~resource disposal. The~Chromium fixtures also check 100-item FileList parity, a~320-pixel drawer, reset/navigation cleanup, focus and~classic/prepared retry without duplicate ingestion. Observed URL/decode counts are~not~a~whole-process memory measurement; real OS clipboard delivery and~Firefox/Safari checks remain manual.

For~coding agents, GitHub Actions is~the~default and~authoritative verification environment (CI-first). The~central audit commands below describe the~hosted runner; agents do~not~execute them locally by~default. Focused local reproduction is~reserved for~an~observed environment-specific CI failure, genuinely inaccessible CI or~an~explicit request. Local evidence never replaces required hosted coverage.

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
# Hosted source feedback (exceptional local diagnosis only):
php scripts/audit.php --profile=release-preflight
php scripts/audit.php --profile=quick
# Hosted full source qualification:
php scripts/audit.php --profile=full
# Hosted authoritative release job:
php scripts/audit.php --profile=release
\end{lstlisting}

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

The~registered breadcrumb Theme test compares the~Theme and~physical-gallery style lists in~\filepath{docs/ADMIN_SETTINGS_INVENTORY.md} with \codeword{breadcrumb_style_registry()}. It~also protects the~\codeword{chevron} fallback and~the~gallery-only \codeword{inherit} sentinel. When adding a~style, update both documented lists; \codeword{inherit} is~not~a~Theme visual style.

\subsection{Manual browser verification}

\subsubsection{Network and~interaction checks}

Browser-dependent software checks use representative data, anonymous and~authenticated sessions, an~empty cache, mobile and~desktop viewports, JavaScript-disabled operation where relevant, reduced-motion preferences, and~network throttling.

For~public thumbnail rendering, inspect the~Elements and~Network panels. Responsive mode should expose its complete active candidate set. Progressive mode should expose a~small active candidate and~inert larger candidates before activation, then perform at~most the~documented concurrent upgrades for~relevant cards. Check layout stability, cache reuse, error fallback, lightbox interaction, maps, voting, protected media, and~direct links.

\subsection{Release hygiene}

Follow \filepath{RELEASE.md} for~the~complete release sequence. Compare the~exact previous stable tag, complete the~release notes and~permanent documentation, and~align all~four maintained manual editions. Rebuild each PDF with the~indexed multi-pass sequence in~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md} and~check compiler success, references, index entries, and~layout warnings. Visual PDF review is~reserved for~an~explicit request or~diagnosis of~a~concrete layout problem; routine successful builds need no~additional visual-approval gate.

The commands in this section describe hosted release ownership and the retained local maintainer recovery path. Normal preparation and qualification run on GitHub; agents do not execute these commands locally by default. Local recovery requires an explicit request or genuinely unavailable CI and remains BLOCKED / not CI-qualified until hosted evidence exists. The later review workflow performs protected merge and tagging; GitHub Release publication stays manual.

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

Create a~production folder or~ZIP only when explicitly requested; hosted release assets use the~same PHP builder automatically. Pass the~candidate epoch with \codeword{--timestamp} for~deterministic metadata. The~builder refuses stale manifests and~existing outputs; \filepath{scripts/release_files.php} inspects membership only. Three OS packaging owners cover the~real builder independently of~the~database matrix. The~main-only locked TinyTeX cache warmer requires reviewed installation before release branches can~reuse its exact key. A~CMS-only release does~not~require a~new WinApp installer. Commit, tag, publish, upload, and~deploy require explicit authorization. CI tests PHP 8.1 as~the~compatibility floor and~PHP 8.5 as~the~newest representative. All~three database variants run on~PHP 8.5; historical evidence retains its actual versions.

During ordinary development, update all~four \codeword{.tex} sources together and~review their actual explanations; do~not~rebuild the~tracked PDFs after every change. The~PDFs may~lag behind the~sources until final release preparation. Automated version/date replacement is~not~semantic documentation generation: maintainers must add new feature, security, compatibility, and~operational content in~English, Czech, German, and~Swedish themselves.

The~current qualification and~release assembly sequence is~described in~the~maintainer verification section and~\filepath{RELEASE.md}.

\section{Making the~gallery feel fast}
\label{sec:performance}
\index{performance}
\index{speed}

Perceived speed is~not~one number. Visitors notice when the~page first appears, when the~first photographs become recognizable, whether scrolling stays responsive, how quickly controls react, and~how long a~larger image takes after they ask for~it. Connection quality, server distance, page size, thumbnail policy, branding assets, and~the~selected renderer all contribute.

\subsection{Practical ways to~improve speed}

\begin{itemize}
  \item Keep the~number of~cards per~page reasonable for~the~target audience and~hosting plan.
  \item Generate the~configured thumbnail sizes in~advance for~large public collections.
  \item Avoid unnecessarily large hero, background, logo, and~other theme assets.
  \item Prefer the~responsive thumbnail renderer when predictability and~no-JavaScript behavior matter most; use the~progressive renderer when its staged sharpening suits the~collection and~browser mix.
  \item Place the~gallery on~hosting geographically close to~its main audience where practical.
  \item Test with an~empty cache. A~warm developer browser can~hide expensive first-visit transfers.
  \item Check PHP/database latency separately from image-transfer time before assuming that every slow gallery is~a~thumbnail problem.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Slow-connection test}

Open the~gallery in~a~private browser window, select a~representative mobile viewport, enable network throttling in~developer tools, and~reload with an~empty cache. Watch the~order in~which the~page, covers, visible cards, and~later image upgrades arrive. Repeat with the~renderer or~page-size setting you intend to~compare; otherwise cached files make the~comparison unreliable.

\subsection{Technical measurements for~maintainers}

Public render profiling and~browser diagnostics are~useful when a~subjective report needs numbers. Record at~least:

\begin{itemize}
  \item time to~first byte and~server-side render duration;
  \item HTML transfer size;
  \item request count and~bytes transferred for~initial thumbnails;
  \item time until the~largest visible content has~settled;
  \item layout shifts while cards and~media load;
  \item main-thread work attributable to~browser modules;
  \item number and~size of~progressive candidate upgrades;
  \item database-query totals from the~development profiler where available;
  \item image decode/memory pressure when testing very large originals or~deep lightbox zoom.
\end{itemize}

The~development profiler and~captured benchmark records are~intended for~before/after comparisons across renderer modes and~gallery layouts. Use representative high-density screens and~constrained connections rather than relying only on~a~fast desktop with a~warm cache.

\section{Troubleshooting}
\label{sec:troubleshooting}
\index{troubleshooting}

\subsection{Application does not~start}

Confirm the~PHP version, required extensions, configuration location, database connectivity, writable paths, and~web-root routing. Review PHP and~web-server logs. Run syntax checks on~recently changed PHP files and~inspect pending migrations.

\subsection{Gallery or~images are~missing}

Confirm filesystem paths, gallery discovery state, database records, visibility, parent access rules, password unlock state, NSFW policy, supported file type, and~image relative path. Test as~both administrator and~anonymous visitor.

\subsection{Thumbnails are~missing or~soft}

Review thumbnail metadata and~maintenance reports. Confirm generated sizes, format support, configured bounds, source geometry, derivative status, and~public endpoint authorization. In~responsive mode, inspect the~browser-selected candidate and~\codeword{sizes}. In~progressive mode, inspect the~small candidate, queue state, selected replacement, and~decode result.

\subsection{Admin actions leave the~side panel}

Confirm the~matching JavaScript module loaded, delegated handlers recognized the~form, AJAX headers and~response contracts are~correct, the~returned fragment contains expected lifecycle markers, and~generic Admin form handlers exclude feature-owned forms. Direct POST behavior can~still provide the~documented fallback route.

\subsection{Migration fails}

Keep a~verified database backup and~record the~migration filename, Gallery version, database-server version, bounded on-screen message, request reference, and~existing schema/ledger state. Retry through the~Gallery administration page so~the~normal idempotent compatibility path can~finish an~interrupted migration. Version~\version{} does~not~need triggers, stored routines, \codeword{SUPER}, \codeword{log\_bin\_trust\_function\_creators}, or~a~global database change. If~the~bounded error remains, inspect Runtime Diagnostics and~give the~request reference to~the~maintainer or~hosting provider; do~not~paste database credentials or~raw production data into a~support report.

\subsection{PDF manual compilation fails}

Run the~commands in~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md}. Confirm \codeword{pdflatex} and~\codeword{makeindex} are~installed. A~first MiKTeX run may~request common packages. Delete ignored intermediate build files after a~failed package transition and~repeat the~documented sequence.

\section{Backup and~recovery checklist}
\label{sec:backup}
\index{recovery}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item Export the~complete application database.
  \item Copy \filepath{galleries/} with source files and~gallery-owned assets.
  \item Copy local configuration and~relevant installation secrets securely.
  \item Preserve custom theme assets and~custom CSS.
  \item Record the~application version and~pending migration state.
  \item Store backups outside the~active web tree.
  \item Test restoration periodically in~an~isolated environment.
  \item Verify public routes, protected access, Admin login, thumbnails, uploads, and~migrations after restoration.
\end{enumerate}

Updater-created backups preserve overwritten application files for~update recovery. A~complete operational backup also includes the~database, gallery tree, local configuration, and~installation-owned assets.

\subsection{Validate a~recovery set}
\index{recovery!assurance}

Keep the~database export, original photographs, local configuration, relevant
\filepath{data/} storage, Trash contents, custom assets, and~exact application
version together as~one coordinated recovery set. A~provider snapshot or~a~bounded
maintenance window can~prevent writes from~splitting database and~file state.
Store an~off-host copy and~agree how much recent work may~be~lost and~how quickly
service must return.

The~CLI entry point \filepath{scripts/recovery.php} can~inventory that set
and~validate restored file samples and~manifest hashes in~a~separate location.
Follow \filepath{docs/RECOVERY_ASSURANCE.md} for~the~evidence format and~commands.
The~tool does~not~execute a~database dump, restore production, or~verify a~backup
provider. Its synthetic corruption drill proves the~checker detects fixture
damage, not~that your actual backup can~restore the~site.

Before opening a~restored installation, isolate email, scheduled jobs,
integrations, and~write access from~production destinations. Verify the~restored
database, relationships, Admin login, private-media denial, and~Trash restoration.
Record backup age, elapsed recovery time, and~the~operator's observations without
copying secrets into reports. Use \filepath{docs/RECOVERY_OFF_HOST.md} for~the~full
procedure. Updater file rollback still does~not~reverse database migrations.

\section{Maintainer verification and~release evidence}
\label{sec:release-evidence}

\subsection{Source qualification and~selected feature workflows}

Feature pushes and~the~final squash receive BASIC syntax and~declaration checks.
In~GitHub Actions, choose \ui{Integrate into develop}, select main or~the~intended
feature in~\ui{Use workflow from}, and~select the~source branch. The~owner's
explicit dispatch runs that revision's actual workflow, audit and~tests.
Changes to~tests or~registries need no~preliminary installation on~main.

The~source is~one commit above observed develop. Hosted preparation generates
runtime modules, inventory and~manifest, folds changed generated data into
the~final candidate and~obtains fresh BASIC. A~separate authority checkout
inspects the~clean candidate. FULL retains all~28 required native owners:
shared source contracts, portable Node/WinApp, packaging on~three operating
systems, four shards for~each~of~three databases, four shards for~each PHP
8.1/8.5 source environment, required Chromium and~the~aggregate.
Missing, failed, cancelled or~skipped mandatory owners block integration.

Successful execution is~sealed before the~protected non-force fast-forward.
\codeword{qualify_only} retains qualification without writing develop.
Supply its~run ID as~\codeword{qualified_run} to~publish the~identical
candidate, complete input tree and~base without another FULL. Changed inputs
require new execution. The~publisher still checks actual owner/run identities,
source leases and~branch protections.

After successful integration and~record upload, the~workflow deletes the~selected
remote feature branch with an~exact candidate-SHA lease. Newer commits prevent
deletion. \codeword{qualify_only} and~\codeword{recover_existing} retain the~branch.
Cleanup failure leaves the~completed integration and~retained evidence intact.

For~an~explicit CI-only installation ahead of~a~release, base the~feature
on~main and~dispatch \ui{Basic source checks} with \codeword{install_ci}.
Complete FULL and~the~authoritative release audit produce a~bot PR;
one real human approval installs it~through normal protected auto-merge.

The~publisher binds the~same verified FULL to~both Checks and~a~standard
Actions commit status, because dispatch-suite Checks can~be~absent from
GitHub's branch-rule summary. It~rechecks the~status identity, state and~run
before each protected fast-forward.

After qualification and~protected installation, a~sole HTTP~422 rejection
for~the~new required matrix check permits up~to~six publication attempts with
two-second waits. Each retry revalidates the~active run, BASIC/FULL evidence,
original check IDs and~branch leases. Other failures stop; FULL is~not~replayed.

\subsection{Release assembly from qualified develop}

Registered Markdown version markers accept soft line breaks within the~same
paragraph. Preparation and~consistency checks preserve the~existing wrapping
and~historical version prose while updating the~registered value.
WinApp README, agent instructions and~tests alone do~not~trigger an~installer
rebuild; runtime and~build-input changes still do.
The~draft attachment reader uses the~same exclusions when reusing verified
published installer bytes. Recovery after tagging preserves Q, M and~the~tag.

In~GitHub Actions, run \ui{Begin Release} from~the~branch containing
the~intended workflow revision, normally \codeword{develop}, and~enter
the~version including \codeword{v_}, for~example \codeword{v_0.136.2}.
The~workflow separately reads current protected develop, freezes its~SHA,
creates \codeword{release/v_0.136.2} and~explicitly dispatches assembly
on~that release branch. Its~new workflow and~scripts execute before they
reach main. No~BASIC/FULL is~repeated. Initialization authenticates
the~original maintainer and~initiating run, attempt and~version;
the~bot identity alone grants no~authority. Retry can~reuse an~unmoved ref
and~avoids duplicate active assembly. If~the~release ref has~advanced,
continue its~existing assembly directly. PR approval and~publication remain
separate maintainer actions.

Before PR review, assembly retains the~frozen main history and~prepared tree.
After approval and~tagging, unchanged develop fast-forwards to~the~actual tagged
main commit. Both branch refs then match; no~content-copy commit is~created.
Its~status represents verified assembly and~qualified source ancestry;
functional FULL is~not~repeated. Parallel develop work is~preserved.
The~permanent receipt records the~actual main result, tree and~base.
Develop's history policy must admit reviewed main merges while retaining
non-force updates, required Actions checks and~no~bypass. The~legacy
linear-history requirement explicitly blocks readiness and~PR creation;
only the~maintainer changes installed policy. A~later release can~join existing
divergent histories without rewriting them. Historical content-copy receipts
remain readable; immediate ref realignment requires separate authorization.
The~next release trusts protected develop without historical reconciliation
archives. Only the~post-assembly observer handles approval, including approval
submitted early; successful origin Git push needs no~immediate REST readback.

A~commit admitted to~protected develop is~qualified. Create the~separately
authorized \codeword{release/v_X.Y[.Z]} branch from that source.
Release does~not rerun BASIC/FULL or~download older qualification archives.
It~freezes source A, updates metadata and~patch notes, aligns all~four manual
editions, compiles their PDFs with the~locked TinyTeX toolchain and~reproducible
timestamp, and~refreshes inventory and~the~complete-file manifest last.
Changed WinApp inputs require the~installer; unchanged inputs retain its
independent version and~verified existing assets.

New patch notes link every evidenced issue or~PR directly addressed by~a~described
change in~that release. Complete commit metadata includes changed paths
for~each commit, supporting comparison with the~source diff and~evidence digests.
Previous notes supply style only; historical examples and~routine release
bookkeeping establish no~current issue work. Release origin messages and~PR
wrappers add~no~fixed references. Actual new~work on~older issues remains eligible.
Copilot may~return \codeword{NOTES_REVIEW_REQUIRED} when extraction,
consolidation or~grounded synthesis cannot finish reliably, including
an~unresolved material issue relationship. Validation then uses the~same external
JSON handoff described below. Format checks do~not~prove semantic relevance.

Editorial notes preserve completed maintainer prose or~use the~existing
bounded Copilot generation from complete Git evidence. Automatic work admits
at~most 100000 diff bytes, 20 source files and~180000 prompt bytes before
provider setup. Larger changes, missing credentials, explicit external mode
or~Copilot/quota/invalid-output failures create \codeword{NOTES_PENDING}.
Download \codeword{release-notes-handoff} from~the~Actions run and~give
\filepath{prompt.txt} to~your chosen AI. The~30-day artifact includes the~full
template, commit evidence, source diff and~hash manifest. Review its~single-line
JSON response, then select the~same release branch in~\ui{Release qualification
> Run workflow}, paste it~into \codeword{external_notes} and~run.
The~bundle README provides CLI continuation when the~button is~not~yet
registered on~main. JSON escapes retain Markdown newlines. Repository, version,
branch, source SHA and~prompt hash must match freshly rebuilt evidence;
changed evidence needs a~new bundle. Existing notes remain authoritative.
Pending handoff uses no~waiting runner and~creates no~prepared commit,
qualification check, manuals build or~release PR. Continuation adds no~BASIC/FULL.
Invalid output is~never applied. Build failures, missing attachments
and~stale generated hashes stop
assembly; they do~not introduce another source qualification stage.
Lifecycle schema 4 records the~qualified develop source and~assembly outcome.

The~existing stable tag remains immutable after CI-only main maintenance.
Initialization follows actual first-parent main history instead of~requiring
a~new tag or~a~version-specific exception. Git leases still protect concurrent
writers. Original release identities and~published assets stay unchanged.

\subsection{Review, version and~complete attachments}

The~workflow opens the~exact bot-authored PR to~main. Approve Q once.
Automation performs the~protected standard merge, verifies its~parents/tree,
creates the~immutable version tag, attempts safe develop synchronization
and~fills an~unpublished release draft with the~original ZIP, metadata,
checksums, all~four PDFs, notes and~permanent assembly/completion evidence.
It~adds missing verified assets and~preserves identical existing ones.

Wait for~\codeword{DRAFT_READY}, then manually choose \ui{Publish release}.
\codeword{TAGGED / DRAFT_PENDING} is~recoverable for~the~same Q;
do~not~rebuild the~candidate or~move a~correct tag. Parallel develop work
blocks unsafe synchronization, not~a~correct completed tag or~draft.
After \codeword{DRAFT_READY} and~retention of~completion evidence, the~final job
automatically removes the~exact release branch with a~Q SHA lease.
It~rechecks uploaded asset hashes, reviewed merge/tag, main/develop ancestry
and~active PR/workflow dependencies. Incomplete synchronization keeps the~branch;
failed proof blocks cleanup while preserving the~draft. Retry only the~failed
cleanup job; immutable Q and~retained evidence support an~already deleted branch.
This starts with the~first release containing the~change in~qualified develop.
Manual publication and~browser/hosting smoke tests remain separate. Historical
branches retain the~separately approved retirement workflow.
See \filepath{RELEASE.md}, \filepath{docs/RELEASE_LIFECYCLE.md},
\filepath{docs/CI_COVERAGE_REUSE_CONTRACT.md} and~\filepath{docs/LATEX_BUILD.md}.

\section{Glossary}
\label{sec:glossary}
\index{glossary}

\begin{description}
  \item[AJAX] Browser request used to~update a~page region while preserving the~surrounding interface.
  \item[Branch scope] A~gallery and~its descendants, when the~selected feature defines recursive scope.
  \item[Canonical pair] Two image identifiers stored in~a~stable low-to-high order.
  \item[CSRF token] Session-bound value used to~validate intentional state-changing form submissions.
  \item[Derivative] Generated media representation such as~a~JPEG or~WebP thumbnail.
  \item[EXIF] Camera and~capture metadata embedded in~supported image files.
  \item[Integrity manifest] Versioned list of~core paths and~expected hashes.
  \item[Lightbox] Fullscreen or~overlay photo viewer with navigation and~related controls.
  \item[Media manifest] Request-local rendering data prepared from thumbnail metadata for~visible images.
  \item[NSFW guard] Age and~gallery/image policy controlling access to~restricted media.
  \item[Progressive sharpening] Small-first thumbnail pipeline that activates a~decoded larger candidate later.
  \item[Responsive selection] Browser-native candidate selection from an~immediately active source set.
  \item[Selected gallery] Gallery represented by~the~current public route.
  \item[Side panel] Admin right-side contextual interface loaded and~refreshed through fragment requests.
  \item[Thumbnail bounds] Gallery-aware minimum and~maximum generated sizes eligible for~selection.
\end{description}
